### 9. Scripts and assets audit

**The skill ships no scripts, no assets, no templates and no fixtures.** There is therefore nothing to assess for Windows compatibility, `--help`, self-test, or exit-code discipline. Per defect class 13, every rule in this skill is a preference: the count of executable checks is **zero**, and the count of assertions those checks make is **zero**.

For calibration, the two checkers in the repository this skill governs — which it should be requiring, and does not know exist:

| Script | Windows | `--help` | Self-test | Exit codes | What it asserts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `service-runtime-kit/scripts/validate-runtime.sh` | Runs under Git Bash; uses `awk`, `bash -n`, and optional `docker compose` / `python3+PyYAML`. Explicitly CR/CRLF-aware — `assert_text_file_lf` runs on twelve container-executed scripts (`:40-58`) before any syntax or Compose check, which is exactly the Windows defect class this programme has already shipped twice | `-h/--help` at `:25-28`, but usage is one `Usage:` line with no description, options or exit-code table | No | `1` for every failure via `die`, including "Need either `docker compose` or `python3 + PyYAML`" (`:142`) — **"could not run" and "clean" are distinguished, but "could not run" and "violation" are not** | (a) generated shell files are LF-only and parse under `bash -n`; (b) a legacy generated secret file does not exist (`:60-65`); (c) every `$VAR`/`${VAR}` in `docker-compose*.yml` carries `:?` or `:-`, skipping comments and honouring `$$` escapes (`:73-118`); (d) each of the three Compose files parses structurally |
| `service-runtime-kit/scripts/test-fail-closed-interpolation.sh` | Uses `mktemp -d`, a `trap ... EXIT` cleanup, and a fake `docker` on `PATH` (`:37-54`) — all portable to Git Bash; the temp directory is outside the repository, so it survives a read-only mount | `-h/--help` at `:24-28` with a real Purpose block | It **is** the self-test | `1` for every assertion failure, `2` for bad arguments (`:31-34`) — argument misuse is distinguished, environment failure is not | Eight named violation shapes are rejected with exact `file:line:variable` output (`:86-93`), including `${V?msg}`, `${V-def}`, `$V` unbraced, and mixed and triple-`$` forms; the clean fixture passes (`:68`); the rendered Compose carries the fail-closed `APP_KEY` form in env mode and `APP_KEY_FILE` with no `APP_KEY` in secret-files mode (`:113-137`); both the kit and generated `up-local.sh` refuse a missing `.env` **without invoking Docker**, verified through a call log (`:145-149`); `ALLOW_NO_ENV_FILE=TRUE` does not bypass the guard while `=true` does (`:152-160`) |

Neither checker asserts the semantic half of the invariant, and neither is referenced by this skill.

**Note on the staged kit.** `scripts/lib/common.sh` is sourced at line 7 of `render-runtime.sh`, `validate-runtime.sh` and `up-local.sh` and is not present in the staged tree, along with `docs/`, `contracts/service-bootstrap.tsv`, `templates/repo-support/`, `scripts/bootstrap-service-repos.sh`, `scripts/test-line-endings.sh` and `scripts/test-queue-worker-mode.sh` — all referenced by `README.md:32-58,91-97`. I treated this as an artifact of partial staging rather than a repository defect, and reconstructed a minimal `common.sh` locally to render the fixture.

### 10. Rewrite brief for Phase 2

#### Target body size

`SKILL.md` is 82 lines / 3,989 bytes today. **Target: 70-78 lines, at or below 3,989 bytes.** The body gains three genuinely new capabilities — the D8 reciprocal half, the runtime-kit seam, and ownership of the fail-closed interpolation invariant — and pays for them by deleting five routing blocks (`:11-15` Purpose, `:32-39` Quick start, `:41-50` Troubleshooting map, `:65-72` Reference navigation, `:74-81` Maintenance rules) totalling 50 lines. Net shrink is available even after the additions.

Body contents after the rewrite, in order: frontmatter; a two-sentence statement of what the skill is; `## Ownership: platform versus stack` (the two D8 sentences from §4 verbatim); `## Three rules that never cost a hop` (see below); `## Navigation` pointing at `references/00-topic-map.md` and nothing else; a model/effort routing line to `/alaa-prompting-guide` (`$alaa-prompting-guide`), `references/50-effort-and-thinking.md`.

The three always-loaded rules, chosen because each is cheap to state and expensive to discover late:

1. **A production-shaped Compose or stack file fails closed on every safety control.** A mandatory variable is `${VAR:?message}`. A deliberately optional one is `${VAR:-default}` and writing that form asserts the default is correct in production. A variable whose default would silently disable a safety control — a credential, an upload cap, an auth hook, a TLS verification flag, a rate limit — takes `:?` with **no default permitted**, including `:-0` and including `:-`, because an empty password and a cap of zero both mean "no control". Compose interpolation reads the shell environment and `--env-file` (including the project `.env`) only; it never reads the service-level `env_file:` key. The register of safety-control variables and the checker are `references/25-fail-closed-interpolation.md`.
2. **The final stage is the whole security posture.** Non-root `user:`, `cap_drop: [ALL]`, `security_opt: [no-new-privileges:true]`, read-only root filesystem with named writable mounts, and no package manager, compiler or dev dependency in the image. If any one of the five cannot be met, name the blocking constraint in the merge request; do not omit it silently. `references/10-dockerfile-authorship.md`.
3. **Verify the rendered model, not the file you wrote.** Run `docker compose config` and the shipped checkers against the *generated* Compose and stack files before any `up` or `stack deploy`. Exit 0 passes, 1 is a violation, 2 means the tree could not be read and is not a pass.

#### Executable checks the skill should ship and does not

All four are Node ESM (`.mjs`) run as `node scripts/<name>.mjs <path>`, must resolve their own location with `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)` and never `new URL(...).pathname`, must write LF only, must accept `--help`, must carry a `--self-test` mode that runs against bundled fixtures, and must exit **0 clean / 1 violation / 2 could-not-read**.

1. **`scripts/check-compose-interpolation.mjs`** — the checker the invariant currently lacks. Port the syntactic pass from `service-runtime-kit/scripts/validate-runtime.sh:73-118` (comment skipping, `$$`-escape counting, nested-default handling) and add the semantic pass the kit has never had: a declared register of safety-control variable name patterns, and a violation for any register member written with `:-` in any form, including `:-` with an empty default. Asserts: (a) no bare `$VAR` or `${VAR}`; (b) no `${VAR?msg}` or `${VAR-def}` colon-less forms; (c) no register member carries any default. Fixture set must include the eight shapes `test-fail-closed-interpolation.sh:70-79` already enumerates plus `${DB_PASSWORD:-}` and `${RABBITMQ_PASSWORD:-app}`, which today's checker passes.
2. **`scripts/check-dockerfile-contract.mjs`** — asserts a `# syntax=docker/dockerfile:` directive on line 1; at least two stages; the final stage sets `USER` to a non-root name or UID; no package manager, compiler or dev-dependency install in the final stage; no `COPY . .` or `COPY . /app` before the dependency-install `RUN`; a `LABEL org.opencontainers.image.revision`; a `HEALTHCHECK` or an explicit `# healthcheck-exempt: <reason>` comment; and that a `.dockerignore` exists next to the Dockerfile and excludes `.env`, `.git` and the local-secrets directory.
3. **`scripts/check-stack-rollout.mjs`** — for each service in a `docker-compose.swarm.yml` that is not a one-shot job: asserts `deploy.update_config` exists with `order: start-first` and `failure_action: rollback`; asserts `deploy.rollback_config` exists; asserts a `healthcheck` other than `["NONE"]` exists, because a health-gated rollout without one is a timer; asserts `stop_grace_period` is set on any service running a queue worker; and asserts every `secrets:` entry carries `mode`. Run against the kit's current output this reports six violations on the app service alone.
4. **`scripts/check-image-pinning.mjs`** — asserts every `image:` in a file matching `docker-compose.yml` or `docker-compose.swarm.yml` resolves to a tag that is not `latest` and not a bare major, and that a file marked as a release manifest pins by digest; asserts every `${IMAGE:-default}` default is itself compliant, which is precisely the `${..._DOCKER_IMAGE:-comment-service:latest}` case today's tooling passes.

Each check must be named at the rule it enforces, not only in a scripts index, so that a rule and the tool that reports its violation are never more than one line apart.

### RFCs against the live repository — `service-runtime-kit` v2.3.0

These are defects in the kit, not in the skill. They are recorded here as RFCs and must not be executed as skill edits. Every one was reproduced by rendering the kit's own fixture; line citations are to `service-runtime-kit/`.

**RFC-1 — Credentials carry `:-` defaults in production-shaped and Swarm files, and the checker passes them.** `scripts/render-runtime.sh:318` renders `DB_PASSWORD: ${DB_PASSWORD:-}` (empty default, by design per `contracts/service.runtime.env.example:52-55`); `:358` renders `RABBITMQ_PASSWORD: ${RABBITMQ_PASSWORD:-app}`; `:325` renders `DB_PROVISION_ADMIN_PASSWORD: ${DB_PROVISION_ADMIN_PASSWORD:-postgres}` (`service.runtime.env.example:62`), which also becomes `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` on the shared-infra database container. `scripts/validate-runtime.sh` returns exit 0 on this output. Under the ratified invariant all three take `:?` with no default. *Proposed fix:* a safety-control register in the kit, consumed by `validate_compose_interpolation_modifiers`, plus `require_env_value DB_PASSWORD` in `validate_service_env` (`render-runtime.sh:91-145`), which today requires `DB_USERNAME` but not `DB_PASSWORD`.

**RFC-2 — The service's own image floats.** `render-runtime.sh:289` renders `image: ${SERVICE_IMAGE_VAR:-<service>:latest}` into `docker-compose.yml` and `docker-compose.swarm.yml`. A stack deploy with the variable unset pulls or reuses whatever `:latest` currently resolves to. This contradicts `alaa-docker-production/references/full-guide.md:21` and `:134`.

**RFC-3 — No long-lived generated service has a healthcheck.** `platform-app-php` and `scheduler` carry no `healthcheck:` key in any of the three generated files; every worker carries `test: ["NONE"]` (`render-runtime.sh:1264-1265`). The Swarm stack therefore has no health signal at all, which makes any future health-gated rollout impossible and makes `restart_policy: condition: any` the only failure detection in the system.

**RFC-4 — The Swarm stack has no rollout control.** `render-runtime.sh:1693-1697` emits `deploy` with `endpoint_mode`, `resources.limits` and `restart_policy` only. `docker stack deploy` then applies its defaults: parallelism 1, no delay, `order: stop-first`, `failure_action: pause`. With no `replicas` key the default is one task, so `stop-first` on a single replica is a full outage of that service on every deployment. For a fleet with a 99.99% target this is the most consequential finding in the kit. *Proposed fix:* `update_config` with `order: start-first`, `failure_action: rollback`, an explicit `monitor`, plus a `rollback_config` — all of which require RFC-3 to be fixed first.

**RFC-5 — No runtime security hardening on any generated service.** `security_opt`, `cap_drop`, `read_only`, `tmpfs`, `pids_limit` and `user:` return zero occurrences across `render-runtime.sh` for `platform-app-php`, `worker` and `scheduler`. `full-guide.md:88-95` asks for four of the six.

**RFC-6 — Swarm secrets carry no ownership or mode.** `render-runtime.sh:1177` emits `source`/`target` pairs only; `uid`, `gid` and `mode` are absent, so secrets land at the default `0444` — world-readable inside the container. This contradicts `full-guide.md:72` and undercuts `entrypoint.sh:51-57`, which `chmod 600`s the Passport keys defensively but cannot touch a read-only secret mount.

**RFC-7 — The entrypoint's `APP_KEY` guard is unreachable for every worker.** `templates/generated/docker/octane/entrypoint.sh:59-61` runs `exec "$@"` when arguments are present, and the `APP_KEY` presence check is at `:63-66`, after it. Every generated worker and every per-queue worker sets `command:` (`render-runtime.sh:1248,1250-1253`), so all of them bypass the check. Today the Compose-level `${APP_KEY:?}` still catches the env-mode case, so this is latent rather than live — but it means the container's own guard protects only the app and scheduler roles, and with secret-files enabled it protects nothing.

**RFC-8 — The kit's own `up-local.sh` lacks the Windows path translation the generated one has.** `scripts/up-local.sh:93` builds `docker compose --project-directory "${repo_root}"` with a POSIX path. The generated wrapper handles MSYS explicitly (`render-runtime.sh:727-744` `is_msys_shell`/`to_docker_host_path`, applied at `:782-798`). Running the kit's copy from Git Bash on Windows passes `/c/Users/...` to Docker Desktop. The kit copy also omits `normalize_public_registry_prefix` and `ensure_runtime_env_fallbacks`, both of which the generated copy calls (`:781-783`).

**RFC-9 — Infra images are hardcoded in the generator and bypass the mirror.** `postgres:18.3` (`:1417,1485`), `rabbitmq:4.2.4-management` (`:1438,1535`), `redis:8.6.1-alpine` (`:1518`), `adminer:5.4.2` (`:1506`), `edoburu/pgbouncer:v1.24.1-p1` (`:1556,1747`) are literals with no `${PUBLIC_DOCKER_REGISTRY}` prefix and no override variable, so the mirror that governs the app's base image does not govern any of them, and bumping one requires a kit release. `README.md:181-182` already records the mirror-normalization gap for direct Dockerfile builds; this is the same gap on the Compose side.

**RFC-10 — The app healthcheck template boots the framework.** `templates/generated/docker/octane/healthcheck.sh:13` runs `php artisan octane:status` and `:16` runs `php artisan schedule:list`. Both boot Laravel per probe, and neither proves the HTTP listener answers. `full-guide.md:80` asks for the opposite. *Proposed fix:* a TCP or minimal HTTP probe against `${OCTANE_PORT:-8000}`.

### Out-of-batch findings

**`alaa-frontend-devops/references/15-build-time-vs-runtime-config.md:32`** states the no-default rule with an example list — "an auth toggle, a TLS verification flag, a rate limit, a CSP source list, a signing key" — that does not include a credential, while the ratified text this batch works from names "a credential in a production-shaped file" explicitly. The frontend file's list is frontend-shaped and reasonable for its own scope; the discrepancy is worth noting only so that Phase 2 writes the *fleet* register in `alaa-docker-production/references/25-` and does not inherit the frontend list as complete. No edit is proposed to `alaa-frontend-devops`.

**`alaa-frontend-devops/SKILL.md:16` and `references/90-companion-boundary.md:16`** already name `alaa-docker-production` with both trigger forms and route Dockerfile authorship to it. Once Phase 2 writes the reciprocal sentence, the boundary is agreed from both sides and no further edit to the frontend skill is required.

### On the size gap

**This skill is 16 KB against a batch peer at 231 KB, and it must grow.** That is not a judgment about tidiness; it is arithmetic against what the skill owns. It is the sole governor of the Dockerfile for every Laravel service in the fleet — `service-runtime-kit` deliberately generates no Dockerfile and says so at `README.md:182` — and it is the named owner of Compose authorship for the whole fleet plus the fail-closed interpolation invariant, and it must absorb five Dockerfile rules from the frontend batch. Its current 8 KB of rules contains zero code blocks and zero mentions of `.dockerignore`, layer ordering, BuildKit, `# syntax=`, `RUN --mount=type=secret`, `LABEL`, PID 1, `STOPSIGNAL`, `stop_grace_period`, `update_config`, `replicas`, `start_period`, `read_only`, `env_file`, or the string `:?`.

**How much.** Target **95-125 KB across `references/`**, roughly a sevenfold increase, with the always-loaded body flat or smaller. Rough per-file budget, sized by how much of it must be concrete values and worked examples rather than prose:

| File | Target | Why that size |
|---|---|---|
| `10-dockerfile-authorship.md` | 18-24 KB | The largest single gap. Needs two complete worked Dockerfiles — an Octane/Swoole PHP service and a Node SSR frontend runtime — because the five inbound rules are only checkable against a concrete layer order. |
| `20-compose-authorship.md` | 12-16 KB | Must cover the fleet's actual thirteen-service shape including profiles, bootstrap services and tool services, with a worked file. |
| `30-swarm-delivery.md` | 10-14 KB | The divergence table alone is thirty rows; rollout control needs values and reasoning, not names. |
| `25-fail-closed-interpolation.md` | 8-10 KB | The invariant is short; the safety-control register for the fleet is not, and the two grep traps need worked examples. |
| `40-healthcheck-and-lifecycle.md` | 8-10 KB | Four role shapes with real values plus the shutdown-signal chain. |
| `35-secret-delivery.md` | 7-9 KB | Two modes, the `_FILE` contract, rotation, and the `docker inspect` exposure argument. |
| `60-resource-limits-and-load.md` | 7-9 KB | Concrete OPcache, JIT, `nofile` and worker-sizing values, plus the `nproc` trap. |
| `45-registry-and-mirrors.md` | 6-8 KB | Two mechanisms stated distinctly, with the Hub-only limitation and the fleet's actual variable split. |
| `50-network-dns-and-exposure.md` | 6-8 KB | Absorbs existing content plus a real scope for "publicly". |
| `15-build-secrets-and-attestations.md` | 6-8 KB | Both secret forms, cache mounts, and the attestation flags with their new defaults. |
| `70-container-observability.md` | 4-5 KB | Mostly the logging keys plus routing; the catalogues belong elsewhere. |
| `90-companion-boundary.md` | 4-5 KB | Fifteen-row owner table, the kit seam, and the maintenance block. |
| `00-topic-map.md` | 2-3 KB | Fourteen rows with trigger conditions. |
| `00-source-map.md` | 2 KB | Preserved as-is. |
| `scripts/` (four checkers plus fixtures) | 15-25 KB | Four `.mjs` checkers with `--self-test` fixture trees. |

**And into what.** The growth is not prose. Roughly half of it should be two things the skill has never had: worked artifacts an agent can copy — a Dockerfile per stack shape, a Compose file, a stack file — and executable checks that report a violation. The remaining growth is the concrete values that turn today's twenty-one preference sentences into constraints: probe intervals, file modes, OPcache settings, `nofile` numbers, rollout parameters. A skill that owns the container layer for a fleet at 99.99% and ships not one number and not one check is not under-documented; it is un-enforced, and the size gap is the visible symptom of that.

---

### Shared closing

#### Is a new skill genuinely required?

**No.**

Every gap I found in this lane sits inside `alaa-docker-production`'s existing charter and closes by growing it. Dockerfile authorship is already its ownership under D8 and is merely unwritten. Compose and Swarm authorship is already its ownership and is merely thin. The fail-closed interpolation invariant is already assigned to it by the ratification and appears nowhere in its text — that is a writing task, not an ownership vacuum. Container-level observability is the one criterion where a new owner might be argued for, but the argument fails: the requirement level belongs to `alaa-observability-soc`, the field and metric names belong to `alaa-services-contract`, and what remains — the `logging:` key and its rotation options — is a Compose key, which is definitionally this skill's.

The one gap that genuinely has no owner today is the **safety-control variable register**: the fleet-wide list of variable names for which a `:-` default is a defect regardless of syntax. It has no owner because nobody has written it, not because writing it would violate anyone's boundary. It belongs in `alaa-docker-production/references/25-fail-closed-interpolation.md`, because the ratification names this skill as the invariant's owner for Compose authorship and because the register is a statement about interpolation form, which is exactly what this skill owns against `service-runtime-kit-governance`'s ownership of variable naming. Proposing a new skill for it would split one rule across two owners and reproduce the precise failure — an invariant enforced syntactically by one party and semantically by nobody — that this lane found in the live kit.
## Appendix B — `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`

### 1. What this skill is today

**Shape.** One `SKILL.md` (10,623 bytes, 181 lines; body after frontmatter 9,951 bytes / 177 lines), one `agents/openai.yaml` (4 lines), eight files under `references/` totalling 28.6 KB, two Python validators under `scripts/` (17.0 KB + 11.0 KB), and six files under `assets/templates/` — not seven. There is no `references/00-topic-map.md`; with exactly eight references the inline router at `SKILL.md:30-37` is the correct form under this programme's convention.

**Frontmatter.** `SKILL.md:3` is 626 characters, contains no angle brackets, and ends with a genuine negative clause: *"Do not use it for unrelated Kubernetes manifests, unrelated Dockerfiles, or non-GitLab CI systems."* This passes the description bar outright — one of the few skills in this batch that does.

**Body structure.** Start-fast fact list (`SKILL.md:8-20`), a when-not-to-use block (`:22-26`), the reference router (`:28-39`), a six-step core workflow (`:41-55`), then five topical rule blocks — authoring (`:57-68`), variables and inputs (`:70-81`), runner (`:83-92`), container build (`:94-104`), security (`:106-114`) — an eight-layer debugging ladder (`:116-131`), an output contract (`:133-142`), a template list (`:144-156`), a validation-script list (`:158-163`), failure-mode handling (`:165-169`) and a subagent block (`:171-181`).

**What it covers well.** The variable-mechanism decision boundary (`references/variables-and-inputs.md:14-52`) is a real decision rule, not a reminder: `spec:inputs` for compile-time configuration, CI/CD variables for runtime values, file variables for opaque credentials tools expect as a path, dotenv for intra-pipeline handoff, with an explicit prohibition at `:46` — *"Do not pretend a YAML-defined variable is a file variable."* The security reference (`references/security-and-hardening.md`) states a preferred credential order (`:53-55`), a threat model per runner type (`:13-31`), and fork/merge-request risk questions (`:64-75`). The failure-triage symptom map (`references/validation-and-debugging.md:122-130`) maps seven literal GitLab error strings to a cause class, which is exactly the shape that makes an agent fast.

**What it is missing, measured mechanically.** Across every `.md`, `.py`, `.yml`, `.yaml` and `.toml` file in the skill:

| Term | Occurrences | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| `allow_failure` | **0** | the single most load-bearing word in RFC 0001 §2 does not appear |
| `environment:` | **0** | no deployment environment, no `environment:url`, no `on_stop` |
| `rollback` | **0** | no rollback path expressed anywhere |
| `cache:key`, `key: files` | **0** | the skill owns cache-key expression under D8 and has no text on it |
| `cache:policy` | **0** | the one `policy:` hit at `SKILL.md:111` is `pull_policy`, unrelated |
| `fallback_keys` | **0** | GitLab's five-key fallback mechanism is unmentioned |
| distributed cache / S3 | **0** | nothing on why a cache does not survive between Kubernetes pods |
| job `timeout:` | **0** | the live kit sets `timeout:` on all twelve jobs; the skill never says to |
| `retry` | 2, both inside one YAML example (`references/pipeline-authoring.md:102-107`) | the correct `retry:when:` shape is *shown* and never *stated as a rule* |

**Cross-skill routing.** The skill names exactly one other skill in its entire text — `/alaa-prompting-guide` (`$alaa-prompting-guide`) at `SKILL.md:175`, with both trigger forms, correctly. There is no companion-boundary section, no ownership table, and no route to `alaa-docker-production`, `alaa-k8s-helm`, `alaa-frontend-devops`, `alaa-security-review`, `alaa-reliability-sla`, `alaa-observability-soc`, `alaa-services-contract` or `alaa-cicd-laravel-postgres`.

---

#### 1a. What the live pipeline actually does, and what the skill would have produced

Ground truth is `/mnt/user-data/uploads/service-ci-kit/gitlab/platform-app-php-service.yml` (339 lines), included in full by `content`, `auth` and `ticket` as their entire pipeline (RFC 0001:6, :24).

**Stage graph.** Seven stages at `:70-78` — `make_tag_version → build → smoke → release → bootstrap → deploy → export`. Twelve concrete jobs plus two YAML-anchor templates (`.base_job` at `:79`, `.build_template` at `:154`).

**`rules:` usage.** A `workflow:rules` block at `:62-68` with three arms: tag pipelines `when: never`, `main` with a commit message not matching `/^chore: Release /` `when: always`, then a terminal `- when: never`. That is a correctly closed workflow gate and matches `references/pipeline-authoring.md:29-37` in shape. But only **one** of the twelve jobs carries job-level `rules:` — `build` at `:172-173`. Every other job is unconditional, and the "should this run?" decision lives inside the shell scripts as `if [[ "${RELEASE_REQUIRED:-false}" != "true" ]]; then ... exit 0; fi` (`release.sh:5-8`, `check_db_ready.sh:7-10`, `verify_app_secret.sh:7-10`, `migrate_db.sh:7-10`, `deploy.sh:5-8`, `bootstrap_signing_keys.sh:5-8`, `build_image.sh:76-79`). The pipeline is therefore green-and-skipped rather than not-created; a reader sees twelve passing jobs that did nothing. The skill has no rule about this pattern in either direction.

**`needs:` usage.** Every job declares `needs:` with explicit `artifacts: true|false` (`:98`, `:169-171`, `:183-187`, `:207-211`, `:227-231`, `:243-247`, `:259-263`, `:275-279`, `:291-295`, `:308-314`, `:328-330`). This is a genuine DAG and it is well formed — the `artifacts: true|false` discrimination is precisely the `needs`-versus-`dependencies` distinction at `references/pipeline-authoring.md:76-81`, applied correctly. **But every one of those twelve jobs also carries `resource_group: "$RELEASE_RESOURCE_GROUP"`** (`:99`, `:158`, `:182`, `:206`, `:226`, `:242`, `:258`, `:274`, `:290`, `:307`, `:327`). A resource group admits one job at a time. With a single group applied to every job, the DAG cannot parallelise anything: `check_db_ready` and `bootstrap_signing_keys` are DAG-siblings and will still run one after the other. The `needs:` graph buys exactly nothing at runtime. The skill states both primitives — `SKILL.md:60` "Prefer `needs` for DAG execution when stages alone would block parallelism" and `SKILL.md:62` "Use `resource_group` for deploys or any job that mutates a shared target" — and never states that the second annihilates the first when applied globally. An agent following this skill would have written exactly this pipeline.

There is a second, sharper `needs:` consequence. `bootstrap_signing_keys` (`:253-267`) is needed by nothing. `deploy` (`:301-319`) needs `make_tag_version`, `release` and `migrate_db` — not `bootstrap_signing_keys`. Under DAG semantics a job runs when *its own* needs complete, so `deploy` is free to run while `bootstrap_signing_keys` is still running or after it has failed. The pipeline goes red, but the deploy already happened. The skill's DAG guidance (`references/pipeline-authoring.md:64-81`) says only "Avoid adding `needs` everywhere" and never states the inverse hazard: a `needs:` graph that omits an edge does not merely lose ordering, it removes a precondition.

**Cache strategy.** There is none. `cache:` does not appear once in 339 lines. Every job re-downloads or rebuilds. `build` runs `docker buildx build --build-arg BUILDKIT_INLINE_CACHE=1` (`build_image.sh:212-219`) with no `--cache-from`, so the inline cache is written and never read. The skill's `references/container-build-strategies.md:90-102` prescribes exactly the missing piece — a separate `buildcache-$CI_COMMIT_REF_SLUG` image and deliberate import/export — so this is a case where the skill's guidance *would* have improved the live pipeline, and did not reach it.

**Artifact retention.** `make_tag_version` publishes `.service-ci-kit/`, `ci/helm/` and a dotenv report with `expire_in: 1 week` (`:145-152`); `.build_template` publishes a dotenv with `expire_in: 1 week` (`:161-164`); `export_db` publishes `artifacts/db-export/*.sql` with `expire_in: 1 week` (`:336-339`). Every artifact block sets `expire_in`. This satisfies `references/pipeline-authoring.md:20` and the validator's `artifacts-expire-missing` note. It is also the only D8-routed obligation the live pipeline gets right, and the skill's total text on the subject is the four words "and `artifacts:expire_in`" in a bulleted list.

**Runner tags and executor.** Three tags in use: `semantic-release` (`:97`, `:205`), `local` (`:157`), `k8s` (`:181`, `:225`, `:241`, `:257`, `:273`, `:289`, `:306`, `:326`). `local` and `semantic-release` jobs use `GIT_STRATEGY: fetch` inherited from `:4` with `GIT_DEPTH: "1"` (`:3`) and run host binaries — `semantic-release` (`make_tag_version.sh:21-24`), `docker buildx` (`build_image.sh:94-96`) — so they are shell executors on persistent hosts. `k8s` jobs set `GIT_STRATEGY: "none"` and shell out to `kubectl`/`helm`, so they are Kubernetes-executor pods with a pre-baked toolchain image supplied by runner config. No job declares `image:` anywhere. The skill's runner guidance is directly on point — `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:37` "Avoid `GIT_STRATEGY: fetch` on shared or less-trusted hosts" — but the skill has no rule for the case the kit actually presents, which is a pipeline with **no `image:` key at all** because the runner supplies it. Its entire pinning apparatus (`SKILL.md:63`, validator rules `image-latest` and `image-unpinned`) inspects a key that does not exist here, and it says nothing about pinning the runner-side `image =` in `config.toml` instead. That gap is exactly the reciprocal half of frontend gate 2, which names "the CI runner image tag" as an inspected artifact (`alaa-frontend-devops references/20-ci-gates-and-predicates.md:16`).

**Image build path.** Shell runner, `docker buildx build --target runtime --push` against a local Docker daemon (`build_image.sh:212-219`). Not BuildKit rootless, not Kaniko, not DinD-in-Kubernetes. The skill's build-strategy ladder (`SKILL.md:96-102`) does not contain this case — "shell runner with a host daemon" is the option it omits, and `references/security-and-hardening.md:40` classes it as high risk ("Shell runner jobs with host-level container tooling") without offering a hardened form of it. The pre-push idempotency logic (`build_image.sh:129-180`: does the semver tag already exist, does its digest match the SHA tag, does the Git tag exist) is genuinely careful work that the skill has no vocabulary for at all.

**Deployment mechanism.** `helm upgrade --install` against an OCI chart (`deploy.sh:262-286`), with `--atomic` (`:283`), `--wait`, and a `--timeout` derived from `CI_JOB_TIMEOUT` minus a 75-second buffer (`:100-111`). Pre-flight: namespace reachability probe (`:69-92`), image-pull-secret existence and type check (`:32-42`), a pending-release-lock recovery path (`:132-154`). On failure it dumps Helm status, history, workloads, HPAs and the last 80 events (`:116-126`). This is a considerably more mature deploy than anything the skill describes; the skill's deploy vocabulary is `resource_group` and nothing else, with `environment:` appearing zero times.

**Secret handling.** All secrets arrive as `$MAIN_*` CI variables mapped into local names at `:2`, `:13-15`, `:22-23`, `:39`, `:47`. None is inline. Registry login uses `--password-stdin` (`build_image.sh:135`), as does Helm registry login (`deploy.sh:169`, `bootstrap_signing_keys.sh:54-57`) — matching the validator's `docker-login-stdin` rule. Secret material written to disk is guarded by `umask 077` before `mktemp` (`check_db_ready.sh:128`, `migrate_db.sh:159`, `export_db.sh:84`, `bootstrap_signing_keys.sh:60`) and removed by an `EXIT` trap. That is careful. **What the skill would not have flagged and should:** `make_tag_version.sh:19` and `release.sh:23` both run `git remote set-url origin "…://oauth2:${GITLAB_TOKEN}@${CI_SERVER_HOST}/…"`, writing a long-lived personal token in cleartext into `.git/config` on a *shell runner* whose build directory persists between jobs and between projects on that host. `references/security-and-hardening.md` has no rule covering a credential written into a persistent workspace file, and neither validator inspects script bodies for `set-url`.

**Net assessment.** Of the eight dimensions above, the skill's current guidance would have produced or improved three (workflow rules shape, `expire_in`, registry cache strategy), is silent on three (cache keys, job timeouts, environments/rollback), and actively contains the two rules whose *interaction* produced the pipeline's most expensive defect (`needs:` plus a global `resource_group`) without ever stating that interaction. Neither validator flags a single line of this file.

---

#### 1b. What RFC 0001 declares, and whether this skill respects the "decides no gate" ruling

**RFC 0001's definition of a gate** is at `docs/rfc/0001-…:53-56`, stated as the source of most CI illusions:

> "**A step that cannot fail the pipeline is advisory, regardless of what it is called.**"

with the corollary at `:56`: *"A pipeline that is 100% advisory and 100% green is indistinguishable from one that verified everything."* The RFC's asks are two, together (`:58`): the gates themselves, and a register saying of every check whether it is a gate or advisory, its threshold, and what a non-zero exit obliges. The register itself is at `:186-195` — eight rows, seven gates and one deliberate advisory. RFC `:265` assigns ownership of that register explicitly: *"The Alaa skills pack `alaa-cicd-laravel-postgres` carries the gate register…"*

**Does `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` contradict it?** No. **Does it duplicate it?** No. **Is it silent on it?** Almost entirely — and the silence is the finding, because under the programme's rule silence is not delegation.

- The word `allow_failure` appears zero times in the skill. So does `|| true`. The RFC's central defect class has no expression in the skill's text and no rule in either validator. A pipeline built to this skill can be 100% advisory and the skill will not notice.
- RFC `:30` and `:217` state the bare-retry defect: *"A bare retry count retries *every* failure class, including an assertion failure… GitLab supports `retry: { max: 1, when: [runner_system_failure, stuck_or_timeout_failure] }`, which retries infrastructure and not logic."* The skill **shows** that exact shape at `references/pipeline-authoring.md:102-107` and never states it as a rule, never explains why, and never checks for its absence. The live pipeline's `.base_job` carries `retry: 1` (`platform-app-php-service.yml:81`) and `validate_gitlab_ci.py` reports nothing.
- RFC `:215` — *"the verify jobs must not set `GIT_STRATEGY: none`"* — and RFC `:205-213` — every new job needs `make_tag_version` with `artifacts: true` — are provider-half obligations about how a gate is *expressed*, which is squarely this skill's half of D8. The skill covers neither.
- RFC `:165` — *"On the releasing branch, unreachable is row two: the job fails"* — requires the skill's half: how "is this the releasing branch" is written as a `rules:if` condition. The skill is silent, and this is precisely the kind of predicate its `references/variables-and-inputs.md:105-112` rules block exists to govern.
- RFC `:219` — *"The PostgreSQL service image must be pinned to the same major and minor as production… from one variable, in one place"* — is a pinning obligation on a `services:` entry. `validate_gitlab_ci.py` inspects `services:` only for the DinD pattern (`:303-317`) and never for tag pinning.

**Test of the "decides no gate" ruling.** The binding sentence is `alaa-frontend-devops/SKILL.md:16`: *"`/alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` (`$alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`) owns how a gate is expressed on a runner and decides no gate."*

Two places where the current text decides a gate:

1. **`SKILL.md:63` — "Pin images and tools to explicit versions. Do not introduce `latest` in production CI."** This is a predicate on a build input, stated as this skill's own rule with no owner named. It is the same predicate as frontend gate 2 (`alaa-frontend-devops references/20-ci-gates-and-predicates.md:16`: *"A floating tag such as `node:lts` fails this gate"*), which that skill declares and this skill therefore does not get to declare. The correct provider-half sentence is the mechanism, not the prohibition: how a pinned reference is *written* (tag versus `@sha256:` digest, `image:name` plus `entrypoint`, `default:image`, the `services:` entry, and the runner-side `image =` in `config.toml`). This is the sentence Phase 2 must rewrite.

2. **The `--fail-on-warnings` flag makes the skill's validators a gate without a register.** `scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py:363-364` returns exit 2 when any warning is present under that flag. Nine warning-severity rules therefore become blocking conditions — `image-latest`, `image-unpinned`, `only-except`, `dind-service`, `run-experimental`, `set-x`, `docker-login-stdin`, `secret-inline`, `debug-trace` — and the skill's text never says whether an agent should use the flag, never states a threshold, and never distinguishes gate from advisory. This is the exact illusion RFC 0001 §2 exists to remove, shipped inside the skill that must not decide gates. The fix is not to remove the flag; it is to state, once, that the flag is the caller's choice and that which of these must block is the calling skill's decision, and to make each rule's severity legible as gate-eligible or advisory.

A third, lesser case: **`SKILL.md:101` — "Do not recommend Kaniko for new designs."** This is a tool-choice policy that overlaps `alaa-docker-production`'s image-expression half. Its factual basis is now stronger than when written (see §7), so keep the rule but state it as a runner-side build-path selection with the container-image half routed.

---

#### 1c. Validator results against the live kit, verbatim

Both scripts were copied to `/tmp/gl/` and run under Python 3.11.15 with PyYAML 6.0.3.

**`validate_gitlab_ci.py` against `gitlab/platform-app-php-service.yml`:**

```
No issues found.
EXIT=0
```

With `--fail-on-warnings`: identical, `EXIT=0`.

The fleet's entire production pipeline — no lint job, no static-analysis job, no test job, a blanket `retry: 1` that converts an assertion failure into a flake, `interruptible: true` inherited onto `migrate_db`, a global `resource_group` that annihilates the `needs:` DAG, a `deploy` that can outrun `bootstrap_signing_keys`, zero cache anywhere, and a `variables:` entry GitLab cannot expand — passes the skill's own checker with zero findings. **A checker that returns clean on a pipeline RFC 0001 opens by calling "no code gate of any kind" (`:13`) is not a conservative checker; it is a checker aimed at a different question than the one that matters.**

**`validate_runner_config.py` against the same file:**

```
ERROR   …/platform-app-php-service.yml [gitlab-url-missing] values.yaml is missing gitlabUrl
WARNING …/platform-app-php-service.yml [runner-token-missing] values.yaml does not declare runnerToken or runnerTokenSecret
WARNING …/platform-app-php-service.yml [runners-config-missing] values.yaml has no runners.config block
EXIT=1
```

Three confident, entirely wrong findings and a failing exit code. `detect_kind` (`validate_runner_config.py:175-189`) routes on file extension alone: any `.yml`/`.yaml` is assumed to be a Helm values file. `validation-and-debugging.md:26-27` tells the agent to run both scripts, so this misrouting is reachable by following the skill's own instructions.

**Against the skill's own templates:** `values-k8s-runner.yaml` → `No issues found`, `EXIT=0`. `config-shell-runner.toml` → one `shell-risk` warning, `EXIT=0`. Both correct.

**Four reproduced defects in `validate_gitlab_ci.py`.**

*(i) `!reference` is reported as a YAML syntax error.* GitLab's `!reference [.job, key]` tag is valid, extremely common, and `yaml.safe_load_all` (`:103`) cannot construct it:

```
ERROR   t1.yml:14 [yaml-syntax] YAML syntax error: could not determine a constructor for the tag '!reference'
EXIT=1
```

The `ConstructorError` is a `YAMLError` subclass, so it is caught at `:104` and mislabelled `yaml-syntax`. The file is then abandoned and **no other check runs on it**.

*(ii) The fleet's own thin wrapper produces two false ERRORs.* `README.md:24` states every service repository keeps a "`.gitlab-ci.yml` (thin wrapper)" that includes the platform file. A wrapper adding a `verify` job exactly as RFC 0001 §6 prescribes:

```
ERROR   t4.yml:5 [extends-missing] Job 'test_sqlite' extends unknown job or template '.base_job'
ERROR   t4.yml:5 [needs-missing] Job 'test_sqlite' needs unknown job 'make_tag_version'
EXIT=1
```

`extends-missing` (`:223-227`), `needs-missing` (`:229-240`), `dependencies-missing` (`:242-246`) and `stage-missing` (`:217-218`) all resolve against `job_names` gathered from the single file (`:200`) and are emitted at `error` severity. Any file with an `include:` will produce false errors for every cross-file reference. The kit's architecture guarantees it for every service in the fleet.

*(iii) The `default:` block is invisible to every job-level check.* Given a file with `default: image: node`, `default: cache:` without a key, and `default: before_script:` containing `set -x; echo $SECRET_TOKEN`:

```
WARNING t2.yml:7 [secret-inline] Variable 'API_TOKEN' looks like a hardcoded secret
WARNING t2.yml:9 [docker-login-stdin] Job 'build' runs 'docker login' without '--password-stdin'
NOTE    t2.yml:9 [artifacts-expire-missing] Job 'build' has artifacts without 'expire_in'
EXIT=0
```

The unpinned `node` image, the keyless cache and the `set -x` around a secret are all missed. `default` is in `GLOBAL_KEYWORDS` (`:25`) so `iter_jobs` skips it (`:136-141`), and the image check (`:274-285`), cache check (`:287-291`) and `set -x` check (`:319-322`) read `job.get(...)` only. `default:` is the documented modern form of exactly the keys GitLab has now deprecated at top level.

*(iv) An unpinned image behind a registry with a port is not flagged.* `registry.example.com:5000/team/php` returns `No issues found`. `UNPINNED_IMAGE_RE = re.compile(r"^[^:@]+$")` (`:62`) treats the port colon as a tag separator. Any private registry with an explicit port defeats the pinning check entirely — and private registries with ports are the fleet's normal case (`PRIVATE_REGISTRY` at `platform-app-php-service.yml:13`).

**What both validators miss that the ground truth contains.** No check for: a bare integer `retry:`; `interruptible: true` on a job that mutates a shared target; a single `resource_group` applied to every job in a `needs:` graph; a DAG job that nothing needs; `allow_failure: true` or a script ending `|| true`; a missing job `timeout:`; a `variables:` value using shell-style `${VAR:-default}` (which GitLab does not expand — `platform-app-php-service.yml:102` sets `CI_SERVER_PROTOCOL: "${CI_SERVER_PROTOCOL:-https}"`, and GitLab's expander reads the whole brace body as a name, yielding empty and overwriting the predefined variable); `cache:key:files` exceeding its documented two-path limit; `fallback_keys` exceeding five; a cache key not derived from a lockfile; deprecated top-level `image`/`services`/`cache`/`before_script`/`after_script`.

**Crash reported as a finding.** With PyYAML unavailable, `validate_gitlab_ci.py` raises an uncaught `ImportError` and exits **1** — the same code as "errors found". `File not found` also exits **1**. Three distinct outcomes (could not run, crashed, found real errors) collapse into one exit code.

### 2. Ten-criteria verdict

| # | Criterion | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Correctness and testability | **FAIL** | Two validators ship 28 and 30 rule ids, but the skill's own checker returns `No issues found` / `EXIT=0` on `platform-app-php-service.yml`, which RFC 0001:13 opens by calling "no code gate of any kind"; `!reference` is mislabelled a syntax error (`scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py:103-106`); the fleet's standard wrapper shape yields two false ERRORs (`:223-227`, `:229-240`); neither script has a self-test |
| 2 | Failure behaviour | **FAIL** | `retry` appears only inside one YAML fragment (`references/pipeline-authoring.md:102-107`) and never as a rule; job `timeout:` appears zero times; `allow_failure` zero times; `environment:`/`on_stop`/rollback zero times; nothing on what a partially failed DAG does; no route to `alaa-reliability-sla` at any call site |
| 3 | Security | **SATISFIED** | Threat model per runner type (`references/security-and-hardening.md:13-31`), preferred credential order (`:53-55`), fork/MR risk questions (`:64-75`), pull-policy rules (`:77-85`); enforced by `secret-inline`, `set-x`, `docker-login-stdin`, `dind-service`, `debug-trace` in `scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py` and `kube-privileged`, `kube-if-not-present`, `helm-privileged` in `scripts/validate_runner_config.py`. Gap noted in §3, not fatal |
| 4 | Observability | **FAIL** | No rule anywhere on job-log structure, on writing evidence to an artifact rather than a log (the obligation RFC 0001:157 states as "a warning nobody reads is not a warning"), on `environment:` for deployment tracking, or on pipeline-duration signals; `alaa-observability-soc` is not named at any call site |
| 5 | Concurrency and load | **FAIL** | `resource_group` is stated once (`references/pipeline-authoring.md:85-91`) with no mention of `process_mode`, no `parallel:`/`parallel:matrix` guidance, nothing on `concurrent` in `config.toml` (set blindly to `4` in `assets/templates/config-shell-runner.toml:1` with no rationale), nothing on cache contention between concurrent jobs sharing a key, and — the live defect — nothing on `resource_group` cancelling the parallelism `needs:` was added to buy |
| 6 | Clean code, SOLID, design patterns | **SATISFIED** | Reuse ladder with a stated stopping condition: `SKILL.md:64` "Reuse hidden jobs, components, or includes when repetition is real. Do not abstract a one-off job."; `references/pipeline-authoring.md:113` "Split files when it improves ownership or reuse, not just to make the main YAML shorter"; hidden-job, include and component boundaries at `:92-135` |
| 7 | Algorithm and data-structure choice | **FAIL** | The job graph is this domain's data structure and no complexity budget is stated: no critical-path reasoning, no `needs:` size limit, no statement of what a stage boundary costs in wall-clock, and `references/pipeline-authoring.md:74` reduces the whole subject to "Avoid adding `needs` everywhere" |
| 8 | Configurability | **SATISFIED** | A real decision boundary between compile-time and runtime configuration (`references/variables-and-inputs.md:14-52`), a precedence-collision list (`:54-66`), a mandated variable inventory table (`:135-143`), and typed `spec:inputs` named as the boundary-validation mechanism (`:22-26`) |
| 9 | Speed of development and debuggability | **SATISFIED** | A cheapest-check-first validation ladder (`references/validation-and-debugging.md:12-20`), concrete `glab ci lint` invocations (`:38-42`), and a symptom map from seven literal GitLab error strings to a cause class (`:122-130`). Weakened by the two conflicting triage taxonomies in §6 but the material is concrete and fast |
| 10 | Documentation | **SATISFIED** | An enumerated output contract at `SKILL.md:133-142` — final YAML/TOML, variable table, runner prerequisites and tags, what was validated statically versus live, assumptions and minimum versions, and a hardening note when privileged mode or shell runners are used — reinforced by `SKILL.md:55` and `:167-169` |

**Tally: 5 SATISFIED, 5 FAIL, 0 NOT-OWNED.** No `NOT-OWNED` verdict is legitimate for this skill: it names exactly one other owner in its entire text (`SKILL.md:175`, `/alaa-prompting-guide`), and that call site covers only model and effort selection.

---

### 3. Defect classes actually found

**1. Stale model pins — NOT PRESENT.** No model name anywhere. `SKILL.md:175` routes correctly and states why: *"a model name written into a skill goes stale silently and is copied forward because it looks authoritative."*

**2. Wrong trigger syntax — NOT PRESENT, but only one call site exists.** `SKILL.md:175` gives both `/alaa-prompting-guide` and `$alaa-prompting-guide`. `agents/openai.yaml:4` uses the bare `$` form, correct for a Codex-only file. The defect is that there is nothing else to check: the boundary this skill owes has no call sites at all.

**3. Duplication between body and references — PRESENT, systematic.** Six pairs found mechanically and more by reading; enumerated in §5.

**4. Project-specific content in an always-loaded body — NOT PRESENT.** The body is generic GitLab guidance. No fleet-specific names.

**5. Long numbered procedures nobody reads in order — PRESENT.** `SKILL.md:116-131` is an eight-item ordered list of failure layers with no symptom, no diagnosis and no escalation attached to any item — it is a list of nouns. `references/validation-and-debugging.md:48-121` restates the same subject as five classes *with* symptoms and checks, which is the correct restructuring. The body keeps the worse version and the reference keeps the better one.

**6. Description without a "do not use for" clause — NOT PRESENT.** `SKILL.md:3` ends with an explicit negative clause; 626 characters, no angle brackets.

**7. Fragile tooling — PARTIALLY PRESENT.** No `Path(__file__).parents[N]`, no temp directories inside the repository, both files read with an explicit `encoding="utf-8"`. But every documented invocation is a bare relative path — `python3 scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py` (`SKILL.md:160-161`, `references/validation-and-debugging.md:26-27`) — which assumes the working directory is the skill root. An agent working inside a service repository resolves that to the *service's* `scripts/` directory or to nothing. Additionally `validate_runner_config.py:10` imports `tomllib` with no version guard (Python 3.11+ only) and both files import `yaml` at module top with no guard.

**8. Shipped `__pycache__` — NOT PRESENT.** No `__pycache__`, no dotfiles anywhere in the skill tree.

**9. Measured against section 2 — done in §2 above.** Five FAILs: failure behaviour, observability, concurrency and load, algorithm/data-structure choice, and correctness-and-testability of the shipped checks.

**10. Shrink where possible — PRESENT.** ~45 body lines (`SKILL.md:57-114`) restate reference content and can move, buying room for the boundary table and the D8 sentence without net growth.

**11. Companion boundary — ABSENT ENTIRELY.** No section names what this skill does not own. Three owners it demonstrably overlaps and never names: `alaa-docker-production` (the whole of `references/container-build-strategies.md` and `SKILL.md:94-104`), `alaa-k8s-helm` (`SKILL.md:88-89` and `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:54-106` write Helm `values.yaml`), and `alaa-frontend-devops` (whose `references/90-companion-boundary.md:15` routes four subjects *into* this skill that it has no text on). Under D8 this skill is the named provider half for three other skills; a provider that names no consumer is a boundary asserted from one side only.

**12. Two routers — NOT PRESENT.** There is no `references/00-topic-map.md`, so there is no duplicate router. With exactly eight references the inline list at `SKILL.md:30-37` is the correct form. `references/SOURCES.md` is the provenance ledger and is legitimate; note only that it is named differently from this programme's `00-source-map.md` convention and is listed in the router as though it were a topical reference (`SKILL.md:37`).

**13. Rules with no tool that reports a violation.** The skill ships **two** executable checks. `scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py` emits **28** distinct rule ids, of which 6 are parse/IO outcomes (`file-not-found`, `file-read-error`, `yaml-syntax`, `yaml-empty`, `yaml-root`, `yaml-documents`), leaving **22 substantive assertions**: stage list type and duplicates; reserved job names; jobs without an action; `only`/`except` presence; undefined stage; invalid `when`; unresolved `extends`/`needs`/`dependencies`; `${VAR}` braces inside `rules:if`; variable syntax inside `rules:changes`/`exists`/`compare_to`; `latest` and untagged images; cache without a key; artifacts without `expire_in`; the `run` keyword; `CI_DEBUG_TRACE`; a DinD service; `set -x`; `docker login` without `--password-stdin`; MR-aware job rules with no `workflow:` block; and a hardcoded-looking secret in a `variables:` map. `scripts/validate_runner_config.py` emits **30** rule ids, 6 parse/IO, leaving **24 substantive**, of which **one is unreachable**: `kube-rofs-paths` (`:79-88`) keys off `read_only_root_filesystem` under `pod_security_context` or `build_container_security_context`, and that key is not documented under any `[runners.kubernetes]` security-context table (verified §7). **Rules with no check at all** — every one of them a preference rather than a rule today: `SKILL.md:61` `interruptible`, `:62` `resource_group`, `:65` include/child boundaries, `:73` file variables, `:78` variable precedence, `:87` explicit runner tags, `:89` the YAML-versus-TOML rule, `:100` Kaniko, `:102` DinD isolation, `:108` `id_tokens`, `:110` fork risk, and the entirety of the D8-routed cache-key, job-graph and retention obligations.

---

### 4. Boundary map

**What this skill owns and must say so.** How a gate is expressed on a runner: the job graph, `rules:` and `needs:`, every expression of a cache key (`cache:key`, `key:files`, `key:prefix`, `fallback_keys`, `policy:`), artifact retention and `expire_in`, the runner image reference and the mechanism by which any image reference is pinned, runner tags and job placement, `config.toml` and Helm `runners.config`, executor selection and tuning, variable and input mechanics and precedence, and CI failure triage. **It decides no gate.**

**What it does not own, and which skill does.** None of the following is named anywhere in the current text; every row is a defect until Phase 2 writes it.

| Subject | Owner | Evidence it belongs there |
|---|---|---|
| the frontend delivery gate register — the predicate a gate asserts, the command that evaluates it, the artifact it inspects | `/alaa-frontend-devops` (`$alaa-frontend-devops`) | `alaa-frontend-devops/SKILL.md:16` and its `references/20-ci-gates-and-predicates.md:5` |
| the PHP/Laravel gate register, the test-database policy, migration reversibility, the `composer audit` severity floor and acceptance record | `/alaa-cicd-laravel-postgres` (`$alaa-cicd-laravel-postgres`) | RFC 0001:265, and the register itself at RFC 0001:186-195 |
| Dockerfile authorship, layer ordering, layer-invalidation avoidance, multi-stage separation, image minimisation, Compose authorship and the fail-closed interpolation invariant | `/alaa-docker-production` (`$alaa-docker-production`) | `alaa-frontend-devops references/90-companion-boundary.md:16`; D8 |
| Helm chart authorship, chart values semantics, RBAC and namespace design as cluster policy | `/alaa-k8s-helm` (`$alaa-k8s-helm`) | this skill writes runner `values.yaml` only; chart authorship is not runner configuration |
| how a cache or routing decision is expressed as a proxy directive | `/alaa-haproxy` (`$alaa-haproxy`) | D8 |
| threat classification, exposure severity, rotation and disclosure, fail-closed doctrine for security decisions | `/alaa-security-review` (`$alaa-security-review`) | programme doctrine-owner list |
| why a timeout, retry, backoff, idempotency or degradation mechanism exists, and fail-open for availability | `/alaa-reliability-sla` (`$alaa-reliability-sla`) | programme doctrine-owner list; this skill states only how a timeout or retry is *written* in YAML |
| what a pipeline or deploy event must emit, its requirement level and retention | `/alaa-observability-soc` (`$alaa-observability-soc`) | programme doctrine-owner list |
| shared names and values: `OTEL_*` names, metric catalog, log fields, canonical shared-infra names, host-port table | `/alaa-services-contract` (`$alaa-services-contract`) | programme doctrine-owner list |
| test design, proof levels, what a given check proves | `/alaa-testing-strategy` (`$alaa-testing-strategy`) | programme doctrine-owner list |
| change control and proof strength for a production change | `/alaa-controlled-ops` (`$alaa-controlled-ops`) | programme doctrine-owner list |
| Make targets and how a command is invoked from a Makefile | `/alaa-makefile` (`$alaa-makefile`) | this skill writes the job that calls the target, not the target |
| CDN origin bucket, lifecycle and invalidation | `/alaa-minio-object-storage` (`$alaa-minio-object-storage`), `/alaa-arvan-object-storage` (`$alaa-arvan-object-storage`) | D8 |
| model selection and reasoning effort | `/alaa-prompting-guide` (`$alaa-prompting-guide`) | already correct at `SKILL.md:175` |

**The reciprocal D8 sentence Phase 2 must write** — this is the exact text, and it belongs in the always-loaded body:

> `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` owns how a gate is expressed on a runner — the job graph, `rules:` and `needs:`, every expression of a cache key, artifact retention and `expire_in`, and the runner image reference — and **decides no gate**. Which checks must exist, what each asserts, and whether a non-zero exit blocks or informs belong to the skill that owns that stack: `/alaa-frontend-devops` (`$alaa-frontend-devops`) for a frontend repository, `/alaa-cicd-laravel-postgres` (`$alaa-cicd-laravel-postgres`) for a PHP or Laravel service. When a request asks this skill to decide whether a check should block, name the owner and write the mechanism, not the verdict.

**The floating-toolchain split, stated as this skill's half.** The prohibition — that a floating tag such as `node:lts` fails the toolchain gate — is `alaa-frontend-devops`'s and is not restated here. This skill owns the **pinning mechanism**: how a pinned reference is written in each of the five places one can appear — `image:` on a job, `default: image:` (with `name:` plus `entrypoint:` when the image has one), a `services:` entry, `image =` inside `[runners.kubernetes]` in `config.toml` or `runners.config`, and `helper_image` — the choice between a version tag and an `@sha256:` digest and when each is required, how a private registry with an explicit port is written so it is still recognisable as pinned, and how a single pinned value is fanned out from one variable to every consumer so a version move is one edit (the obligation RFC 0001:219 states as "from one variable, in one place").
