### 5. Duplication

Every pair below states the same instruction twice. The body copy should go; the reference copy stays and absorbs the body's wording where the body's is sharper.

| Body | Reference | Instruction stated twice |
|---|---|---|
| `SKILL.md:16` | `references/container-build-strategies.md:108` | "Whether the job really needs a Docker daemon" / "Does the job truly need a Docker daemon?" |
| `SKILL.md:59` | `references/pipeline-authoring.md:16`, `:27` | prefer `workflow:rules` plus job `rules` |
| `SKILL.md:60` | `references/pipeline-authoring.md:15`, `:66` | prefer `needs` where stages block parallelism |
| `SKILL.md:61` | `references/pipeline-authoring.md:19` | `interruptible: true` on cancel-safe jobs |
| `SKILL.md:62` | `references/pipeline-authoring.md:85` | `resource_group` for any job that mutates a shared target |
| `SKILL.md:72` | `references/variables-and-inputs.md:20` | `spec:inputs` for compile-time reusable configuration |
| `SKILL.md:76` | `references/security-and-hardening.md:59` | never hardcode secrets |
| `SKILL.md:77` | `references/variables-and-inputs.md:72` | masked variables are not safely composable |
| `SKILL.md:80` | `references/variables-and-inputs.md:111` | no variable expansion in `rules:changes` / `rules:exists` |
| `SKILL.md:86` | `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:16-24`, `references/security-and-hardening.md:17` | shell runner is trusted-code-on-a-trusted-host, stated in **three** places |
| `SKILL.md:87` | `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:36` | explicit runner tags, deliberate placement |
| `SKILL.md:89` | `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:58-63` | `runners.config` is TOML, not YAML |
| `SKILL.md:90` | `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:95` | restrict `allowed_images` / `allowed_services` / `allowed_pull_policies` |
| `SKILL.md:99` | `references/container-build-strategies.md:49` | DinD only when daemon behaviour is truly required |
| `SKILL.md:101` | `references/container-build-strategies.md:20` | do not recommend Kaniko |
| `SKILL.md:102` | `references/container-build-strategies.md:55-60` | DinD needs a dedicated privileged runner and isolation |
| `SKILL.md:108` | `references/security-and-hardening.md:53-55` | prefer `id_tokens` and `secrets` over long-lived keys |
| `SKILL.md:109` | `references/security-and-hardening.md:35-40` | privileged runners, shell runners, `docker.sock` are high risk |
| `SKILL.md:110` | `references/security-and-hardening.md:64-75` | be explicit about merge-request and fork risk |
| `SKILL.md:111` | `references/security-and-hardening.md:81-84` | prefer `pull_policy: always` on shared runners |

**A duplication that is also a contradiction.** `SKILL.md:116-131` enumerates **eight** failure layers; `references/validation-and-debugging.md:48-121` enumerates **five** classes for the same triage decision. An agent that loads both gets two incompatible taxonomies and no rule for choosing. State the five-class version once, in the reference, and leave the body a single sentence naming the discipline: classify before editing.

**One reference-to-reference duplication.** `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:119-158` (troubleshooting checklist: job never starts / pod slow / pod starts but scripts fail / DinD fails) restates `references/validation-and-debugging.md:75-121` (runner matching / executor startup / runtime failure) at a different granularity. Keep the executor-specific detail in the runner file and the classification in the debugging file, and cross-route once.

---

### 6. Wording-test failures

Each of these could be followed exactly by a competent agent that still does the wrong thing.

1. **`SKILL.md:20` — "make safe modern assumptions, state them briefly, and continue."** "Safe" and "modern" are abstract nouns standing in for observable conditions, and the sentence gives no way to tell whether an assumption qualifies. The concrete half is in the same sentence and should be all of it.

2. **`SKILL.md:20` — "Default to GitLab 18.x behavior, Runner 18.x behavior."** A stated version floor that is one full major behind reality (§7). An agent follows this exactly and writes to a superseded baseline.

3. **`SKILL.md:60` — "Prefer `needs` for DAG execution when stages alone would block parallelism."** A preference verb where a constraint was meant, and — worse — the sentence is silently false wherever a shared `resource_group` covers the same jobs. The rule needs its scope: `needs:` buys parallelism only among jobs that do not contend for one resource group.

4. **`SKILL.md:62` — "Use `resource_group` for deploys or any job that mutates a shared target."** Correct in isolation and dangerous at scale, because it does not bound the group. The live pipeline applies one group to all twelve jobs and serialises everything. The missing clause: one resource group per shared target, named after the target, never one group for the pipeline.

5. **`SKILL.md:63` — "Pin images and tools to explicit versions. Do not introduce `latest` in production CI."** A prohibition with no positive replacement (tag or digest? which one when?), and a gate decided by a skill that decides no gate (§1b). It also names only the `latest` string, so `moby/buildkit:rootless` — a floating tag shipped in this skill's own template at `assets/templates/k8s-buildkit-rootless.gitlab-ci.yml:11` — passes the rule as written.

6. **`SKILL.md:64` — "Reuse hidden jobs, components, or includes when repetition is real."** "Real" is unobservable. State a count or a change-coupling test.

7. **`SKILL.md:78` — "Watch precedence carefully when mixing pipeline variables, project or group variables, job variables, dotenv artifacts, and downstream pipelines."** "Watch carefully" is not an instruction. The reference already has the observable version (`references/variables-and-inputs.md:124-133`, a six-question ordered check); the body should route to it, not gesture at it.

8. **`SKILL.md:85` — "Shell runner is for trusted code on a trusted host. Do not treat it as the default safe runner."** A prohibition with no positive replacement and no definition of "trusted". `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:18-24` gives four conditions; the body should carry the pointer, not the adjective.

9. **`SKILL.md:102` — "prefer a dedicated privileged runner, isolated nodes, and TLS-enabled setup unless a documented platform constraint forces otherwise."** Three requirements joined by a preference verb, with an escape hatch that names no one who may grant it. If DinD without isolation is ever acceptable, say who decides; if it is not, say "must".

10. **`SKILL.md:112` — "Separate safe defaults from risky alternatives. If the user requests the risky path, provide it with clear isolation requirements."** "Clear" is unobservable and the sentence permits shipping the risky path on request with no named approver. Compare RFC 0001:241, which draws exactly this line and draws it between humans and agents: *"No agent may accept, ever."*

11. **`references/pipeline-authoring.md:20` — "Use pinned images, explicit caches, and `artifacts:expire_in`."** Three obligations in nine words with no mechanism for any of them, in the reference that is supposed to hold the mechanism. This one line is the entirety of the skill's text on two of the four subjects D8 routes to it.

12. **`references/pipeline-authoring.md:74` — "Avoid adding `needs` everywhere. Over-specified DAGs are harder to maintain and easier to break during refactors."** States the cost of too many edges and never the cost of too few — which is the failure the live pipeline actually has, where `deploy` omits an edge to `bootstrap_signing_keys` and can therefore deploy without it.

13. **`references/validation-and-debugging.md:30` — "These scripts are intentionally conservative."** Read as a disclosure of a design choice, this is now false in both directions: the scripts are not conservative enough to catch anything in the live pipeline, and not conservative enough to avoid two false ERRORs on the fleet's standard wrapper. Replace with a stated scope: what each script asserts, and what it cannot see (anything behind an `include:`, anything in `default:`, anything a `!reference` tag touches).

14. **`SKILL.md:167` — "If a feature is beta, experimental, or version-gated, say so and include the minimum version."** Correct, and unenforceable as written because `references/feature-version-notes.md` supplies no version numbers to include (§7).

---

### 7. Stale or unverifiable claims

All checks performed 2026-07-29 against official sources.

| # | Claim and location | Source checked | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `SKILL.md:20`, `references/feature-version-notes.md:7`, `:23` — "Default to GitLab 18.x behavior, Runner 18.x behavior" / "assume a modern GitLab 18.x baseline" / "This design assumes GitLab 18.x and Runner 18.x" | https://docs.gitlab.com/policy/maintenance/ ; https://docs.gitlab.com/releases/ | **STALE — one full major behind.** GitLab 19.0 shipped 2026-05-21; 19.1 is released, 19.2 is in flight, and the docs site serves version **19.3**. Patch releases now read "19.1.2, 19.0.4, 18.11.7". The runner docs serve **19.0**. Every 18.x baseline statement in this skill must become 19.x |
| 2 | Implied release cadence (the skill states none) | https://docs.gitlab.com/policy/maintenance/ | **MISSING, and it is the fix for #1.** Minor releases are "Monthly, scheduled for the third Thursday of each month"; patch releases twice monthly; majors "in May each year, by default", with 20.0 expected May 2027. Backports: bug fixes to the current stable release only; security fixes to the current plus the previous two monthlies. A skill that stated the cadence would not need to restate a version number |
| 3 | `references/feature-version-notes.md:7` — "`spec:inputs` is mature enough for modern reusable CI design, but very old GitLab versions may not support it" | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/components/ | **OBSOLETE CAUTION.** Components and the CI/CD Catalog were "Made generally available in GitLab 17.0" (experiment 16.0-16.1, beta 16.6-16.7). On an 18/19 fleet the caveat is dead weight. The docs also state "using a version published to the CI/CD catalog is recommended", and give the version-resolution precedence — commit SHA over tag over branch, with `~latest` and partial semver for published components — none of which this skill mentions |
| 4 | `references/feature-version-notes.md:12` — "Some component-expression contexts and matrix features are still evolving" | as above | **UNVERIFIABLE AS WRITTEN.** No feature named, no version, no test. Cannot be acted on or refuted |
| 5 | `references/feature-version-notes.md:9` — "`run` is not the safe default for production pipeline design… verify current status and prefer normal `script` jobs" | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/functions/ | **HOLDS, but the name has moved.** `run` now invokes **GitLab Functions**, which is explicitly "an experimental feature in active development and is subject to breaking changes". The feature was renamed from *CI/CD Steps*: `step:` → `func:`, `step.yml` → `func.yml`, both old forms deprecated. The advice is right; the skill cannot help an agent that meets a `func.yml` |
| 6 | `SKILL.md:59` — "Avoid introducing `only` or `except`"; `scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py:214-215` `only-except` warning | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/yaml/deprecated_keywords/ | **HOLDS.** "`only` and `except` are deprecated. To control when to add jobs to pipelines, use `rules`." Not removed; they "could be scheduled for removal in a future major milestone". Warning severity is the right severity |
| 7 | *Not claimed anywhere* — deprecated **global** keywords | same page | **GAP, and the skill ships a violation.** Globally-defined `image`, `services`, `cache`, `before_script`, `after_script` are deprecated in favour of `default:`. `assets/templates/k8s-dind-tls.gitlab-ci.yml:19` declares a top-level `services:` block. `validate_gitlab_ci.py:24-38` lists all five in `GLOBAL_KEYWORDS` and flags none. The same page also deprecates `only:refs`/`only:variables`/`only:changes`/`only:kubernetes`, the `publish` keyword and the `pages` job name for Pages, and `environment:kubernetes:namespace` / `:flux_resource_path` — the skill mentions none |
| 8 | `SKILL.md:60`, `references/pipeline-authoring.md:64-81` — `needs:` and DAG behaviour, no limit stated | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/yaml/ ; https://docs.gitlab.com/administration/instance_limits/ | **INCOMPLETE.** The YAML reference does not state a per-job `needs` maximum; the instance-limits page exposes a `ci_needs_size_limit` plan limit, adjustable on self-managed via the Plan Limits API or Rails console. The skill should state that a limit exists, is a plan limit, and is instance-dependent — rather than a number that will go stale |
| 9 | `SKILL.md:108`, `references/security-and-hardening.md:53` — prefer `id_tokens` / OIDC | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/secrets/id_token_authentication/ | **HOLDS and is now stronger.** ID tokens are GA across Free/Premium/Ultimate on .com, Self-Managed and Dedicated, with additions in 17.3, 18.4, 18.7 and 18.9. `CI_JOB_JWT` / `CI_JOB_JWT_V2` are deprecated and produce `401 Unauthorized`. The skill states the preference and shows **no `id_tokens:` YAML anywhere** — a preference for a mechanism whose syntax the skill never gives |
| 10 | *Not claimed anywhere* — secure files | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/secure_files/ | **GAP.** Secure files are GA on all tiers. The `download-secure-files` tool was deprecated in **GitLab 18.6**; the current method is `glab securefile`. `references/variables-and-inputs.md:37-45` recommends file variables for kubeconfigs, TLS certs and JSON credentials without naming the platform feature built for exactly that, which violates the standing "prefer the official capability and wrap it" preference |
| 11 | `SKILL.md:100`, `references/container-build-strategies.md:20` — "Do not recommend Kaniko for new designs" | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/docker/using_kaniko/ | **HOLDS, and is now understated.** GitLab's own page states "kaniko is no longer a maintained project" and directs users to Docker, Buildah, or Podman. The skill's "do not recommend" can be strengthened to "do not use", with the maintenance status as the reason |
| 12 | `SKILL.md:97-98`, `references/container-build-strategies.md:14-20` — "Default to BuildKit rootless"; DinD third | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/docker/using_docker_build/ | **DIVERGES FROM GITLAB'S OWN ORDER, defensibly — but the divergence must be stated.** GitLab documents shell executor, then Docker-in-Docker ("This is the recommended approach when your runner supports `privileged` mode"), then socket binding, then Windows pipe binding, and lists BuildKit and Buildah under "Docker alternatives" for when privileged mode is unavailable. This skill inverts that on security grounds, which is the right call for a 99.99% security-sensitive fleet — but a claim that contradicts the vendor's stated recommendation must say so and say why, or the next reader will "correct" it back |
| 13 | `assets/templates/k8s-dind-tls.gitlab-ci.yml:11`, `:20` — `docker:24.0.5-cli`, `docker:24.0.5-dind` | https://endoflife.date/docker-engine | **STALE AND UNSAFE.** Docker Engine 24.0 reached end of life **2024-06-08**, over two years ago; the branch's final release was 24.0.9 (2024-02-01). Current is 29.6.2 (2026-07-16); 29 and 25.0 are the only supported lines, and 25.0 ends 2026-12-04. Note that GitLab's own `using_docker_build` page still shows `docker:24.0.5-cli`/`-dind` in its examples — the template copied a stale vendor example. `SKILL.md:156` calls these templates "hardened starting points" |
| 14 | `assets/templates/component-with-inputs.gitlab-ci.yml:6`, `assets/templates/values-k8s-runner.yaml:21`, `:27` — `alpine:3.20` | https://alpinelinux.org/releases/ | **STALE.** Alpine 3.20 ended support **2026-04-01**. Current stable is **3.24** (released 2026-06-09, supported to 2028-06-01); 3.23, 3.22 and 3.21 are also still supported. `values-k8s-runner.yaml:21` makes 3.20 the *default job image for every Kubernetes runner job* |
| 15 | `assets/templates/k8s-buildkit-rootless.gitlab-ci.yml:11` — `moby/buildkit:rootless` | this skill's own `SKILL.md:63` | **SELF-CONTRADICTION.** `rootless` is a floating tag with no version component — functionally identical to `latest` for the purpose of the rule at `SKILL.md:63`. The skill's own validator misses it too, because `LATEST_TAG_RE` (`scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py:61`) matches only the literal string `latest` |
| 16 | `scripts/validate_runner_config.py:79-88` — `kube-rofs-paths`, keyed on `read_only_root_filesystem` under `pod_security_context` / `build_container_security_context` | https://docs.gitlab.com/runner/executors/kubernetes/ | **UNREACHABLE RULE.** The executor documents `pod_security_context`, `build_container_security_context`, `helper_container_security_context`, `service_container_security_context` and `init_permissions_container_security_context`, and `read_only_root_filesystem` appears in none of them. The rule cannot fire on a valid config. Everything else the script keys on — `logs_base_dir`, `scripts_base_dir`, `allowed_images`, `allowed_services`, `allowed_pull_policies`, `namespace_per_job` — is confirmed correct |
| 17 | `references/feature-version-notes.md:11` — "Runner registration tokens are on the way out" | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/runners/new_creation_workflow/ | **HOLDS but is three years understated.** "The legacy workflow that uses registration tokens is not recommended." Since **GitLab 17.0** instance administrators and group owners can disable legacy registration outright. Authentication tokens are `glrt-`-prefixed. The skill's own templates already use `glrt-` (`assets/templates/config-shell-runner.toml:7`, `assets/templates/values-k8s-runner.yaml:3`), so only the prose is soft |
| 18 | *Not claimed anywhere* — `cache:key:files` and `fallback_keys` limits | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/yaml/ ; https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/caching/ | **GAP with hard numbers available.** `cache:key:files` accepts "an array of up to two file paths or patterns" — a hard limit and therefore a checkable rule. `fallback_keys` supports "up to five fallback keys". `artifacts:expire_in`, when unset, "defaults to the instance wide setting" — which is why "set it deliberately" is a real rule and not housekeeping. Cache is stored where the Runner is installed and uploaded to S3 only when distributed cache is enabled; without it, a Kubernetes-executor cache does not survive between pods. The skill owns all of this under D8 and states none of it |

**Is `references/feature-version-notes.md` an adequate freshness story?** No. It is 1,489 bytes and 24 lines, and it contains **not one version number** — every entry is a hedge ("very old GitLab versions may not support it", "can require a recent Runner", "sometimes version- or platform-scoped", "on the way out", "still evolving"). The only concrete claim it makes, the 18.x baseline at `:7` and `:23`, is the one thing in it that is now wrong. It also cannot answer the question it exists for: an agent asking "is this feature safe in production?" gets "verify before recommending them" with no source to verify against and no rule for what to do with the answer.

The replacement is structural, not additive. State the **cadence** (monthly minors on the third Thursday, majors in May, backports to current stable plus two for security) so an agent can compute the current baseline instead of reading a stale one. State, per feature, only the facts that do not move: the GA milestone where one exists (components and catalog GA in 17.0; ID tokens GA), the experimental status where it persists (GitLab Functions, and its `step:`→`func:` rename), the hard limits (`cache:key:files` ≤ 2, `fallback_keys` ≤ 5), and the deprecations that carry a replacement (`only`/`except` → `rules`; global `image`/`services`/`cache`/`before_script`/`after_script` → `default:`; `CI_JOB_JWT*` → `id_tokens`; registration tokens → authentication tokens, disable-able since 17.0; `download-secure-files` → `glab securefile`, deprecated 18.6). Then route the moving parts to `references/SOURCES.md`, which already lists the right URLs — `SOURCES.md:22` points at the release-notes index and `:32` at the Runner releases feed, and those two links are worth more than every hedge in the current file.

---

#### 7a. Defects found in the live `service-ci-kit` repository — these become RFCs, not skill edits

Ordered by consequence. None of these is a skill change.

**R1. `migrate_db` is `interruptible: true`.** `.base_job` sets `interruptible: true` (`gitlab/platform-app-php-service.yml:80`) and every job inherits it. `release` (`:204`), `deploy` (`:305`) and `export_db` (`:325`) explicitly override to `false`. **`migrate_db` (`:285-299`), `ensure_db_privileges` (`:269-283`) and `bootstrap_signing_keys` (`:253-267`) do not.** A superseding push to `main` cancels a running `php artisan migrate --seed` mid-transaction. The Kubernetes Job itself carries `backoffLimit: 0` (`ci/scripts/migrate_db.sh:171`), so nothing retries it, and the GitLab job is killed while the pod runs on unsupervised. Every job that mutates the database must set `interruptible: false`.

**R2. One `resource_group` on every job destroys the `needs:` DAG.** All twelve jobs set `resource_group: "$RELEASE_RESOURCE_GROUP"` (`:99`, `:158`, `:182`, `:206`, `:226`, `:242`, `:258`, `:274`, `:290`, `:307`, `:327`). A resource group admits one job at a time, so the carefully built DAG runs strictly serially and the pipeline pays the full sum of its twelve `timeout:` budgets. Scope the group to what it protects: the shared deployment target needs it (`release`, `bootstrap`, `deploy`, `export`); `make_tag_version`, `build` and `smoke` do not.

**R3. `deploy` can outrun `bootstrap_signing_keys`.** `bootstrap_signing_keys` (`:253-267`) is needed by no job. `deploy` (`:308-314`) needs `make_tag_version`, `release` and `migrate_db` only. Under DAG semantics `deploy` starts as soon as those three finish, so it can deploy an application whose signing keys were never created — or were being created concurrently. Currently masked because `SIGNING_KEYS_BOOTSTRAP_ENABLED` defaults to `"false"` (`:55`); the day it is switched on, the race is live. Add the missing `needs:` edge.

**R4. `CI_SERVER_PROTOCOL: "${CI_SERVER_PROTOCOL:-https}"` cannot work.** `make_tag_version` sets this at `:102`. GitLab's variable expansion supports `$VAR` and `${VAR}`; it does not implement shell-style `:-` defaults, and reads the entire brace body as the variable name. The name `CI_SERVER_PROTOCOL:-https` does not exist, so the expansion yields empty — and this assignment **overwrites the predefined `CI_SERVER_PROTOCOL`** for the job. `make_tag_version.sh:19` and `release.sh:23` then build `git remote set-url origin "${CI_SERVER_PROTOCOL:-https}://…"`, where the shell default *does* apply and rescues it. The YAML line is at best inert and at worst destroys a predefined variable other tooling may read. Delete it.

**R5. A long-lived token is written into `.git/config` on a persistent shell runner.** `ci/scripts/make_tag_version.sh:19` and `ci/scripts/release.sh:23` run `git remote set-url origin "…://oauth2:${GITLAB_TOKEN}@${CI_SERVER_HOST}/${CI_PROJECT_PATH}.git"`. `GITLAB_TOKEN` is `$MAIN_GITLAB_TOKEN` (`:2`), a personal token with `read_repository` and push rights. These jobs run on the `semantic-release` shell runner with `GIT_STRATEGY: fetch` (`:4`), so the build directory — and the `.git/config` holding the cleartext token — persists on the host between jobs and between projects sharing that runner. Use a credential helper writing to a job-scoped file, or `http.extraHeader` supplied per invocation, and never `set-url`.

**R6. `pg_dump` output is exfiltrated through container logs, silently truncatable.** `ci/scripts/export_db.sh:135` runs `pg_dump --clean --if-exists --format=plain --no-owner --no-privileges` writing to **stdout**, then `:156` collects it with `kubectl logs "${pod_name}" -c db-export > "${dump_file}"`. Two consequences. First, the entire production database — schema and data — passes through the kubelet log pipeline and any log shipper attached to the cluster, then lands as a GitLab artifact retained for a week (`gitlab/platform-app-php-service.yml:336-339`). Second, `kubectl logs` returns only what the container-log rotation retains (10 MiB per file by default): a dump larger than that is **silently truncated**, `wait_for_export_job` still sees `succeeded > 0`, and the job reports success while publishing a corrupt partial dump. Write to a volume and copy it, or stream to object storage, and verify the dump before declaring success.

**R7. `retry: 1` is applied to every job, including `release` and `migrate_db`.** `.base_job:81`. RFC 0001:30 already states the general defect. Two specific instances the RFC does not name: `release` runs `semantic-release` (`ci/scripts/release.sh:52`), which pushes a Git tag and creates a release — retrying a partially completed publish is not idempotent; and `migrate_db` retries a migration whose Kubernetes Job deliberately sets `backoffLimit: 0` to prevent exactly that. Narrow to `retry: {max: 1, when: [runner_system_failure, stuck_or_timeout_failure]}` and opt the mutating jobs out entirely.

**R8. Two different fallback chart versions for the same chart.** `ci/scripts/deploy.sh:166` uses `chart_ver="${HELM_CHART_VERSION:-0.2.6}"`; `ci/scripts/bootstrap_signing_keys.sh:31` uses `chart_ver="${HELM_CHART_VERSION:-0.1.1}"`. `HELM_CHART_VERSION` comes from `$MAIN_HELM_CHART_VERSION` (`gitlab/platform-app-php-service.yml:21`); when that CI variable is unset the mapping yields empty, both fallbacks fire, and the same pipeline bootstraps against chart 0.1.1 and deploys against 0.2.6. A missing required version must fail, not default.

**R9. Fail-open defaults for the target namespace.** `namespace="${HELM_NAMESPACE:-vk}"` appears in `deploy.sh:15`, `migrate_db.sh:15`, `check_db_ready.sh:14`, `verify_app_secret.sh:12`, `export_db.sh:7` and `bootstrap_signing_keys.sh:15`. If `MAIN_HELM_NAMESPACE` is unset the pipeline migrates and deploys into a namespace literally named `vk` rather than stopping. Same class: `image_pull_secret_name="${IMAGE_PULL_SECRET_NAME:-gitlab-runner-registry}"` (`deploy.sh:17`, `migrate_db.sh:16`) and `DOCKER_OCTANE_BASE_IMAGE:-registry.takhtenegar.ir/docker/octane-base:v1.3.1` (`build_image.sh:107`), which hardcodes a registry hostname and a base-image pin inside a script default. Every value that selects a deployment target should be `require_env`.

**R10. `php artisan migrate --seed` runs on every production deploy.** `ci/scripts/migrate_db.sh:186`, in a pod with `APP_ENV=production` (`:201`). Seeders on a production database on every release is a data-integrity hazard independent of whether today's seeders happen to be idempotent. Separate the seed step and gate it.

**R11. Mixed expansion levels inside the migration heredoc.** `ci/scripts/migrate_db.sh:163-218` uses an unquoted heredoc, so `${PASSPORT_ENSURE_PERSONAL_CLIENT:-true}` at `:190` is expanded by the *CI runner's* shell before the manifest is written, while `\${provider}` at `:193-194` is escaped to survive into the pod. Two expansion levels in one block with no marker. The default also disagrees with the platform default: the script defaults to `true` (`:190`), the pipeline sets `"false"` (`gitlab/platform-app-php-service.yml:54`).

**R12. World-readable secret file in the `mktemp` fallback path.** `ci/scripts/lib.sh:94-101` falls back to `( set -o noclobber; : > "${candidate}" )`, which creates the file with the process umask rather than `mktemp`'s 0600. Three callers set `umask 077` first (`check_db_ready.sh:128`, `migrate_db.sh:159`, `export_db.sh:84`, `bootstrap_signing_keys.sh:60`) and are safe; `deploy.sh:241` writes `DB_PASSWORD` into a values overlay via `mktemp_portable_file` with **no preceding `umask`**. Set the umask inside `mktemp_portable_file`, or `chmod 600` immediately after creation.

**R13. `docker buildx` writes an inline cache nothing reads.** `ci/scripts/build_image.sh:214` passes `--build-arg BUILDKIT_INLINE_CACHE=1` with no `--cache-from`. Every build is cold. The kit's own skill guidance for this is `references/container-build-strategies.md:99-102`.

**R14. Divergent timeout defaults between the pipeline and the scripts.** `EXPORT_JOB_DEADLINE_SECONDS` is `1200` in the pipeline (`gitlab/platform-app-php-service.yml:51`) and `480` in the script default (`ci/scripts/export_db.sh:14`); `EXPORT_WAIT_SECONDS` is `1500` versus `540` (`:52` / `:15`). The script defaults are currently dead because the pipeline always sets them, which is precisely why they will rot unnoticed and mislead the next reader.

**R15. `smoke` remains a toolchain check named as a verification step.** `gitlab/platform-app-php-service.yml:190-198` runs `helm version` and `kubectl version --client`. RFC 0001:257 records this as deliberately deferred, so it is not a new finding — but it is the live instance of the RFC's own §2 illusion and should be tracked against the deferral, not forgotten.

---

### 8. Router audit

**Form is correct.** Eight reference files, listed inline at `SKILL.md:30-37` with one trigger condition each and a stopping instruction at `:39` — *"Do not read every reference by default. Load the narrowest file that matches the task."* No `references/00-topic-map.md` exists, so there is no duplicated router and no defect class 12.

**Trigger conditions, judged one by one.** Six of the eight are usable: `pipeline-authoring.md` ("new or refactored pipelines, rules, DAGs, includes, components, child pipelines"), `variables-and-inputs.md`, `runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md`, `container-build-strategies.md`, `security-and-hardening.md` and `validation-and-debugging.md` all name observable task features. Two are weak:

- `SKILL.md:36` — *"`references/feature-version-notes.md` when a feature might be gated, beta, experimental, or version-dependent."* "Might be" is not a condition an agent can evaluate before opening the file, and the file cannot answer once opened (§7).
- `SKILL.md:37` — *"`references/SOURCES.md` when latest/current GitLab, Runner, security, or feature behavior matters."* Correct in substance, but `SOURCES.md` is a provenance ledger, not a topical reference, and listing it in the same router as the six topical files invites an agent to treat it as content. Under this programme's convention it should be named `00-source-map.md` and routed from a maintenance line, not from the topic router.

**Six routing pointers are repeated after their own rule block** — `SKILL.md:68`, `:81`, `:92`, `:104`, `:114`, `:131` — each naming the file the router at `:30-37` already named. That is a second router in effect, spread across the body. It is defensible as an in-context reminder and cheap to keep, but only if the rule blocks themselves shrink to the pointer (§10).

**What the router does not cover.** No route for a cache question, a job-timeout question, an artifact-retention question, an `environment:`/rollback question, or a "does this check block?" question — because no reference answers any of those. The router is honest about the skill's contents; the contents are the problem.

**Reference sizing.** All eight are between 1.5 KB and 5.0 KB, well within one-hop budget. `feature-version-notes.md` at 1.5 KB is the smallest and the emptiest.

---

### 9. Scripts and assets audit

#### `scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py` (17,040 bytes, 369 lines)

**What it asserts.** 28 rule ids, 6 of them parse/IO outcomes, leaving 22 substantive assertions: `stages` is a list of unique strings; job names do not collide with reserved keywords; a non-hidden job has `script`/`run`/`trigger`/`release`; `only`/`except` are not used; `stage` is declared; `when` is one of six values; `extends`/`needs`/`dependencies` resolve within the file; `rules:if` does not use `${VAR}`; `rules:changes`/`exists`/`compare_to` contain no variable syntax; a job image is not `latest` and not untagged; a job cache has a key; job artifacts have `expire_in`; `run` is flagged experimental; `CI_DEBUG_TRACE` is not enabled; a DinD service is called out; `set -x` is not used; `docker login` uses `--password-stdin`; MR-aware job rules are accompanied by `workflow:`; and top-level or job-level `variables:` do not carry a hardcoded-looking secret.

**Windows.** **Yes, with two caveats.** Pure Python, no shell-out, no `__file__` path arithmetic, and — importantly — both file reads pass `encoding="utf-8"` explicitly (`:94`), so the Windows cp1252 default cannot corrupt a UTF-8 pipeline. It handles CRLF correctly because `splitlines()` (`:78`) strips `\r` and the YAML parser normalises line endings, so no trailing-carriage-return class exists here. Caveats: `import yaml` (`:22`) is unguarded, so a missing PyYAML produces a traceback rather than a diagnosis; and every documented invocation is `python3 …`, which is not the interpreter name on a stock python.org Windows install.

**`--help`.** Yes, via argparse (`:334-338`): `--json` and `--fail-on-warnings` are both documented.

**Self-test.** **No.** Zero test functions, no fixture corpus, no `--self-test` flag. A checker whose rules cannot be regression-tested will drift silently — and four of its rules are already wrong (§1c).

**Exit codes.** **They do not distinguish "could not run" from "clean".** `:359-365` returns 1 on any error-severity issue, 2 on warnings under `--fail-on-warnings`, 0 otherwise. But `file-not-found` and `file-read-error` are error severity, so a missing file also returns 1; and an uncaught `ImportError` also returns 1. Three outcomes, one code. Reproduced in §1c.

**Correctness defects.** Four reproduced in §1c: `!reference` mislabelled as a syntax error and the file abandoned; cross-`include` references emitted as ERRORs; `default:` invisible to the image, cache, `set -x` and secret checks; registry-with-port images treated as pinned. Two smaller ones: the top-level `services:` check runs once **per job** (`:303`, falling back to `config.get("services")`), so one global DinD service produces one warning per job in the file; and `build_line_map` (`:76-84`) matches only `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+:` at line start, so a job name containing any other character reports line 0.

#### `scripts/validate_runner_config.py` (10,972 bytes, 234 lines)

**What it asserts.** 30 rule ids, 6 parse/IO, leaving 24 substantive: every `[[runners]]` has an `executor`; shell executor is flagged as weak isolation and checked for `builds_dir`/`cache_dir`; Kubernetes executor is checked for `privileged` (and, if privileged, for a node selector), `allowed_images`, `allowed_services`, `allowed_pull_policies` (with a specific warning for `if-not-present`), `namespace_per_job`, `pod_spec`, and the unreachable read-only-root-filesystem pairing; Helm values are checked for `gitlabUrl`, `runnerRegistrationToken`, `runnerToken`/`runnerTokenSecret`, an `rbac.create: false` without a named service account, `runners.privileged`, a present and TOML-valid `runners.config`, and `[[runners]]` inside it.

**Windows.** **Yes, with one hard gate.** Same clean profile as the sibling — UTF-8 reads at `:95` and `:117`, no path arithmetic — but `import tomllib` (`:10`) requires Python 3.11 or newer with no guard or fallback to `tomli`. On a Windows machine running 3.10 the script dies at import.

**`--help`.** Yes (`:193-197`), same two flags.

**Self-test.** **No.**

**Exit codes.** **Worse than the sibling.** `:224-230` uses the same 1/2/0 scheme, but the misrouting defect means a *correct* pipeline file returns 1 with three false findings (§1c). A caller cannot distinguish "this runner config is broken", "you handed me the wrong file", and "I could not start".

**Correctness defects.** Three. (a) `detect_kind` (`:175-189`) routes every `.yml`/`.yaml` to the Helm-values path on extension alone; it should require a positive signal (`gitlabUrl`, `runnerToken`, or a `runners:` mapping containing `config`) and otherwise exit "wrong tool". (b) `line_map` is computed at `:129` and **never read**; no `make_issue` call passes `line=`, so every issue this script emits has line 0 and the text formatter prints no location at all (`:220`) — confirmed in the §1c output. (c) `kube-rofs-paths` (`:79-88`) keys off a configuration key GitLab Runner does not document (§7 row 16), so the rule is unreachable. Both files also import `sys` and never use it (`validate_gitlab_ci.py:17`, `validate_runner_config.py:9`).

#### `assets/templates/` — six files, and one that does not exist

**`SKILL.md:148` lists `app-basic.gitlab-ci.yml`. The file is not in the repository.** It is referenced nowhere else. An agent following `SKILL.md:146` — "Use the templates in `assets/templates/` when they match the task closely" — will attempt to open a template that has never shipped, and `app-basic` is the name most likely to match a plain request.

| Template | Assessment |
|---|---|
| `component-with-inputs.gitlab-ci.yml` (17 lines) | Correct two-document component shape and correct `$[[ inputs.x ]]` interpolation. **Defects:** `alpine:3.20` default (`:6`) is out of support since 2026-04-01; no input declares `type:`, and none uses `options:` or `regex:`, so the compile-time validation the skill sells at `references/variables-and-inputs.md:22-26` is not demonstrated by the template that exists to demonstrate it; `:17` interpolates a free-text input directly into `sh -lc '…'`, which is a quoting hazard worth a comment |
| `config-shell-runner.toml` (15 lines) | Correct `glrt-` authentication token placeholder (`:7`), explicit `builds_dir`/`cache_dir` (`:10-11`) matching `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:38`. **Defects:** `concurrent = 4` (`:1`) with no rationale and no relation to host capacity; `MaxUploadedArchiveSize = 0` (`:15`) is an unexplained unlimited setting in a security-sensitive template — exactly the "a cap of 0 can mean unlimited" hazard this programme ratified for Compose; no `[runners.cache]` `Type`, so a reader learns nothing about distributed cache |
| `k8s-buildkit-rootless.gitlab-ci.yml` (38 lines) | The best template here: rootless BuildKit, runtime Docker config write (`:23-35`), registry cache import and export with a branch-scoped cache image (`:16`, `:38`). **Defects:** `moby/buildkit:rootless` (`:11`) is a floating tag, violating `SKILL.md:63`; the `buildctl-daemonless.sh` invocation at `:38` has been flattened into one line with runs of spaces where continuations belonged — it executes correctly but is unreadable and will be miscopied; the config.json heredoc writes registry credentials to `~/.docker/config.json` with no `umask` and no cleanup |
| `k8s-dind-tls.gitlab-ci.yml` (34 lines) | Correct TLS wiring (`DOCKER_HOST` 2376, `DOCKER_TLS_CERTDIR`, `DOCKER_CERT_PATH` at `:15-17`), `--password-stdin` (`:32`), `--pull` (`:33`), and an honest header comment about the privileged prerequisite (`:1`). **Defects:** `docker:24.0.5-cli` and `docker:24.0.5-dind` (`:11`, `:20`) are two years past end of life; `services:` is declared at top level (`:19`), which GitLab now lists as a deprecated global keyword |
| `shell-runner-trusted-host.gitlab-ci.yml` (37 lines) | Correct `GIT_STRATEGY: clone` for a shared host (`:12`), `resource_group: production` (`:33`), honest header comment. **Defects:** the cache key `${CI_PROJECT_PATH_SLUG}-${CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH}` (`:15`) is identical for every branch, so on a persistent shell runner every branch writes to one cache — a cross-branch poisoning path in the one template whose header calls out trust; no `policy:` and no lockfile-derived key, in the skill that owns cache-key expression; the `deploy` job gates on `$CI_COMMIT_BRANCH == $CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH` (`:35`), which is not the same as a protected ref, contradicting `SKILL.md:20` ("protected branches for release or deploy jobs"); no job sets `timeout:`; `pip install -r requirements.txt` (`:28`) with no `--require-hashes` in a deploy-capable pipeline |
| `values-k8s-runner.yaml` (32 lines) | The most complete artefact in the skill: `rbac.create`, named service account, `privileged: false` at both levels, `allowed_pull_policies = ["always"]`, `allowed_images`/`allowed_services` allowlists, `logs_base_dir`/`scripts_base_dir` for restricted clusters, an OS node selector. Passes its own validator cleanly. **Defects:** the default job image `alpine:3.20` (`:21`) is out of support, and the `allowed_images` allowlist (`:27`) contains `alpine:*` and `docker.io/library/*:*` — wildcards over an entire namespace, which is an allowlist in form only; `helper_image` is not pinned; no `[runners.cache]` block, so a reader gets no distributed-cache guidance |

### 10. Rewrite brief for Phase 2

#### Target sizes

- **`SKILL.md` body: at most 7,500 bytes / ~140 lines** (today 9,951 bytes / 177 lines). The five rule blocks at `:57-114` shrink to a pointer plus at most two sentences each once the twenty duplicated instructions listed in §5 move; that reclaims roughly 45 lines, of which about 25 are spent on the D8 sentence, the boundary pointer and the gate-versus-advisory disclaimer. Net shrink is required — the skill gains one genuinely new capability (the D8 provider half) but gains it in references and in two body paragraphs, not in a new body section.
- **Frontmatter:** keep `SKILL.md:3` almost as-is (626 chars, negative clause present, no angle brackets). Add one clause to the negative half: *"Do not use it to decide whether a check should block a pipeline; that belongs to the skill that owns the stack."* Keep the total at or below 900 characters.
- References may grow. This skill is under-covered on four subjects it owns and growth there is the correct answer.

#### Reference file list, one-line charter each

Twelve files, so a separate router becomes mandatory and the inline list at `SKILL.md:30-37` is replaced by a single pointer to it.

| File | Status | Charter |
|---|---|---|
| `references/00-topic-map.md` | **new** | The one router: a trigger condition per file and nothing else. `SKILL.md` carries a pointer to it and no second table |
| `references/00-source-map.md` | **rename** of `SOURCES.md` | The provenance ledger: which official URL is authoritative for which claim, the freshness triggers, and the conflict-resolution order. Keep every URL in the current `SOURCES.md:9-41` and the ordering at `:43-48`; it is the strongest part of the file |
| `references/90-companion-boundary.md` | **new** | What this skill owns, what it does not, and who does — the table in §4 above, with `/name` and `$name` at every row. Carries the reciprocal D8 sentence in full and the floating-toolchain split |
| `references/pipeline-authoring.md` | keep, trimmed | Workflow and job `rules:` semantics, include and component boundaries, child pipelines, and the reuse ladder. Sheds the DAG section to the new job-graph file |
| `references/job-graph-and-scheduling.md` | **new** | The job graph as this domain's data structure: `stages` versus `needs:`, what a stage boundary costs, the critical path, `needs:artifacts:`, the plan-limit on `needs` size, the missing-edge hazard, `parallel:` and `parallel:matrix`, `resource_group` scoping and process modes, `interruptible`, job `timeout:`, and `retry:` with its `when:` classes. **States explicitly that a shared `resource_group` cancels the parallelism `needs:` buys, and that a resource group is named after the target it protects, never after the pipeline** |
| `references/cache-artifacts-and-pinning.md` | **new** | Every expression of a cache key — `cache:key` string, `key:prefix`, `key:files` with its documented two-path limit, `fallback_keys` with its five-key limit, `policy: pull \| push \| pull-push`, `untracked`, `when` — plus where a cache lives, why it does not survive between Kubernetes pods without distributed cache, artifact scoping, `artifacts:when`, `artifacts:reports:dotenv`, `expire_in` and its instance-wide default, and the pinning mechanism for every place an image reference can appear (`image:`, `default:image` with `name:`/`entrypoint:`, `services:`, `image =` in `[runners.kubernetes]`, `helper_image`), including tag-versus-digest and the registry-with-port form |
| `references/variables-and-inputs.md` | keep, extended | Unchanged in substance; add `id_tokens:` YAML shape, the `secrets:` block, GitLab secure files with `glab securefile`, `spec:inputs` `type:`/`options:`/`regex:`, and the rule that GitLab expansion has no shell-style `${VAR:-default}` |
| `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md` | keep, trimmed | Executor architecture, `config.toml`, Helm `values.yaml` versus embedded TOML, RBAC and namespace design, restricted clusters, `concurrent` and per-runner limits, `[runners.cache]` distributed cache. Sheds its troubleshooting checklist (`:119-158`) to the debugging file |
| `references/container-build-strategies.md` | keep, corrected | Build-path selection with the shell-runner-plus-host-daemon case added, the reason GitLab's own documented order differs from this skill's, and Kaniko stated as unmaintained rather than merely not recommended. Routes image *content* to `alaa-docker-production` |
| `references/security-and-hardening.md` | keep, extended | Unchanged in substance; add the persistent-workspace credential rule (no token in `.git/config`, no credential surviving a job on a shell runner), tighten pull-policy wording, and route threat classification and rotation to `alaa-security-review` |
| `references/validation-and-debugging.md` | keep, restructured | The five failure classes as symptom / diagnosis / smallest retry / escalation, absorbing the body's eight-item list and the runner file's checklist. States exactly what each bundled script can and cannot see, and states that `--fail-on-warnings` turns the checker into a gate whose thresholds are the calling skill's to set |
| `references/feature-version-notes.md` | keep, rewritten | Cadence first (monthly minors on the third Thursday, majors in May, security backports to current plus two), then only immovable facts: GA milestones, persistent experimental status, hard numeric limits, and each deprecation paired with its replacement. **No baseline version number in prose** — the cadence lets an agent compute it |

#### Content moves

1. `SKILL.md:57-114` → the five rule blocks collapse to a pointer plus at most two sentences each. Every one of the twenty instructions in §5 is deleted from the body; its reference copy is authoritative. Where the body's wording is sharper (`:64` "Do not abstract a one-off job", `:76` "Never hardcode secrets in YAML"), move the body's sentence into the reference and delete the reference's weaker version.
2. `SKILL.md:116-131` → the eight-layer list is deleted. `references/validation-and-debugging.md:48-121` becomes the single triage taxonomy, restructured by failure class. The body keeps one sentence: classify the failure layer before editing anything.
3. `references/pipeline-authoring.md:62-91` (DAG, `dependencies` versus `needs`, resource serialisation) → `references/job-graph-and-scheduling.md`.
4. `references/pipeline-authoring.md:20` ("Use pinned images, explicit caches, and `artifacts:expire_in`") → deleted and replaced by the whole of `references/cache-artifacts-and-pinning.md`.
5. `references/pipeline-authoring.md:102-107` (the `retry:when:` YAML fragment) → `references/job-graph-and-scheduling.md`, and promoted from an incidental example to a stated rule with its reason: a bare integer `retry:` retries every failure class, including an assertion failure, which converts a defect into a flake.
6. `references/runner-shell-and-kubernetes.md:119-158` → `references/validation-and-debugging.md`, merged into the executor-startup class.
7. `references/SOURCES.md` → `references/00-source-map.md`, content preserved, removed from the topic router and referenced from a maintenance line instead.
8. `SKILL.md:144-156` (template list) → keep in the body, since it is a manifest and not an instruction, but correct it to the six files that exist.

#### Files nominated for retirement

**None outright.** The skill's content is thin, not wrong; deleting any reference would lose rules. Two moves only, and both are renames rather than retirements: `references/SOURCES.md` → `references/00-source-map.md`, and the inline router at `SKILL.md:30-37` → `references/00-topic-map.md`. Nothing goes to `_to_delete/`.

#### Asset corrections

- **Create `assets/templates/app-basic.gitlab-ci.yml`** or delete the bullet at `SKILL.md:148`. Creating it is the better answer: a minimal correct pipeline — `workflow:rules`, `default:` with a pinned image, one lockfile-derived cache key with an explicit `policy:`, `artifacts:expire_in`, a job `timeout:`, a narrowed `retry:when:` — is the most-reached-for artefact in the skill and currently the only one missing.
- Repin `docker:24.0.5-cli` / `docker:24.0.5-dind` (`k8s-dind-tls.gitlab-ci.yml:11`, `:20`) to a supported Docker Engine line, and move the top-level `services:` block (`:19`) under `default:`.
- Repin every `alpine:3.20` (`component-with-inputs.gitlab-ci.yml:6`, `values-k8s-runner.yaml:21`, and the `alpine:*` allowlist entry at `:27`) to a supported Alpine branch, and narrow the `allowed_images` wildcards so the allowlist constrains something.
- Repin `moby/buildkit:rootless` (`k8s-buildkit-rootless.gitlab-ci.yml:11`) to a versioned rootless tag or digest, and restore the line continuations in the `buildctl-daemonless.sh` invocation at `:38`.
- Give `shell-runner-trusted-host.gitlab-ci.yml` a lockfile-derived cache key with an explicit `policy:` (`:14-17`), a job `timeout:`, and a comment stating that `$CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH` equality is not protected-ref equality (`:35`).
- Explain or remove `MaxUploadedArchiveSize = 0` (`config-shell-runner.toml:15`) and tie `concurrent` (`:1`) to a stated host-capacity rule.
- Add a `[runners.cache]` block with a `Type` to `values-k8s-runner.yaml`, so the template teaches that a Kubernetes cache needs distributed storage.

#### Executable checks the skill should ship and does not

**In `scripts/validate_gitlab_ci.py`:**

1. Register a passthrough constructor for `!reference` (and any unknown `!` tag) so a valid GitLab file parses instead of being reported as a syntax error and abandoned.
2. When the file contains `include:`, downgrade `extends-missing`, `needs-missing`, `dependencies-missing` and `stage-missing` from `error` to `note` and emit one `unresolved-include` note explaining that these names may be defined in the included file. Optionally accept `--merged` for a pre-resolved config.
3. Apply the image, cache, `set -x`, secret, `retry`, `interruptible` and `timeout` checks to `default:` as well as to each job.
4. `deprecated-global-keyword`: warn on top-level `image`, `services`, `cache`, `before_script`, `after_script`, naming `default:` as the replacement.
5. `retry-bare-count`: warn when `retry:` is an integer, or `retry:max` is present with no `when:` list. Message names the two infrastructure classes.
6. `interruptible-on-mutating-job`: warn when a job that carries `resource_group`, or whose stage or name matches migrate/deploy/release/publish, resolves to `interruptible: true` — including by inheritance from `default:` or a merge key.
7. `resource-group-saturation`: warn when every job in the file shares one `resource_group` value while `needs:` is in use, stating that the DAG cannot parallelise.
8. `dag-orphan`: note a job that no other job `needs:` while `needs:` is in use elsewhere in the file.
9. `advisory-not-gate`: warn on `allow_failure: true`, and on any script line ending `|| true`, `; true`, `|| exit 0` or `|| :`. The message reports the fact and names the owning skill as the one who decides whether it should block — this is detection, not a gate decision.
10. `job-timeout-missing`: note a job with no `timeout:` that carries `resource_group` or `environment:`.
11. `variables-shell-default`: **error** on a `variables:` value matching `\$\{[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*[:!-]`, because GitLab does not implement shell-style defaults and the expansion silently yields empty. This catches `platform-app-php-service.yml:102` exactly.
12. `cache-key-files-limit`: error when `cache:key:files` has more than two entries. `fallback-keys-limit`: error above five.
13. `cache-key-not-lockfile-derived`: note a `cache:` with `paths` whose `key` is not `files:`-derived — the provider-side detection for the frontend skill's cache-key predicates.
14. `cache-policy-unset`: note a cache with no `policy:` on a job that only reads it.
15. Fix `image-unpinned`: strip a registry host and optional port before testing for a tag, recognise `@sha256:` digests, and apply the check to `services:` entries and to `default:image`.
16. Extend `image-latest` to any tag with no version component — `latest`, `rootless`, `stable`, `edge`, `main`, `master` — so the skill's own BuildKit template would fail its own rule.
17. `script-credential-in-url`: warn on `git remote set-url` or any URL literal containing `://…:…@` in a script body.
18. `--self-test`: run an embedded fixture corpus of known-good and known-bad snippets and report pass/fail per rule.
19. Exit codes: reserve **3** for "could not run" (file missing, unreadable, dependency import failure), keep 1 for errors found and 2 for warnings under `--fail-on-warnings`. Wrap `import yaml` in a try/except that prints a named remediation and exits 3.

**In `scripts/validate_runner_config.py`:**

20. Fix `detect_kind` (`:175-189`): require a positive Helm-values signal (`gitlabUrl`, `runnerToken`, or `runners:` containing `config`) before taking the values path; otherwise exit 3 with `wrong-tool`, naming `validate_gitlab_ci.py`. It must never report `gitlab-url-missing` on a pipeline file.
21. Use the `line_map` computed at `:129`, so every issue carries a line number. Today none do.
22. Delete or re-target `kube-rofs-paths` (`:79-88`) — `read_only_root_filesystem` is not a documented `[runners.kubernetes]` key.
23. Add: `concurrent` unset or zero; a Kubernetes executor with no `[runners.cache]` `Type`, warning that a cache will not survive between pods; an unpinned `image =` or `helper_image` in `[runners.kubernetes]`; a `pull_policy` that contradicts `allowed_pull_policies`; and an `allowed_images` entry that wildcards an entire registry namespace.
24. Guard `import tomllib` with a Python-version message and a `tomli` fallback, exiting 3 rather than tracebacking on Python 3.10.
25. `--self-test`, and the same 3/2/1/0 exit scheme.

**One documentation obligation that goes with all of the above.** `references/validation-and-debugging.md` must state, for each script, which rules are gate-eligible and which are advisory, and must state that the choice of `--fail-on-warnings` belongs to the caller — because a script that can fail a pipeline while the skill that ships it "decides no gate" is the precise shape RFC 0001 §2 was written to prevent.

---

### Out-of-batch findings

None. `alaa-frontend-devops` was read as required for the boundary. `SKILL.md:16` and `references/90-companion-boundary.md:15` state its half of D8 correctly and name this skill with both trigger forms; `references/20-ci-gates-and-predicates.md:5` and `:29` correctly withhold provider syntax and hand cache-key syntax, the job graph, `rules:`/`needs:`, retention and the runner image reference to this skill. The asymmetry — that this skill has no text on any of the four — is a defect in `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`, recorded in §3 and §4, not in `alaa-frontend-devops`. No edit to it is proposed.

---

### Is a new skill required?

**No.** Every gap found in this lane has an existing owner that can take it without violating its own boundary.

The four largest gaps — cache-key expression, the job graph and its scheduling primitives, artifact retention, and the runner image reference — are the four subjects D8 already routes *into* `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`. They are not homeless; they are unwritten inside their own owner, and §10 places them in two new references under that owner. The gate-shaped gaps are already assigned elsewhere and explicitly: RFC 0001:265 names `alaa-cicd-laravel-postgres` as the carrier of the PHP gate register, and `alaa-frontend-devops/SKILL.md:16` claims the frontend one. The doctrine gaps behind the five FAIL verdicts have named owners in this programme's own list — `alaa-reliability-sla` for why a timeout or retry exists, `alaa-observability-soc` for what a pipeline event must emit, `alaa-security-review` for threat classification. What is missing in each case is a call site, and a call site is a sentence, not a skill.

The one subject that briefly looks homeless is the shell-runner-with-host-Docker-daemon build path that `service-ci-kit` actually uses and that this skill's build ladder omits. It is not homeless either: how a build job is expressed on a runner is this skill's half, and what goes into the image is `alaa-docker-production`'s. A new skill there would split a single build decision across three owners instead of two, which is strictly worse than adding one row to `references/container-build-strategies.md`.

Proposing a new skill here would create a boundary this programme would then have to defend. The correct outcome is that `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` writes the text it already owns and names the owners it already has.

# Batch 7, Phase 1 — Appendices C and D

Lane: `alaa-k8s-helm` (Appendix C) and `caas-arvan-kuber` (Appendix D), analysed as a pair because
they overlap and the seam between them is this lane's to settle. Read-only pass. All line citations
are against the files as staged at `/mnt/user-data/uploads/skills/skills/sohrab/` on 2026-07-29.

---

## Appendix C — `alaa-k8s-helm`

### 1. What this skill is today

A routing-first, five-lane entrypoint for Helm charts, Kubernetes manifests, OpenShift workloads and
day-2 troubleshooting. It declares its purpose as replacing "separate generator, validator, and
debugger skills while keeping context usage low through routing-first instructions" (`SKILL.md:8`).

Shape on disk: `SKILL.md` at 9207 bytes total, of which the always-loaded body after frontmatter is
**8604 bytes / 168 lines**. Nine reference files (43 KB), seven scripts (15 KB), four assets (4.8 KB),
one `agents/openai.yaml`. Nine references means the topic-map convention applies, and
`references/00-topic-map.md` exists — correct.

The body is organised as: a five-step "Start here" (`SKILL.md:10-23`), a "When NOT to use" block
(`SKILL.md:25-29`), seven default operating rules (`SKILL.md:31-39`), five task lanes with per-lane
reading lists (`SKILL.md:41-127`), four access surfaces (`SKILL.md:128-137`), seven guardrails
(`SKILL.md:139-147`), a subagent strategy (`SKILL.md:149-160`) and four deliverable rules
(`SKILL.md:162-167`).

The strongest thing in the skill is the **access-surface model** — cluster-admin, namespace admin,
developer, container-only (`SKILL.md:128-137`, expanded at
`references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md:68-120`). It is a genuinely useful discriminator that
most Kubernetes guidance omits, it is stated as four named cases rather than as a caution, and it is
backed by an observable command (`kubectl auth can-i`, `SKILL.md:137`). The second strongest is the
failure-layer triage model at `references/runtime-debug-playbooks.md:14-25`, which names seven layers
and tells the agent to identify the layer before proposing a fix.

The weakest thing is that almost none of it is enforced. See §9.

### 2. Ten-criteria verdict

| # | Criterion | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Correctness and testability | **FAIL** | The skill defines validation as a seven-stage pipeline (`references/validation-workflows.md:19-25`) and ships two scripts that assert anything at all (`scripts/check_tools.sh`, `scripts/validate_chart_structure.sh`). Neither tests rendered output against the skill's own rules. `references/authoring-workflows.md:137` says "use chart tests for smoke checks" and the shipped `assets/.helmignore:18-19` strips `templates/tests/` from the packaged chart (see §9). Nothing in the skill would fail against a plausibly broken chart: a Deployment with no probes, no resources, `privileged: true` and a mutated selector passes every shipped check. |
| 2 | Failure behaviour | **FAIL** | The skill covers *diagnosing* failure well (`references/runtime-debug-playbooks.md:51-247`) and *designing for* failure barely. Rollout deadlock is one sentence (`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:181`: "A 'valid' rollout can still deadlock if these values conflict") with no rule that computes a safe `maxUnavailable`/`maxSurge` against replica count and PDB. `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` appears once as a checklist item (`references/authoring-workflows.md:112`) with no relationship stated to `preStop`, to the app's own drain time, or to the readiness-removal race that causes 502s on every rolling update. `PodDisruptionBudget` guidance (`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:154-171`) names good and poor candidates but sets no value. No mention of `alaa-reliability-sla` anywhere in the skill, so this is silence, not delegation. |
| 3 | Security | **FAIL** | The security defaults exist and are correct as far as they go (`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:210-229`: `runAsNonRoot`, `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, drop all capabilities, `readOnlyRootFilesystem`, `seccompProfile: RuntimeDefault`). But: no rule about Secret handling at all beyond "ConfigMaps and Secrets" appearing in an access list (`references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md:92`); no rule that a rendered manifest must not be written to a shared path; no `automountServiceAccountToken: false` default; no tenant-isolation rule; no NetworkPolicy default-deny posture (NetworkPolicy is described as an allow-list mechanism at `references/networking-observability-and-tuning.md:85-89` but never prescribed). `alaa-security-review` is never named. |
| 4 | Observability | **FAIL** | `references/networking-observability-and-tuning.md:102-141` lists signal *categories* — restart count, `OOMKilled`, CPU throttling, latency percentiles, CoreDNS latency. There is no contract: no metric names, no label cardinality rule, no required annotation for scrape config, no statement of which of these are mandatory versus nice-to-have. `alaa-observability-soc` and `alaa-services-contract` — both named in the programme's doctrine-owner list as owning exactly this — are not referenced anywhere in the skill. Silence, not delegation. |
| 5 | Concurrency and load | **FAIL** | HPA guidance (`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:253-262`) is three bullets and one real rule ("Do not bolt on an HPA without requests"). Nothing on connection pool sizing against replica count, nothing on `maxSurge` amplifying downstream connection counts during rollout, nothing on backpressure or load shedding, nothing on the `readinessProbe`-versus-in-flight-request interaction. `references/networking-observability-and-tuning.md:147-155` lists "connection pool sizes in app config" and "worker concurrency" as *things that are tunable with namespace access* and then says nothing about how to choose them. |
| 6 | Clean code, SOLID, design patterns | **SATISFIED** | The chart-design rules are the domain equivalent and they are real and testable: `references/authoring-workflows.md:83-90` (values reflect operational decisions, not raw API fields; keep identity fields unconfigurable; nested groups; `values.schema.json` early), `:181-202` (parameterise only what varies; keep selectors, controller type, label keys and helper-derived names fixed), and `:129-139` (upgrade safety over template cleverness). |
| 7 | Algorithm and data-structure choice | **NOT-OWNED: none — FAIL** | Not applicable in the usual sense, but the domain analogue *is* applicable and absent: no complexity or capacity budget anywhere. `references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:236-241` justifies requests and limits by "scheduling accuracy" and "cluster fairness" and never says how to derive a number. No skill is named as owning sizing. |
| 8 | Configurability | **SATISFIED** | `references/authoring-workflows.md:81-90` and `:181-202` state what must be configurable and what must not, `assets/values-schema-template.json` provides boundary validation, and `references/authoring-workflows.md:86` requires the schema "early so bad input fails fast". The schema itself is too permissive (§9) but the rule is stated and a checker could test it. |
| 9 | Speed of development and debuggability | **SATISFIED** | This is the skill's best axis. `references/runtime-debug-playbooks.md:28-47` gives a cheapest-first evidence order; `:51-247` gives eight symptom-keyed playbooks each with the exact commands; `references/networking-observability-and-tuning.md:36-61` gives a six-step service-path trace. An agent can move fast from a symptom to a command. |
| 10 | Documentation | **FAIL** | `SKILL.md:162-167` requires the agent to return YAML plus validation commands, a three-way split of validation findings, and a root cause with evidence. It requires no artifact that outlives the session: no README, no runbook, no record of what shipped or how it fails. Contrast `caas-arvan-kuber`, which ships both templates (§Appendix D). |

Tally: 3 SATISFIED, 7 FAIL, 0 legitimate NOT-OWNED.

The seven FAILs cluster on one cause. This skill routes to exactly two other skills in its whole body
— `/alaa-minio-object-storage` (`SKILL.md:147`) and `/alaa-prompting-guide` (`SKILL.md:153`). Every
other cross-cutting concern is either restated shallowly or omitted. Under the brief's rule that
silence is `FAIL` and only a named call site earns `NOT-OWNED`, most of these become recoverable in
Phase 2 by adding call sites rather than by adding content.

### 3. Defect classes actually found

**1. Stale model pins — NOT FOUND.** The batch carry-over (`UPGRADE-CARRYOVER.md:188`) expects a
stale hardcoded model name in this skill. There is none. `SKILL.md:153` already carries the corrected
form: "Take every model and reasoning-effort choice from `/alaa-prompting-guide`
(`$alaa-prompting-guide`) `references/50-effort-and-thinking.md`, and name no model here, because a
model name written into a skill goes stale silently and is copied forward because it looks
authoritative." A grep for `gpt-`, `claude-`, `opus`, `sonnet`, `haiku`, `model:`, `reasoning_effort`,
`effort:`, `thinking` and `temperature` across every file in the directory, including
`agents/openai.yaml` and all four assets, returns only that line. `agents/openai.yaml` carries four
keys and none of them is a model or effort key. **The carry-over expectation is no longer true and
should be struck from the carry-over.**

**2. Wrong trigger syntax — PASS.** The two cross-skill call sites at `SKILL.md:147` and `SKILL.md:153`
both give the dual form `/name` (`$name`). `agents/openai.yaml:4` uses the bare `$` form, which is
correct for a Codex-only file.

**3. Duplication between body and references — FOUND, severe.** See §5.

**4. Project-specific content in an always-loaded body — FOUND.** `SKILL.md:112` ("Arvan Cloud
Container or similar managed namespace/container platforms") puts a named vendor into the
always-loaded body of a general Kubernetes skill, and the frontmatter description does the same
(`SKILL.md:3`, "managed namespace platforms such as Arvan Cloud Container"). This is the seam defect;
see the Shared closing.

**5. Long numbered procedures nobody reads in order — PARTIAL PASS.**
`references/runtime-debug-playbooks.md` is already restructured by failure class — each playbook is
symptom-keyed (`:51`, `:76`, `:98`, `:122`, `:141`, `:163`, `:191`, `:211`, `:230`) with checks, causes
and fixes. That is the target shape. `references/validation-workflows.md:19-25`, by contrast, is a
seven-stage numbered pipeline that then repeats itself as "Stage 1..Stage 5" for raw manifests
(`:43-100`) and "Stage 1..Stage 7" for charts (`:104-176`) — two parallel numbered ladders that an
agent must read in order to find the one stage it needs.

**6. Description that only says when to use — PASS.** `SKILL.md:3` carries a "Do not use it for"
clause. 561 characters, no angle brackets, under the 900 limit.

**7. Fragile tooling — PARTIAL.** No `Path(__file__).parents[N]` anywhere;
`scripts/detect_crd_wrapper.sh:9` uses the correct `cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd`
idiom. No temp directory is created inside the repository — `scripts/detect_crd_wrapper.sh:18` uses
`mktemp -d -t`, which lands in the system temp dir. But the same wrapper is broken on native Windows
(§9).

**8. Shipped `__pycache__` — NOT FOUND.** No `__pycache__`, no `.pyc`, and no `.gitignore` in the
skill directory.

**11. Companion boundary — FOUND, the central defect.** The skill names what it does not own in only
one place (`SKILL.md:147`, object storage). It does not name the owner of reliability doctrine,
security review, observability contracts, testing strategy, CI expression, image authorship, or —
critically — Arvan. It claims Arvan instead.

**12. Two routers — FOUND.** `SKILL.md:41-127` ("Task lanes") and `references/00-topic-map.md:9-69`
("Task routing") are two routers for the same decision. They are not verbatim duplicates — the body
organises by five lanes, the topic map by nine task phrasings — but they encode the same mapping and
must be kept in sync by hand. `SKILL.md:12` already tells the agent to read the topic map first, so
the body's copy is loaded on every invocation and then superseded.

**13. Rules with no checker — FOUND, the largest gap.** See §9 for the count and what each shipped
script actually asserts.

### 4. Boundary map

**What the skill says it does not own, at a call site, today:**

- Object storage. `SKILL.md:147`: "Take the bucket, the bucket policy, the lifecycle rules, and the
  credential posture behind an object-storage volume or an S3 secret from
  `/alaa-minio-object-storage` (`$alaa-minio-object-storage`), and keep only the Kubernetes
  expression of that decision here, because a Secret, a PVC, or a CSI volume carries whatever storage
  policy it is handed and enforces none of it." This is a model call site: it names the owner, gives
  both trigger forms, states what stays here, and gives the reason.
- Model and effort selection. `SKILL.md:153`.

**What it claims that another skill owns:**

- **Arvan Cloud Container.** Claimed in the frontmatter description (`SKILL.md:3`), in a lane
  (`SKILL.md:112`), in the topic map twice (`references/00-topic-map.md:15`, `:61-64`) and in a
  dedicated 19-line section (`references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md:194-212`). `caas-arvan-kuber`
  is not named anywhere in this skill. See Shared closing.

**What it is silent on and must name in Phase 2:**

| Concern | Named owner to add |
|---|---|
| Timeouts, retries, backpressure, degradation, idempotency, why a probe threshold is what it is | `/alaa-reliability-sla` (`$alaa-reliability-sla`) |
| Threat classes, fail-closed doctrine, when a manifest change triggers a security review | `/alaa-security-review` (`$alaa-security-review`) |
| Requirement level and gate for telemetry | `/alaa-observability-soc` (`$alaa-observability-soc`) |
| Metric names, log fields, `OTEL_*` names, host-port table, canonical shared-infra names | `/alaa-services-contract` (`$alaa-services-contract`) |
| Chart tests versus integration suites, what a smoke test must assert | `/alaa-testing-strategy` (`$alaa-testing-strategy`) |
| How a validation gate is expressed on a GitLab runner | `/alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` (`$alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`) |
| How the image the chart references is built and hardened | `/alaa-docker-production` (`$alaa-docker-production`) |
| Arvan CaaS platform facts | `/caas-arvan-kuber` (`$caas-arvan-kuber`) |

**Reciprocal D8 half this skill must write.** D8 names `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`, `alaa-docker-production`
and `alaa-haproxy` as the three platform skills that must state their own half. `alaa-k8s-helm` is not
one of the three, but it sits directly against `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` and needs one sentence of its own:
this skill decides *which* checks must pass before a Kubernetes or Helm change is applied and what
each check inspects; `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` decides how those checks are placed on a runner and decides
none of them. That sentence has a consequence for D8 itself — see the Shared closing, question 3.

**Who names this skill from outside:** `alaa-makefile/SKILL.md:141` ("Pair with `$alaa-k8s-helm` when
Make targets are wrappers around Helm or Kubernetes operations"), `caas-arvan-kuber/SKILL.md:164,168,
170,171`, and `ansible-validator/references/playbook.md:1106,1108` — the last of which lists
`alaa-k8s-helm` twice in a three-item list, which is an out-of-batch defect (§Out-of-batch findings).

Each of these states one instruction in two or more places.

| Instruction | Copies |
|---|---|
| Task routing (which reference to open for which task) | `SKILL.md:41-127` and `references/00-topic-map.md:9-69` |
| The four access surfaces and what each unlocks | `SKILL.md:128-137` and `references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md:68-120` |
| OpenShift-hostile defaults to avoid (fixed UID, `privileged`, `hostPath`, `hostNetwork`, low ports) | `SKILL.md:143`, `references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md:146-153`, `references/authoring-workflows.md:214-221` — three copies |
| The version band (Helm 3.20+/4, Kubernetes 1.33–1.35, OpenShift 4.19–4.21) | `SKILL.md:35`, `references/version-awareness.md:36-39`, `references/authoring-workflows.md:57-59` — three copies, and all three are now wrong (§7) |
| Pre-change dry-run commands (`helm template`, `helm lint`, `--dry-run=server`, `kubectl diff`) | `SKILL.md:141` and `references/validation-workflows.md:76-98`, `:136-158` |
| Route usage and TLS termination modes (`edge`, `reencrypt`, `passthrough`) | `references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:126-136`, `references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md:156-168`, `references/runtime-debug-playbooks.md:185-189` |
| Service / Ingress / Route / PDB / PVC recommendations | `references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:66-136` and `:264-295` — twice **inside the same file**; the "Practical recommendations by object" tail restates what the body already said |
| Controller selection rules (Deployment / StatefulSet / DaemonSet / Job / Pod) | `references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:15-64` and `references/authoring-workflows.md:94-101` |
| Arbitrary-UID and group-0 image rules | `references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md:144-154` and `references/authoring-workflows.md:214-221` |
| The freshness-recheck instruction | `SKILL.md:23`, `SKILL.md:33`, `references/SOURCES.md:7`, `references/version-awareness.md:70-78` — four copies |

The version band appearing three times is the expensive one: it is the claim most likely to go stale,
and Phase 2 must fix it in three files or leave two of them lying.

### 6. Wording-test failures

Could a competent agent follow this sentence exactly and still do the wrong thing?

1. **`SKILL.md:35`** — "**Prefer** stable APIs and portable patterns that work across Kubernetes
   1.33-1.35 and OpenShift 4.19-4.21 unless the user explicitly asks for newer or platform-specific
   behavior." Preference verb where a constraint was meant. An agent that emits a beta API has not
   violated a "prefer". Replacement: "Emit only API versions that `kubectl api-versions` reports on the
   target cluster. When the cluster is unknown, emit only versions that are GA in every currently
   supported Kubernetes minor, and state the floor in the output."

2. **`SKILL.md:142`** — "Keep selectors stable. Do not **silently** change immutable selectors or
   StatefulSet storage identity." The prohibition is conditioned on announcement, not on correctness.
   An agent that announces the change has complied and has still made the Deployment unpatchable. And
   there is no positive replacement. Replacement: "Never emit a change to `spec.selector` on an
   existing Deployment, StatefulSet, DaemonSet or Job, or to a StatefulSet's
   `volumeClaimTemplates[].metadata.name`. When the selector must change, emit a new resource under a
   new name plus the cutover and deletion order, and say that the old resource must be deleted before
   the new one takes its traffic."

3. **`SKILL.md:143`** — "Avoid OpenShift-hostile defaults such as fixed non-portable UIDs,
   `privileged: true`, `hostPath`, `hostNetwork`, or binding directly to ports below 1024 **unless the
   user explicitly controls the policy surface**." "Controls the policy surface" is an abstract noun
   standing in for an observable condition, and "such as" leaves the list open. Replacement: name the
   list closed, and make the exception observable — `oc auth can-i use scc/<name> -n <ns>` returns
   `yes` for an SCC that permits the specific capability requested.

4. **`SKILL.md:167`** — "When the platform is uncertain, state the assumption briefly and prefer the
   safer, more restrictive path." "Safer" and "more restrictive" are abstract nouns. Replacement:
   spell out the restrictive path — no cluster-scoped object, no Route, no `hostPath`, no privileged
   container, non-root with an unset `runAsUser`, ports above 1024.

5. **`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:221`** — "`readOnlyRootFilesystem: true` **when the
   app can support it**." Unobservable before deployment and no procedure is given. Replacement: set
   it true by default and mount `emptyDir` at every path the process writes; if the write paths are
   unknown, say so and give the command that finds them.

6. **`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:151`** — "avoid **aggressive** liveness probes that
   turn dependency outages into restart storms." Abstract adjective. Replacement: a liveness probe
   must not test a downstream dependency; its `failureThreshold × periodSeconds` must exceed the
   application's worst observed self-recovery time; slow starts use a `startupProbe`, not an inflated
   liveness threshold.

7. **`references/validation-workflows.md:73`** — "Use `-ignore-missing-schemas` **only when custom or
   platform resources are expected**. Missing schemas are not proof that a manifest is valid." Correct
   warning, no positive replacement. Replacement: when a schema is missing, fetch the CRD from the
   cluster (`kubectl get crd <name> -o json`) and pass its `openAPIV3Schema` to kubeconform via
   `-schema-location`; only when no cluster is reachable may the resource be listed as unvalidated in
   the report.

8. **`references/networking-observability-and-tuning.md:177`** — "do not 'tune' around a broken
   dependency, bad selector, or wrong probe." True and unactionable as written; it names no observable
   that tells the agent it is in that situation.

9. **`SKILL.md:39`** — "**Report-only when asked to audit**." The rule is correct and the trigger words
   are enumerated ("validate, lint, review, or audit"), which is good. But it collides with
   `SKILL.md:12`'s instruction to read the topic map first, which contains no report-only branch, and
   with `references/validation-workflows.md:27`, which restates the same rule. State it once.

With 4.2.0 compiled against 1.36, the current band is **1.36 through 1.33**.
