**One structural point about §7 for this skill.** Every claim above lives in a file the agent is told
to read "whenever compatibility or 'latest' behavior matters" (`SKILL.md:33`). The file's own escape
hatch (`references/version-awareness.md:70-78`, "Re-check official vendor docs instead of trusting
this snapshot") is correct but it is an instruction, not a check. Ten of these thirteen claims would
have been caught by a script that fetches four URLs and compares (§10).

### 8. Router audit

- **Convention:** 9 reference files → 9 or more → a separate `references/00-topic-map.md` is required.
  It exists (`references/00-topic-map.md`). Correct.
- **Is there a second router?** Yes. `SKILL.md:41-127` is a full parallel routing table, 87 lines,
  loaded on every invocation, immediately after `SKILL.md:12` tells the agent to read the topic map
  first. Not verbatim — the body organises by five lanes and the map by nine task phrasings — but the
  same decision is encoded twice and must be maintained twice. Two routers.
- **Is there a provenance ledger?** `references/SOURCES.md` is a freshness-trigger list plus a URL
  catalogue plus a conflict-resolution order (`:43-48`). Under the programme's rule that a source map
  is a legitimate ledger, keep it. It is not a router and does not compete with the topic map.
- **Script map:** `references/00-topic-map.md:82-88` lists six of the seven scripts. `scripts/detect_crd.py`
  is absent, correctly, because `detect_crd_wrapper.sh` is the entrypoint. Consistent.
- **Does the topic map route to everything?** All nine references appear in it except `SOURCES.md`
  itself and `00-topic-map.md`. `SOURCES.md` is routed only from `SKILL.md:23`. Acceptable, but the
  topic map should also carry it so that an agent that lands on the map never needs the body again.
- **Trigger conditions:** every entry in `references/00-topic-map.md:9-69` has a task-phrased trigger
  and a conditional "also read" clause. This is the right shape.

Verdict: the map is good; the body's copy of it should go.

### 9. Scripts and assets audit

Seven scripts. **Two of them assert anything.** The other five collect evidence and always report
success.

| Script | What it asserts | Windows | `--help` | Self-test | Exit codes distinguish "could not run" from "clean"? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `scripts/check_tools.sh` | Required tools for a lane are on PATH; `yamllint`+`kubeconform` for `yaml`, plus `helm` for `helm`, none for `debug` (`:25-40`). Exits 1 listing what is missing (`:88-91`). | Runs under Git Bash/WSL. Emits `✅`/`❌`/`⚠️` (`:43,50,64,75,83`) — mojibake outside a UTF-8 console. `required=()` for the `debug` lane expanded at `:55` under `set -u` is an unbound-variable error on bash ≤ 4.3, which is macOS's system bash, not Windows's Git Bash. | **No.** Usage prints only on an invalid lane, to stderr, exit 1 (`:8-9`). | **No.** | **No.** Exit 1 means both "you passed a bad lane" and "a required tool is missing". |
| `scripts/validate_chart_structure.sh` | `Chart.yaml`, `values.yaml`, `templates/` exist; `Chart.yaml` `apiVersion == v2`, `name` and `version` non-null, `type` is `application` or `library`; `values.schema.json` is parseable JSON (`:36-78`). Exits 1 on any error. | Runs under Git Bash. **CRLF-fragile:** `printf ' - %s` with a literal newline inside the format string (`:90-91` and `:95-96`) means a CRLF checkout puts `\r\n` in the format and leaves a carriage return on every bullet. This is exactly the shipped defect class the brief names. Emoji as above. `yq` and `jq` are optional (`:46`, `:74`) so the `Chart.yaml` field checks silently do not run when `yq` is absent — a clean exit that proved nothing. | **No.** | **No.** | **No.** Exit 1 covers bad usage, missing directory, and real findings alike. |
| `scripts/detect_crd_wrapper.sh` | Nothing. Delegates. | **Broken on native Windows.** `:24` sources `"$tmp_venv/bin/activate"`; CPython on Windows creates `Scripts/activate`. The fallback path fails wherever PyYAML is absent and Python is the Windows build. Also `pip install --quiet pyyaml` (`:25`) with no timeout, no `--disable-pip-version-check`, no proxy handling and no offline fallback — it hangs rather than fails on a restricted network. `mktemp -d -t` (`:18`) is correct on GNU, deprecated on BSD. | **No.** Usage on wrong arity, exit 1 (`:5-6`). | **No.** | **No.** |
| `scripts/detect_crd.py` | Classifies each document as `kubernetes` / `openshift` / `custom` against a 26-entry allowlist (`:25-51`) and reports parse errors per document. | Pure stdlib + PyYAML, `pathlib`, no `parents[N]`. Clean. | **No** — no argparse; a bare usage string at `:149`. | **No.** | **No, and worse: it exits 0 when parsing fails.** `main()` (`:147-171`) prints `parseErrors` into the JSON and returns normally. A file whose every document is malformed produces `"parsedSuccessfully": 0` and exit code 0. The wrapper (`:15`) then exits 0 too. Any caller that branches on exit status treats a totally unparseable manifest as clean. **This is the sharpest script defect in the skill.** |
| `scripts/cluster_health.sh` | Nothing. 16 read-only sections, each swallowing failure into `(unavailable)` (`:13-19`). | Runs under Git Bash. `bash -lc` at `:34` and `:39` spawns a *login* shell to run a pipeline — sources the profile, slow and non-deterministic, and unnecessary. `$cli` unquoted at `:26-41` relies on word splitting. | **No.** | **No.** | Partially. Exit 1 if neither `kubectl` nor `oc` exists (`:9-10`) — that is "could not run". Otherwise always 0, including when all 16 sections printed `(unavailable)`. |
| `scripts/pod_diagnostics.py` | Nothing. Seven `kubectl`/`oc` sections. | Cross-platform; `shutil.which` (`:14`), `subprocess.run(..., timeout=45)` (`:22`) — the only script in the skill with a timeout. Good. | **Yes**, free from argparse (`:38`). | **No.** | **No.** Returns 0 unconditionally (`:58`); a command that times out prints `<command timed out>` (`:24`) into the report and the process still succeeds. |
| `scripts/network_debug.sh` | Nothing. Eight read-only sections. | Runs under Git Bash. | **No.** Usage on wrong arity, exit 1 (`:5-6`). | **No.** | **No.** Always 0 after the arity check. |

**Count: 7 scripts, 2 checkers, 0 with `--help` except the one that gets it free from argparse, 0
self-tests, 0 that distinguish "could not run" from "clean".**

**What has no checker at all.** Every security default at
`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:210-229`. Every guardrail at `SKILL.md:141-147`. The
selector-stability rule (`SKILL.md:142`). The requests-and-limits rule
(`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:235-241`). The "HPA needs requests" rule (`:262`). The
probe rules (`:141-152`). The OpenShift-safe authoring defaults
(`references/authoring-workflows.md:212-221`). The delivery checklist
(`references/authoring-workflows.md:223-234`). Under defect class 13, all of these are currently
preferences.

**Assets.**

- `assets/values-schema-template.json` — draft-07, 112 lines. **It cannot fail a bad chart.** Root is
  `"additionalProperties": true` (`:5`) with no root-level `required`, so a values file containing
  nothing at all validates. `required` appears exactly once, on `image.repository` (`:14`). `resources`
  (`:25-31`) declares `requests` and `limits` as bare objects with no required keys — the skill's own
  mandatory-resources rule is unenforceable through it. There is no `securityContext`, no `probes`, no
  `podSecurityContext` property, so a chart that ignores every security default in
  `references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:210-229` validates. `podDisruptionBudget.minAvailable`
  and `maxUnavailable` (`:100-101`) are empty schemas accepting any type including `null`, and nothing
  forbids setting both, which the API rejects. `autoscaling` (`:88-94`) does not constrain
  `maxReplicas >= minReplicas`. `replicaCount` allows 0 (`:9`) with no note that 0 plus an enabled HPA
  is a scale-to-zero configuration most clusters will not honour.
- `assets/.helmignore` — **`test/` and `tests/` at `:18-19` are unanchored, and Helm matches an
  unanchored directory pattern at any depth** (https://helm.sh/docs/chart_template_guide/helm_ignore_file/:
  anchoring requires a leading slash; `**` is not supported because the matcher is Go's
  `filepath.Match`). So this asset strips `templates/tests/` from every packaged chart, and
  `references/authoring-workflows.md:137` tells the agent to "use chart tests for smoke checks". **The
  shipped asset silently deletes the artifact the shipped instruction requires.** `docs/` (`:17`) and
  `coverage/` (`:20`) have the same shape. Fix: anchor to `/test/`, `/tests/`, `/docs/`, `/coverage/`.
- `assets/_helpers-template.tpl` — standard scaffold, correct, no defect. `CHARTNAME` placeholder is
  flagged in a comment (`:1`).
- `assets/.yamllint` — sensible. `line-length: 120` at warning level, 2-space indent,
  `document-start: disable`, empty values permitted. Correctly never pointed at Helm templates: the
  skill only runs it against raw YAML and *rendered* output
  (`references/validation-workflows.md:48`, `:148`).
- **No model or effort key in any asset.** Verified.

### 10. Rewrite brief for Phase 2

**Target always-loaded body size: ≤ 7,000 bytes (from 8,604).** The skill gains no new capability, so
it must not grow. The reduction comes from deleting the second router and the Arvan claim, not from
deleting rules.

**Body keeps, and only these:** the frontmatter (rewritten, below); "Start here" (`SKILL.md:10-23`)
reduced to lane-detection plus "read `references/00-topic-map.md`"; "When NOT to use"
(`:25-29`) plus the new Arvan exclusion; the seven default operating rules (`:31-39`) with the version
band replaced by a pointer; the four access surfaces as a four-line table (`:128-137`); the seven
guardrails (`:139-147`); the subagent strategy (`:149-160`); the deliverable rules (`:162-167`); and a
new eight-line companion-boundary block.

**Body loses:** `SKILL.md:41-127` in its entirety — the five lane bodies are the second router and
`references/00-topic-map.md:9-69` already carries them. Replace with four lines naming the five lanes
and pointing at the map. Saving ≈ 3.1 KB.

**Frontmatter description, rewritten.** Remove "or managed namespace platforms such as Arvan Cloud
Container" from `SKILL.md:3`. Add to the do-not-use clause: "Do not use it for ArvanCloud CaaS
platform constraints, the Arvan namespaced-only API surface, or Arvan alias-versus-canonical namespace
RBAC — that is `caas-arvan-kuber`." Keep under 900 characters and keep the zero-angle-bracket rule.

  (`references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md`), pointed at from `SKILL.md` and

**`references/version-awareness.md`, rewritten.** Stop shipping a dated snapshot. Ship (a) the *rules*
that do not rot — Kubernetes supports the three most recent minors; kubectl skew is ±1 minor; kubelet
may be 3 minors older and never newer; controller-manager and scheduler 1 older and never newer; Helm
is compatible with `n-3` of the Kubernetes version it was compiled against — (b) the check sequence,
and (c) a one-line "the numbers live in `scripts/check_versions.sh`, run it". Record the Helm 3 EOL
date of 2027-02-10 as a hard date because the dual-support promise ends there.

**Files nominated for retirement:** none wholesale. Two section-level moves to
`_to_delete/<date>-batch7/` only if Phase 2 decides not to merge them:
`references/kubernetes-resource-patterns.md:264-295` (self-duplicate) and
`references/openshift-and-managed-platforms.md:194-212` (moves to the other skill, so it is a move,
not a retirement).

**Executable checks the skill must ship and does not:**

1. `scripts/check_versions.sh` — fetch https://kubernetes.io/releases/,
   https://github.com/helm/helm/releases and the OpenShift docs index; compare the supported bands
   against the values written in `references/version-awareness.md`; exit 3 on drift, 0 on match, 2 on
   "could not reach the network". Must carry `--help`, `--self-test` (a fixture that forces a known
   drift), a per-request timeout, and `HTTPS_PROXY`/`NO_PROXY` handling — the pattern the programme
   already settled on in `alaa-quasar-app-vite-v3`. **This one check would have caught ten of the
   thirteen §7 findings.**
2. `scripts/check_rendered_manifests.sh <rendered.yaml>` — the missing enforcement layer. Assert, per
   container: `resources.requests` and `resources.limits` both present; `securityContext.runAsNonRoot:
   true`; `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`; `capabilities.drop` contains `ALL`;
   `seccompProfile.type: RuntimeDefault`; `readinessProbe` present on every container in a Deployment
   or StatefulSet. Assert, per document: no `hostNetwork`, `hostPID`, `hostIPC`, `hostPath`, no
   `privileged: true`, no container port below 1024. Assert, per Service: every `targetPort` resolves
   to a declared container port. Exit 0 clean, 3 findings, 2 could-not-run. Emit findings as
   `file:line kind/name rule` so a human and a pipeline read the same output.
3. `scripts/check_upgrade_safety.sh <old-rendered.yaml> <new-rendered.yaml>` — assert that no
   `spec.selector` changed on any Deployment, StatefulSet, DaemonSet or Job, and that no
   `volumeClaimTemplates[].metadata.name` changed on any StatefulSet. This is the only shipped
   enforcement of `SKILL.md:142` and it is cheap.
4. Add `--help` and `--self-test` to all seven existing scripts; standardise exit codes on **0 clean,
   2 could-not-run, 3 findings** across every script in the skill.
5. Fix `scripts/detect_crd.py:147-171` to exit 3 when `parseErrors` is non-empty. It currently reports
   total parse failure as success.
6. Fix `scripts/detect_crd_wrapper.sh:24` to select `Scripts/activate` on Windows, add a `pip` timeout,
   and fail with exit 2 rather than hanging when the network is unavailable.
7. Fix the CRLF-fragile `printf` at `scripts/validate_chart_structure.sh:90-91` and `:95-96` to use an
   escaped `\n` inside a single-line format string.
8. Anchor `assets/.helmignore:17-20` to the chart root.
9. Tighten `assets/values-schema-template.json`: root `required: ["image", "resources"]`;
   `resources.requests` and `resources.limits` each `required: ["cpu", "memory"]`; add
   `securityContext` and `probes` objects; make `podDisruptionBudget` a `oneOf` over `minAvailable`
   and `maxUnavailable`; constrain `autoscaling.maxReplicas` against `minReplicas`.

**Companion boundary block to add to the body** (eight lines, naming the eight owners in §4 with both
trigger forms), plus this skill's D8 sentence: `references/validation-workflows.md` is the Kubernetes
and Helm delivery gate register; `/alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` (`$alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`) owns how each gate is
placed on a runner and decides no gate; `/alaa-docker-production` (`$alaa-docker-production`) owns how
the image the chart references is built and decides no gate.

---

## Appendix D — `caas-arvan-kuber`

### 1. What this skill is today

The largest always-loaded body in the batch: `SKILL.md` is 16,078 bytes total, **15,797 bytes / 440
lines** after frontmatter — 1.8× `alaa-k8s-helm`'s body while carrying half the references. It
declares itself "the single source of truth for Arvan work in this repository" (`SKILL.md:13`) and
claims Kubernetes, Helm **and CI/CD** (`SKILL.md:3`, `:11`).

Shape: five markdown references plus one 1.5 MB vendored OpenAPI JSON, three scripts, three assets,
one `agents/openai.yaml` with `policy.allow_implicit_invocation: true` (`agents/openai.yaml:6-7`) —
the only skill in this lane that asks to be invoked implicitly.

The body runs: source precedence (`:16-34`), web-research rules (`:37-51`), when to use and not use
(`:53-66`), eight Arvan non-negotiables (`:68-108`), an OpenAPI capability baseline (`:110-142`), RBAC
identity facts (`:144-156`), companion-skill orchestration (`:158-211`), agent prompting practice
(`:213-247`), two prompt templates (`:249-291`), a four-phase execution loop (`:293-375`), Arvan
operational details (`:377-383`), GitLab Runner on Arvan (`:385-392`), CI/CD rules (`:394-404`), a
definition of done (`:406-416`) and a package-resource list (`:418-439`).

**The valuable core is small and excellent.** Three things in this skill could not have come from
anywhere but a real Arvan cluster:

1. **The alias-versus-canonical namespace identity model**
   (`references/arvan-rbac-namespace-facts.md:10-11, 20-28`): the operator-facing namespace is `vk`
   while RBAC evaluates a hash-prefixed canonical form such as `bcbf1234-vk`, RoleBinding subjects may
   be rewritten between the two by platform components (`:25`), and a Helm release can be visible in
   namespace listings while runner jobs still fail authorization (`:26`). This is field knowledge with
   a named failure signature, and nothing in Arvan's public documentation says it.
2. **The namespaced-only API surface** (`references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:20-22`): 134 of
   134 documented paths carry `/namespaces/{namespace}`; no cluster-scoped endpoint exists in the
   document. From that follows the whole "treat DaemonSet, NetworkPolicy, PDB, StorageClass,
   ClusterRole, CRD as unsupported" posture (`:82-95`).
3. **The `LoadBalancer` + `arvancloud.ir/domain` + `metallb.universe.tf/ip-allocated-from-pool`
   exposure pattern** (`SKILL.md:106`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:59-62`). Arvan's own
   dedicated-IP page says only that the feature "leverages the Kubernetes Load Balancer feature" and
   documents no annotations at all. This pattern exists nowhere in Arvan's docs; it is observed.

Around that core sits roughly three-quarters of a body that restates generic Kubernetes, generic Helm,
generic prompting practice and generic CI/CD. See the Shared closing, question 2, for the line-level
split.

### 2. Ten-criteria verdict

| # | Criterion | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Correctness and testability | **FAIL** | `scripts/render-helm.sh` runs `helm lint` and `helm template` and therefore does gate on render errors (`:66`, `:69`, under `set -e` at `:2`). Nothing else is asserted. The skill's own central claims — `requests == limits` on every container (`SKILL.md:71`), no unsupported API kinds in the rendered output (`:130-138`), no secret leakage (`:365`) — have no checker, and `scripts/verify-cluster.sh` **always exits 0** even when the required Arvan resources are missing (`:122-126`). The one script that could enforce the capability baseline reports and never fails. |
| 2 | Failure behaviour | **FAIL** | The only failure content is diagnostic: the incident playbook in `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:91-165` and the runner/CI reliability map at `references/arvan-constraints.md:90-96`. Nothing on timeouts, retries, or degradation. `--wait --timeout 10m` appears in both templates (`README.operator.md.template:46`, `RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:39`) as a magic number with no derivation and no relationship to the CI job timeout. `alaa-reliability-sla` is never named. |
| 3 | Security | **PARTIAL, and the shipped workflow contradicts the rule — FAIL** | The rule is strong and stated four times: secrets in `Secret` or a gitignored `values.secret.yaml`, never in `values.yaml`, `--set`, shell history, CI logs, templates or notes (`SKILL.md:76-82`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:17-23`, `assets/README.operator.md.template:68-71`). **And the skill's own mandatory render step writes decoded secrets to a file it never protects.** `scripts/render-helm.sh:60` appends `values.secret.yaml` last, `:69` writes the rendered manifests to `${OUT}` (default `/tmp/rendered.yaml`, `:22`), and there is no `chmod`, no `umask`, no trap-based cleanup. The rendered output contains every `Secret` object the chart produces. `assets/README.operator.md.template:34-35` then tells the operator to write it as `rendered.yaml` in the working directory — and the gitignore guidance at `assets/values.secret.yaml.example:6-9` lists `values.secret.yaml`, `*.secret.yaml` and `*.secrets.yaml` and **does not list `rendered.yaml`**. The script header's claim at `:16` — "This script never prints secret file contents" — is true of stdout and false of the artifact it exists to produce. |
| 4 | Observability | **FAIL** | Nothing. No metric, no log field, no trace, no alert. The word does not appear. `alaa-observability-soc` and `alaa-services-contract` are not named. |
| 5 | Concurrency and load | **FAIL** | HPA policy by workload type is stated (`SKILL.md:83-85`) and is genuinely Arvan-grounded — Arvan's own scaling page states horizontal scaling "is only applicable to stateless applications" and that persistent storage disables both manual and automatic scaling. But there is no rule about what happens under load: no pool sizing, no backpressure, no rollout-time connection amplification, no LimitRange interaction beyond "treat as discovery input" (`references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:104`). |
| 6 | Clean code, SOLID, design patterns | **FAIL** | The domain analogue is chart design, and the skill has none — it defers chart structure to `alaa-k8s-helm` (`SKILL.md:168-170`) without naming a single design rule of its own. The one chart-shape rule it does state, the create-or-reference-existing-secret dual mode (`SKILL.md:79-81`), is good and is stated four times (§5). |
| 7 | Algorithm and data-structure choice | **NOT-OWNED: alaa-k8s-helm** | Call site: `SKILL.md:168` — "Build or refine templates/manifests with `alaa-k8s-helm`." The delegation is real, though the trigger syntax is wrong (§3). Note that `alaa-k8s-helm` also FAILs this criterion, so the concern is delegated to a skill that does not discharge it — record it, do not double-count it here. |
| 8 | Configurability | **SATISFIED** | `references/arvan-constraints.md:81-88` is a named, closed list of portability toggles — `exposureMode=public-ip|ingress|internal`, `openshift.enabled`, `route.enabled` vs `ingress.enabled`, `hpa.enabled` defaulting false for stateful, `affinityPreset.cpuGeneration` off by default, `privateRegistry.create` vs `privateRegistry.existingSecretName`. Each has a safe default and each is checkable in a values file. `assets/values.secret.yaml.example` gives the boundary shape. This is the skill's best criterion. |
| 9 | Speed of development and debuggability | **SATISFIED** | `scripts/verify-cluster.sh` collects, in one read-only pass, exactly the evidence an Arvan RBAC incident needs: API versions filtered to the OpenAPI-aligned set (`:87`), required-versus-present namespaced resources (`:95-149`), quota and LimitRange (`:153`), can-i for the caller and optionally impersonating the runner SA (`:163-184`), and the RoleBinding subject table with kinds, namespaces and names in one custom-columns line (`:190`). That last line is the single most useful command in the skill — it puts the alias/canonical mismatch on screen. Both operator templates are copy-paste ready. |
| 10 | Documentation | **SATISFIED** | The only skill in this lane that ships operator documentation as a deliverable: `assets/README.operator.md.template` (what it is, prerequisites, preflight, install, rollback, uninstall, safety requirements, verify, debug) and `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template` (day-0 checklist, day-1 operations, day-2 maintenance, five incident playbooks). `SKILL.md:373-375` makes them mandatory when scope is production or stateful. The templates carry defects (§9) but the criterion is met: the skill requires an artifact that says what shipped, how it is operated, and how it fails. |

Tally: 2 SATISFIED, 7 FAIL, 1 legitimate NOT-OWNED.

### 3. Defect classes actually found

**1. Stale model pins — NOT FOUND.** As with `alaa-k8s-helm`, the carry-over
(`UPGRADE-CARRYOVER.md:188`) expects one here. There is none. `SKILL.md:217` already carries the
corrected pointer, in a form nearly identical to `alaa-k8s-helm/SKILL.md:153` plus a lane-description
sentence. A grep across every file including both operator templates, `values.secret.yaml.example` and
`agents/openai.yaml` finds no model name and no effort key. `SKILL.md:150` reads "Required reasoning
model:" — that is a *reasoning* model in the epistemics sense (five rules about how to reason about
RBAC identity), not an LLM name. **Record the carry-over as satisfied for both skills in this lane.**

**2. Wrong trigger syntax — FOUND.** Five companion skills are named as bare backticked strings with
neither `/` nor `$`: `SKILL.md:164` `` `alaa-k8s-helm` ``, `:175` `` `alaa-bash-shell` ``, `:176`
`` `alaa-makefile` ``, `:185` `` `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` ``, `:197` `` `alaa-docker-production` ``, plus
`:186` `github-actions-generator` + `github-actions-validator` and `:187` `jenkinsfile-generator` +
`jenkinsfile-validator`, and three more bare uses of `alaa-k8s-helm` at `:168`, `:170`, `:171`. A bare
name is invisible as an invocation from both runtimes. Only two call sites are correct —
`SKILL.md:205-206` and `:210-211` (object storage) and `:217` (prompting guide) — which shows the
correct form was known and applied unevenly. `agents/openai.yaml:4` correctly uses `$`.

**3. Duplication between body and references — FOUND, the worst in the batch.** See §5. Essentially
every rule in this skill exists in three or four places.

**4. Project-specific content in an always-loaded body — FOUND, inverted.** The usual form of this
defect is a project leaking into a general skill. Here it is the opposite and it is worse: a
platform-specific skill's always-loaded body is 75% generic material that a general skill already
owns. `SKILL.md:213-291` (prompting practice and two prompt templates, 79 lines) and `SKILL.md:293-375`
(the four-phase execution loop, 83 lines) contain almost nothing Arvan-specific and are loaded on every
invocation.

**5. Long numbered procedures nobody reads in order — FOUND.** `SKILL.md:293-375` is a strictly
sequential "Execution loop (mandatory)" with Phase 0 through Phase 4. `SKILL.md:219-240` is a second
numbered sequence (Objective, Constraints, Discovery, Plan, Implement, Validate, Deliver) covering the
same ground with different numbers. `SKILL.md:68-108` is a third numbered list of eight
non-negotiables. `references/arvan-constraints.md` is a fourth. An agent hitting an RBAC denial must
read past three numbered ladders to reach `SKILL.md:144-156`, the part that would actually help.
Restructure by failure class: symptom → diagnosis → smallest retry → escalation. The material for that
already exists at `references/arvan-constraints.md:90-96` and
`assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:91-165`.

**6. Description that only says when to use — PASS.** `SKILL.md:3` carries "Do not use generic
Kubernetes defaults when live Arvan facts disagree." 234 characters parsed, no angle brackets.
It is however a weak do-not-use clause: it tells the agent how to behave once loaded rather than when
*not to load*. The three-bullet "When NOT to use" block at `SKILL.md:63-66` is much better and the
first bullet — "do not use this skill for generic Kubernetes guidance when the target platform is not
Arvan and its constraints are irrelevant" — should be pulled up into the description.

**7. Fragile tooling — FOUND, a different flavour.** No `Path(__file__).parents[N]` and no temp
directory inside the repository. But **every documented invocation hardcodes a Codex-only install
path**: `bash .codex/skills/caas-arvan-kuber/scripts/verify-cluster.sh` at `SKILL.md:300`,
`summarize-openapi.sh` at `:306`, `render-helm.sh` at `:354`,
`assets/README.operator.md.template:33-34`, `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:30` and `:78` — six
sites. A Claude Code installation does not have `.codex/skills/`. Every one of these fails there. And
`SKILL.md:306` compounds it by passing `references/arvan-caas-openAPI-1.25.json` as a relative path
resolved from the caller's CWD, which only works if CWD happens to be the skill root — while
`scripts/summarize-openapi.sh:12` already computes the correct default from `BASH_SOURCE`, so the
argument is both wrong and unnecessary.

**8. Shipped `__pycache__` — NOT FOUND.** None, and no `.gitignore` in the directory.

**A ninth, unlisted defect: three broken path references to the vendored spec.** The file on disk (per
`SKILL.md:19`, `:112`, `:423`, `scripts/summarize-openapi.sh:12`, both operator templates) is
`arvan-caas-openAPI-1.25.json`. Three places spell it with a doubled *n*:
`references/arvan-constraints.md:39` (`./arvann-caas-openAPI-1.25.json`),
`references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:5` (same) and `scripts/summarize-openapi.sh:9` (the
documented example, which is additionally wrong about the directory — from `scripts/` the path is
`../references/`, not `../`). The two references that a reader would follow to find the spec both
point at a filename that does not exist.

**11. Companion boundary — PARTIAL.** The skill has a whole section for it (`SKILL.md:158-211`) and it
is the most generous boundary statement in the lane. But it is written as *orchestration* — "use these
skills together", "`caas-arvan-kuber` sets policy; companion skills perform domain-specialized
generation/validation" (`:160`) — rather than as ownership. It never says what this skill does **not**
own. And its claim to set policy for CI/CD (`:185-193`, `:394-404`) collides with D8. See the Shared
closing, question 3.

**12. Two routers — FOUND, three routers.** With six reference files (five markdown plus the JSON) the
convention correctly says list them in `SKILL.md` with a trigger condition each, and correctly says no
`00-topic-map.md` — and there is none. But the same six files are listed three times, in three
different orders, for three different stated purposes: `SKILL.md:16-24` as a precedence order,
`SKILL.md:418-434` as a package-resource list, and `references/SOURCES.md:11-14` as first-check
sources. None of the three gives a trigger condition per file, which is what the convention actually
requires.

**13. Rules with no checker — FOUND, and it is this skill's defining gap.** See §9.

### 4. Boundary map

**What it says about others, at a call site:**

- `SKILL.md:164-171` — `alaa-k8s-helm` for building, refining and validating templates and manifests,
  in a four-step usage pattern. Real delegation; wrong trigger syntax.
- `SKILL.md:175-181` — `alaa-bash-shell` and `alaa-makefile` for helper scripts, with a constraint
  attached ("Ensure scripts remain non-mutating by default for discovery stages"). Wrong syntax.
- `SKILL.md:185-193` — `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`, `github-actions-*`, `jenkinsfile-*` for pipelines — but
  the usage pattern under it (`:191-193`) *decides the gates* rather than deferring them. See question 3.
- `SKILL.md:197-201` — `alaa-docker-production` for image hardening when Arvan failures are rooted in
  image or runtime behaviour. Wrong syntax, correct division.
- `SKILL.md:205-211` — the two object-storage skills, with correct dual trigger syntax and a clean
  statement of what stays here ("this skill keeps only the Kubernetes and Arvan expression of that
  decision"). This is the model the other five call sites should copy.
- `SKILL.md:217` — `/alaa-prompting-guide` for model and effort. Correct.

**What names it from outside:** `alaa-docker-production/SKILL.md:56`, `alaa-makefile/SKILL.md:141`,
`alaa-haproxy/SKILL.md:88`. Three sibling skills route to `caas-arvan-kuber` by name. This is the
strongest single argument against absorbing it (Shared closing, question 4).

**What it is silent on:** reliability doctrine, security review, observability, testing strategy, the
services contract. All FAIL, none delegated.

**Reciprocal D8 half:** `caas-arvan-kuber` is not one of D8's three named platform skills, but it
writes runner configuration (`SKILL.md:385-392`) and decides deployment gates (`:394-404`), which is
both halves of a boundary it was not given. Its half must be written as: this skill owns the Arvan
platform facts that change a manifest or a deployment decision, and owns no runner configuration and
no gate placement.

### 5. Duplication

This is the most duplicated skill in the lane. Each row is one instruction stated in three or more
places.

| Instruction | Copies |
|---|---|
| Resources mandatory, `requests == limits`, decimal cores, memory 2× CPU, `ephemeral-storage` | `SKILL.md:70-74`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:10-15`, `assets/README.operator.md.template:64-66`, `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:16-19`, and again as an incident fix at `RUNBOOK…:154-165` — **five copies** |
| Secrets: `Secret` or gitignored `values.secret.yaml`, never `--set`, never in logs, dual create/reference mode | `SKILL.md:76-82`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:17-23`, `assets/README.operator.md.template:68-71` and `:82-85`, `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:21-25` — **five copies** |
| HPA only for stateless; stateful gets manual knobs plus a runbook | `SKILL.md:83-85`, `SKILL.md:342-343`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:24-26`, `assets/README.operator.md.template:73-75`, `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:51-55` — **five copies** |
| Namespace-scoped RBAC first; no cluster-scoped objects; assume the namespace exists | `SKILL.md:87-90`, `SKILL.md:347`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:28-31`, `references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:99-100`, `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:137` — **five copies** |
| Config mount safety: dedicated subdirectories, do not shadow app files | `SKILL.md:92-94`, `SKILL.md:383`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:33-35`, `assets/README.operator.md.template:77-78` — **four copies** |
| The unsupported-API list (DaemonSet, NetworkPolicy, PDB, StorageClass, ClusterRole/Binding, CRD, Route/BuildConfig) | `SKILL.md:130-138`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:45-49`, `references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:82-95`, and as a probe list in `scripts/verify-cluster.sh:129-136` — **four copies, one of them executable** |
| OpenAPI snapshot metadata (`3.0.3`, `Arvan CaaS`, `1.25`, 134 paths, 299 operations, all namespaced) | `SKILL.md:113-119`, `references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:9-22` — **two copies, and both are hand-transcribed from a file `scripts/summarize-openapi.sh` already computes** |
| The exposure pattern (`LoadBalancer` + `arvancloud.ir/domain` + MetalLB pool; ingress mode separate; TLS at the edge) | `SKILL.md:103-108`, `SKILL.md:344`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:58-63` — **three copies** |
| The RBAC reasoning model (state both namespace forms; exact principal; Helm visibility is not proof; `--as` is not conclusive; state uncertainty) | `SKILL.md:150-156`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:69-79`, `references/arvan-rbac-namespace-facts.md:35-43`, `assets/README.operator.md.template:117-125` — **four copies** |
| The reference file list | `SKILL.md:19-24`, `SKILL.md:422-427`, `references/SOURCES.md:11-13`, `references/arvan-links.md:9-11` — **four copies** |
| The Arvan documentation URL set | `references/arvan-links.md:17-29` and `references/SOURCES.md:18-29` — near-identical lists |
| Validation gates (`helm lint`, `helm template`, optional kubeconform, optional `--dry-run=client`) | `SKILL.md:351-361`, `SKILL.md:399-403`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:96`, `assets/README.operator.md.template:32-36`, `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:27-32` and `:93-100` — **six copies** |
| Discovery command block (`api-resources`, `api-versions | grep`, `resourcequota,limitrange`, two `can-i`) | `SKILL.md:311-319`, `references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:111-118`, and executed by `scripts/verify-cluster.sh:87-171` — **three copies** |
| The render-helm invocation line | `SKILL.md:354`, `assets/README.operator.md.template:34`, `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:30` and `:78` — **four copies** |
| Install/upgrade/rollback command blocks | `assets/README.operator.md.template:41-53` and `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:37-41`, `:79-89` |

The practical consequence: the `--create-namespace` contradiction in §6 exists *because* the rule was
written in five places and one copy drifted. Deduplication here is not tidiness, it is the fix.

### 6. Wording-test failures

1. **`SKILL.md:73` / `references/arvan-constraints.md:14`** — "Memory must default to `2x` CPU and use
   integer `Mi` or whole `Gi` (for example `0.2 -> 400Mi`, `0.5 -> 1Gi`, `2 -> 4Gi`)." **The three
   worked examples use two incompatible unit conventions.** 2 × 0.2 cores is 400 MB decimal or 409.6
   MiB binary — the example picks decimal. 2 × 0.5 is 1000 Mi decimal but the example says `1Gi`,
   which is 1024 Mi — binary. An agent following this sentence exactly produces `400Mi` for one input
   and `1Gi` for another by two different rules, and has no rule at all for `0.3` or `1.5`. Replacement:
   state one function — memory in MiB = `ceil(cpu_cores × 2 × 1024)` rounded up to the nearest 64Mi,
   expressed as `Gi` when the result is a whole multiple of 1024Mi — and regenerate the examples from
   it.

2. **`SKILL.md:72` / `references/arvan-constraints.md:13`** — "CPU values **must** use decimal cores
   (for example `0.2`) instead of millicores (`200m`)." `0.2` and `200m` are the identical quantity to
   the Kubernetes API; a `resource.Quantity` parses both to the same value and serialises back to
   `200m`. As written this is a hard constraint on a formatting choice with no stated reason, no source
   in Arvan's documentation, and no failure it prevents. Either it is a real Arvan panel or admission
   behaviour, in which case the sentence must say which component rejects `200m` and what the error
   looks like, or it is a house style, in which case `must` is the wrong verb. **This is the one
   Arvan-specific rule in the skill I could not corroborate against any Arvan source, and it is stated
   in the strongest possible terms.**

3. **`SKILL.md:90` / `references/arvan-constraints.md:31`** — "do not add namespace creation or
   cluster-scoped namespace checks to Arvan CI flows" and "Do not rely on `kubectl create namespace`,
   `kubectl get namespace`, or `helm --create-namespace` in runner jobs unless live evidence proves the
   runner has that scope" (`SKILL.md:391`). **Contradicted by the skill's own shipped asset:**
   `assets/README.operator.md.template:42` writes `--create-namespace` into the canonical install
   command the operator is told to copy. A competent agent following the body writes one thing and
   handing over the template ships the other.

4. **`SKILL.md:19-24` versus `SKILL.md:32-34`** — the precedence list puts the 1.25 JSON at position 1
   and live discovery at position 2; four lines later, "Live facts beat local references. When live
   cluster discovery and local reference files disagree, prefer current platform facts from the target
   cluster." Two contradictory orderings on the same page. `references/SOURCES.md:42-46` gives a third
   ordering with live discovery first. Given the spec's age (§7), the live-first ordering is the correct
   one and the precedence list must be rewritten to match.

5. **`SKILL.md:105` / `references/arvan-constraints.md:58`** — "prefer `service.type=LoadBalancer` plus
   service annotations for the public domain and pool **when the cluster already uses that pattern**."
   "Already uses that pattern" is not observable through any command the skill provides. Replacement:
   give the check — `kubectl -n <ns> get svc -o jsonpath` over
   `.items[*].metadata.annotations` filtered for `arvancloud.ir/domain`; if any existing Service carries
   it, follow the pattern; if none does and no public IP is assigned in the panel, use ingress mode.

6. **`SKILL.md:154`** — "Do not treat `kubectl auth can-i --as=...` alone as conclusive, because
   impersonation rights may be missing for the caller." Correct, and the positive replacement is
   missing. Replacement: the conclusive check is a `SelfSubjectAccessReview` issued with the
   ServiceAccount's own token — `kubectl --token="$(kubectl -n <ns> create token <sa>)" -n <ns> auth
   can-i <verb> <resource>` — because that evaluates the real principal with no impersonation involved.
   This is the highest-value single sentence Phase 2 could add to this skill.

7. **`SKILL.md:365` / `:237`** — "Always verify: no secrets were printed" and "Lint/render/dry-run and
   inspect for secret leakage." Abstract instruction, no observable, no tool, and the workflow
   immediately violates it (§2 criterion 3). Replacement: a `scripts/check-rendered-secrets.sh` that
   greps the rendered file for `kind: Secret` and base64-decodes `data:` values against the values in
   `values.secret.yaml`, plus a hard rule that `${OUT}` is created with mode 600 and removed on exit.

8. **`SKILL.md:130`** — "Treat as unsupported unless discovery proves otherwise." Good shape. But
   `scripts/verify-cluster.sh:145-148` runs exactly this discovery and, when it finds the "unsupported"
   resources present, prints `Present but not in Arvan OpenAPI 1.25: …` and exits 0 with no guidance on
   what the agent should now do. The rule has a detector and no decision.

9. **`SKILL.md:100-101`** — "CPU generation scheduling is optional and value-driven. Affinity presets
   are opt-in, not forced by default." The node labels this depends on appear only in
   `references/arvan-constraints.md:57` ("for example `cloud-container-g2`/`g3` families") hedged with
   "may require". Neither the label key nor a way to discover it is given. An agent asked to pin a
   workload to a CPU generation cannot act on this.

### 7. Stale or unverifiable claims

**The headline finding: the 1.25 baseline is between eleven and twelve Kubernetes minors out of date,
and I can date it from the skill's own evidence rather than from the version string.**

The capability matrix lists `autoscaling/v2beta2/horizontalpodautoscalers` as a supported group and
resource (`references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:29`, `:72`), and `SKILL.md:125` repeats it
(`autoscaling/v1|v2|v2beta2`). **`autoscaling/v2beta2` was removed from Kubernetes in v1.26** — it is
in the 1.26 removals list. An API server that still serves `autoscaling/v2beta2` is at most Kubernetes
1.25. So the `1.25` in the spec's `info.version` is not merely a document version; the surface it
describes really is a 1.25-era API server. Kubernetes 1.25 went GA in August 2022 and left upstream
support in October 2023.

Today the supported Kubernetes minors are 1.36, 1.35 and 1.34 (https://kubernetes.io/releases/). The
baseline this skill pins is **eleven minors behind the current release and roughly two years and nine
months past its own upstream end of life.**

**What Arvan documents today.** I could find no Kubernetes version and no API version published by
ArvanCloud anywhere. The Cloud Container getting-started page carries none; the API-usage page
(https://docs.arvancloud.ir/en/developer-tools/api/api-usage) documents no CaaS versioning scheme, no
base URLs and no OpenAPI reference. **Arvan does not publish the number this skill pins.** That has two
consequences: the skill cannot be checked against a vendor statement, and the skill's precedence list
(`SKILL.md:19-24`) elevates an unpublishable, unverifiable, four-year-old local artifact above live
discovery. That ordering must invert.

**What an agent following the 1.25 matrix against a current Arvan cluster would get wrong.** Assuming
Arvan has moved at all since the spec was captured:

| Following the matrix | What actually happens on a current cluster |
|---|---|
| Emits `autoscaling/v2beta2` HPA as a documented fallback (`SKILL.md:98`, matrix `:107`) | Rejected — the group has not existed since 1.26. The "fallback only when live discovery requires" hedge saves it *only if* the agent runs discovery, and `SKILL.md:19` tells it the JSON outranks discovery. |
| Refuses to emit `NetworkPolicy` (`SKILL.md:133`) | On a modern managed namespace platform NetworkPolicy is the normal tenant-isolation primitive. Refusing it means the skill's answer to "how do I isolate this workload's traffic" is silence — and this is exactly why criterion 3 fails. |
| Refuses to emit `PodDisruptionBudget` (`SKILL.md:134`) | `policy/v1` PDB has been GA since 1.21 and is namespaced. If Arvan serves it, the skill has talked the agent out of the single control that protects a workload during node drain — on a platform whose whole premise is that the vendor drains nodes. |
| Refuses `DaemonSet` (`SKILL.md:132`) | Correct for a namespace-scoped tenant regardless of version — DaemonSets on a shared managed platform are a vendor concern. This one survives. |
| Refuses CRDs and treats operator CRs as unsupported (`SKILL.md:137`) | Plausible and probably still right for a namespaced tenant, but it is now a guess. |
| Trusts "134 paths / 299 operations / all namespaced" (`SKILL.md:116-119`) | Unverifiable. The spec is not staged, and nothing regenerates these numbers (§9). |
| Treats `limitranges` and `resourcequotas` as read-only (matrix `:64-65`, `:104`) | Correct behaviour for a tenant either way. Survives. |

**Net:** the version-sensitive parts of the matrix have decayed, the tenant-scope parts have not. That
distinction is the basis for the §10 rewrite: keep the *scope* conclusions, re-derive the *API version*
conclusions from live discovery every time.

**Other version-sensitive claims:**

| Claim | Where | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Helm v3 (for chart-based deployments)" as the stated prerequisite | `assets/README.operator.md.template:25` | https://helm.sh/blog/helm-v3-end-of-life/ | **STALE and internally contradictory.** Helm 4 is the current major (released November 2025, now at 4.2.x); Helm 3's last line is 3.21.0 and it reaches end of life **2027-02-10**, after which it receives no updates including Kubernetes client-library and security patches. And the same template uses a Helm-4-only flag — next row. |
| `helm upgrade … --rollback-on-failure` | `assets/README.operator.md.template:46`, `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template:39` | https://github.com/helm/helm/issues/31900 | **BROKEN under the prerequisite the same file states.** `--atomic` was renamed to `--rollback-on-failure` in Helm 4. Helm 3 does not know the flag and errors out. So the template tells the operator to install Helm 3 and then hands them a command that only runs on Helm 4. (Note the reverse trap for anyone standardising on `--atomic`: Helm 4 keeps the legacy binding on `helm upgrade` with a deprecation warning but **removed it from `helm install`**, so `helm install --atomic` now fails with `unknown flag`.) |
| `runnerRegistrationToken` as a supported secret field | `assets/values.secret.yaml.example:49` | https://docs.gitlab.com/ci/runners/new_creation_workflow/ | **DEPRECATED.** Registration tokens were superseded by runner authentication tokens (`glrt-` prefixed); from GitLab **17.0** administrators and group owners can disable the legacy workflow, after which registration returns `410 Gone - runner registration disallowed`. The current field is `runnerToken` / the `runner-token` secret key — which the same file already has at `:48`. Drop `:49` or comment it as legacy-only. |
| GitLab Runner chart "0.52 vs 0.53+ behavior" | `references/arvan-links.md:62-63` | — | **UNVERIFIED and now very old.** Two chart minors named with no date and no statement of what differs. Either state the behaviour and the version floor or delete the parenthetical. |
| "Last verification snapshot: **2026-02-15**" | `references/arvan-links.md:5` | — | **165 days old.** Older than `alaa-k8s-helm`'s snapshot and covering a platform whose docs are not versioned, so drift is undetectable by reading. |
| `requests == limits` required on Arvan | `SKILL.md:71`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:12` | https://docs.arvancloud.ir/en/cloud-container/create-app/manifest | **CONFIRMED by Arvan.** The manifest page states resource consumption must be specified per container and "the values of Limits and Requests must be the same". Genuinely Arvan-specific and genuinely current. |
| Horizontal scaling is stateless-only and restricted when persistent storage is enabled | `SKILL.md:83-85`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:56` | https://docs.arvancloud.ir/en/cloud-container/manage-app/scaling | **CONFIRMED by Arvan.** "If your application has Persistent Storage enabled, you cannot use manual or automatic scaling" and "this feature is only applicable to stateless applications". |
| Disk: container filesystem ephemeral, size increase-only, detach and delete disruptive | `references/arvan-constraints.md:64-67`, `SKILL.md:382` | https://docs.arvancloud.ir/en/cloud-container/disk/ | **CONFIRMED by Arvan.** Ephemeral by default; "you can only increase the disk size, and decreasing it is not possible"; detaching restarts the application and deleting is irreversible. |
| Public exposure uses a Kubernetes LoadBalancer | `SKILL.md:105`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:58` | https://docs.arvancloud.ir/en/cloud-container/manage-app/dedicated-ip | **CONFIRMED in outline.** Arvan states the public-IP feature "leverages the Kubernetes Load Balancer feature". |
| The `arvancloud.ir/domain` and `metallb.universe.tf/ip-allocated-from-pool` annotations | `SKILL.md:106`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:59-62` | same page | **NOT DOCUMENTED BY ARVAN — and that is why it is worth keeping.** Arvan's dedicated-IP page documents the panel workflow and no annotations at all. This is observed field knowledge. Phase 2 must keep it and must mark it as observed-not-documented with the date and cluster it was observed on, because an undocumented vendor annotation is exactly the kind of fact that changes without notice. |
| Domain flows depend on Arvan CDN-managed DNS and active CDN | `SKILL.md:381`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:57` | https://docs.arvancloud.ir/en/cloud-container/manage-app/domain (linked, not re-fetched) | **PLAUSIBLE, unverified in this pass.** |
| Node-affinity labels for CPU generation, `cloud-container-g2`/`g3` | `references/arvan-constraints.md:57` | — | **UNVERIFIED.** No label key given, hedged with "may require". |
| Panel accepts multi-document YAML separated by `---` | `SKILL.md:379`, `references/arvan-constraints.md:53` | manifest page | **PLAUSIBLE.** The manifest page shows a three-document example. |

**Two claims I could not check at all**, because `references/arvan-caas-openAPI-1.25.json` was
deliberately not staged: the 134/299 path and operation counts (`SKILL.md:116-117`,
`references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:12-13`) and the two zone server URLs
(`.../zones/ir-tbz-sh1`, `.../zones/ir-thr-ba1`, matrix `:15-16`). Nothing in the skill can check them
either — see §9.

### 8. Router audit

- **Convention:** five markdown references plus one JSON → 6 → 8 or fewer → list them in `SKILL.md`
  with a trigger condition each, and no `references/00-topic-map.md`. There is no topic map. Correct.
- **Trigger conditions:** absent. `SKILL.md:418-434` lists twelve paths under "Read only what is
  needed" with no condition attached to any of them. `SKILL.md:16-24` lists six with a precedence
  number, which is not a trigger. Only two files get a real condition anywhere:
  `references/arvan-rbac-namespace-facts.md` ("Load this reference when RBAC signals are
  inconsistent", `SKILL.md:146`) and `references/SOURCES.md` ("as the one-hop official-first source map
  when current behavior matters", `SKILL.md:48`). The convention is half-met.
- **Three routers.** `SKILL.md:16-24`, `SKILL.md:418-434` and `references/SOURCES.md:11-14` list
  overlapping sets in three orders for three purposes, and `references/arvan-links.md:9-11` makes a
  fourth partial list. Consolidate to one conditioned list in `SKILL.md` plus `SOURCES.md` as the
  provenance ledger.
- **Source map versus topic map.** `references/SOURCES.md` is a legitimate provenance ledger under the
  programme's rule and must not be deleted. `references/arvan-links.md` is a second ledger whose URL
  section (`:17-29`) duplicates `SOURCES.md:18-29`. Its non-duplicated value is the verification date
  (`:5`) and the three confirmed-from-docs facts (`:56-63`). Reduce it to that.
- **The 1.5 MB JSON is listed as a readable resource.** `SKILL.md:423` puts
  `references/arvan-caas-openAPI-1.25.json` in a list headed "Read only what is needed", and
  `SKILL.md:19` calls it source of truth number one. An agent that follows either instruction literally
  attempts to read 1.5 MB into context. It must be marked explicitly as machine-only. See §9.

### 9. Scripts and assets audit

Three scripts. **One gates. One reports and never fails. One summarises and compares against nothing.**

| Script | What it asserts | Windows | `--help` | Self-test | Exit codes distinguish "could not run" from "clean"? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `scripts/render-helm.sh` | That `helm` is on PATH, the chart dir exists with a `Chart.yaml`, and both the values file and the secret values file exist (`:44-52`); then that `helm dependency build`, `helm lint` and `helm template` all succeed (`:63-69`, under `set -euo pipefail`). It is a real gate for render and lint errors. It asserts **nothing Arvan-specific** — not `requests == limits`, not the absence of unsupported kinds, not secret leakage. | Bash-only; runs under Git Bash. Default `OUT=/tmp/rendered.yaml` (`:22`) resolves under the Git Bash virtual root, acceptable. `mkdir -p "$(dirname "${OUT}")"` (`:54`) is fine. | **Yes but wrong** — `:33-42` prints usage for `-h`/`--help` and then **`exit 1`**. A `--help` that exits non-zero breaks any wrapper that checks status, and it disagrees with `verify-cluster.sh`, which exits 0. | **No.** | **No.** Every `die` is exit 1, as is `--help`; `helm`'s own status propagates for lint and template. |
| `scripts/summarize-openapi.sh` | That the spec file exists (`:15-18`) and that a Python interpreter exists (`:20-28`). Then it *computes* `openapi`, title, version, path count, operation count, namespaced-path ratio, server URLs, and a per-resource collection/item/subresource table (`:82-108`). | Heredoc Python; runs under Git Bash. Correctly falls back from `python3` to `python` (`:21-27`), which is the right handling for native Windows where `python3` often does not exist. Best cross-platform handling of the three. | **No.** `--help` is treated as a spec path and dies with "spec file not found". | **No.** | Partially — exit 1 for missing spec and exit 1 for missing Python are both "could not run", and 0 is success. There is no findings state because it makes no findings. |
| `scripts/verify-cluster.sh` | Nothing. It *detects*: which OpenAPI-aligned API versions are served (`:87`), which of 17 required Arvan resources are missing (`:95-126`), which of 6 non-OpenAPI resources are unexpectedly present (`:129-149`), quota and LimitRange, five workload smoke reads, nine `can-i` checks for the caller and six impersonating the runner SA, and the RoleBinding subject table (`:190`). | Bash-only; runs under Git Bash. **CRLF-fragile in two places that would fail silently:** `:87` anchors a `grep -E "^(...)$"` against `kubectl api-versions` output, and `:117`/`:140` use `grep -qx` against `kubectl api-resources` output. If any layer introduces CR line endings, `$` and `-x` never match, every API version is reported absent and every required resource is reported missing — while the bytes look identical on screen. This is precisely the shipped defect class the brief names. Add `tr -d '\r'` to both pipelines. | **Yes, and correct** — `:33-47`, exits 0. The only script in this lane that gets `--help` right. | **No.** | **No, and this is the skill's central enforcement failure.** The script **always exits 0**. `:122-126` computes `MISSING_REQUIRED` and, when it is non-empty, prints `Required Arvan OpenAPI resources: missing -> …` as a note and continues. `:145-148` detects "unsupported" resources present and prints a note. `kubectl_safe` (`:28-31`) swallows every non-zero status. The final line is `echo "Done."`. A cluster that serves none of the required APIs and every forbidden one produces the same exit status as a perfect cluster. |

**Count: 3 scripts, 1 gate (render), 0 that enforce any Arvan constraint, 1 with a correct `--help`,
0 self-tests, 0 that distinguish "could not run" from "clean" on findings.**

**The 1.5 MB vendored OpenAPI document — is that the right shape, and can the matrix drift?**

The file is `references/arvan-caas-openAPI-1.25.json`, 1.5 MB, not staged for this analysis. Beside it
sit a 10,643-byte hand-written `references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md` and a
`scripts/summarize-openapi.sh` that reads the JSON.

**Vendoring the JSON is right.** It is the only machine-readable evidence for the platform's API
surface, Arvan publishes no equivalent, and without it the matrix's claims have no provenance at all.
Deleting it would make the skill's central factual claim unfalsifiable. Keep it.

**Everything about how it is wired up is wrong.**

1. **It is presented as human-readable.** `SKILL.md:19` ranks it source-of-truth number one and
   `SKILL.md:423` lists it under "Read only what is needed". An agent that obeys either literally pulls
   1.5 MB into context and has no budget left for the task. The file must be marked machine-only, with
   `scripts/summarize-openapi.sh` named as the only permitted reader.
2. **Two of the three paths pointing at it are wrong.** `references/arvan-constraints.md:39` and
   `references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:5` both spell it `arvann-caas-openAPI-1.25.json` —
   doubled *n*. The two files a reader would consult to learn about the spec both cite a filename that
   does not exist. `scripts/summarize-openapi.sh:9`'s documented example repeats the typo *and* points
   at the wrong directory (`../` rather than `../references/`), though the script's computed default at
   `:12` is correct.
3. **Yes, the matrix and the JSON can drift apart with nothing noticing — this is the sharpest
   structural defect in the skill.** `summarize-openapi.sh` prints its summary to stdout. It has no
   `--check` mode, no golden-file comparison, no diff, and no non-zero exit on mismatch. The matrix is
   hand-transcribed prose. `SKILL.md:113-119` is a *third* hand-transcription of the same numbers.
   Nothing in the skill, and nothing in any repository that consumes it, ever compares the three. If
   someone re-exports the spec from a newer cluster and drops it in, the matrix keeps asserting 134
   paths, 299 operations, `autoscaling/v2beta2` support and a fixed unsupported-API list, and the only
   symptom is wrong manifests in production. Conversely, the matrix could be edited by hand to say
   anything and the JSON would not object. **The skill currently has three copies of a machine-derivable
   fact and zero mechanisms to keep them equal.**

   The fix is small and mechanical. Give `summarize-openapi.sh` a `--check` mode that regenerates the
   machine-derivable sections and diffs them against the committed matrix, exiting 3 on drift. Delimit
   those sections in the matrix with generated-block markers so the hand-written parts —
   `references/openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md:97-107` ("Agent behavior derived from this matrix")
   and the absent-API list at `:82-95`, which is a judgment about what *absence* means, not a fact the
   script can compute — survive regeneration untouched. Delete the third copy at `SKILL.md:113-119`
   entirely; the body should carry the conclusion, not the transcription. Then wire `--check` into
   whatever CI touches the skills tree, so drift fails a pipeline rather than a production deploy.

4. **The version string should not be in the filename or the title.** `arvan-caas-openAPI-1.25.json`,
   `openapi-1.25-capability-matrix.md`, "OpenAPI-driven capability baseline (Arvan 1.25)" — the number
   is baked into three names, which means re-exporting from a current cluster requires renaming files
   and rewriting every reference. Name them `arvan-caas-openapi.json` and
   `arvan-capability-matrix.md`, and let the version live in a metadata line the script regenerates.

**Assets.**

- `assets/README.operator.md.template` — good structure, four defects: `--create-namespace` at `:42`
  contradicts `SKILL.md:90` and `:391`; `--rollback-on-failure` at `:46` is Helm-4-only while `:25`
  states Helm 3 as the prerequisite; `:33-34` hardcode `.codex/skills/...`; `:34-35` instruct the
  operator to write `rendered.yaml` — containing every decoded chart Secret — into the working
  directory, and no gitignore guidance anywhere covers that filename.
- `assets/RUNBOOK.operator.md.template` — the strongest asset in the lane. Day-0/1/2 split, five
  symptom-keyed incident playbooks (`:93`, `:107`, `:122`, `:139`, `:154`) each with checks and fixes.
  Same `--rollback-on-failure` defect at `:39`, same hardcoded `.codex/` paths at `:30` and `:78`, and
  `:8` cites the spec with a `../references/` prefix that is correct relative to the *asset's* location
  but wrong once the template is copied into a consumer repository, which is the whole point of a
  template.
- `assets/values.secret.yaml.example` — correct dual-mode shape for all three secret groups
  (`create` + `existingSecretName`), sensible `REPLACE_ME` placeholders, an explicit do-not-commit
  banner and gitignore guidance (`:1-9`). Two defects: `runnerRegistrationToken` at `:49` is the
  deprecated GitLab field (§7), and the gitignore guidance omits `rendered.yaml` / `*.rendered.yaml`,
  which the skill's own workflow creates and which contains the same secrets.
- **No model or effort key in any asset or template.** Verified across all three files.

### 10. Rewrite brief for Phase 2

**Target always-loaded body size: ≤ 6,000 bytes (from 15,797) — a 62% reduction.** No capability is
lost; everything moves to references, and the duplicate copies collapse. This skill is the batch's
clearest case for defect class 10.

**What the body keeps (and only this):**

1. Frontmatter, rewritten (below).
2. **Source precedence, inverted:** live discovery first, then the capability matrix, then the
   constraints file, then the vendored spec marked machine-only. Delete `SKILL.md:32-34` — once the
   list is correct, the contradicting paragraph is unnecessary.
3. **The eight Arvan non-negotiables** (`SKILL.md:68-108`), reduced to their Arvan-specific core and
   stated **once**, with the generic halves (secret handling, HPA-by-workload-type, namespace-scoped
   RBAC, config-mount safety) replaced by one line each delegating to `/alaa-k8s-helm`. Fix the memory
   formula (§6.1) and either justify or downgrade the decimal-cores rule (§6.2).
4. **The Arvan capability conclusion in three lines** — the API surface is namespace-scoped only;
   cluster-scoped kinds are unavailable; the current version-specific list lives in the matrix and must
   be confirmed by discovery on every run. Delete the transcribed metadata at `:113-119` and the
   supported-groups list at `:121-128`; both live in the matrix.
5. **The RBAC identity model in five lines** (`SKILL.md:150-156`) with the conclusive-check replacement
   from §6.6 added, pointing at `references/arvan-rbac-namespace-facts.md`.
6. **A companion-boundary block** with correct dual trigger syntax at every one of the ten call sites,
   plus this skill's D8 half.
7. **A conditioned reference list** — one line per file with the condition that opens it.
8. **When to use / when not to use**, with `SKILL.md:64` promoted into the description.

**What leaves the body:**

| `SKILL.md:377-392` (Arvan operational details, GitLab Runner on Arvan) | `references/arvan-constraints.md` (operational) and **`/alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`** (runner expression) | See the Shared closing, question 3, for the exact split. |
| `SKILL.md:394-404` (CI/CD rules) | gate predicates → `alaa-k8s-helm/references/validation-workflows.md`; gate placement → `/alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`; image tag and digest policy → `/alaa-docker-production` | D8. Only the Arvan-specific predicate — "no rendered container may omit resources, and `requests` must equal `limits`" — stays here as a contributed predicate. |

Preserve it completely; reconcile its "prefer Deployment, Service, Ingress, Route-like platform workflows, PVC, HPA" list against this skill's stricter capability matrix, because the two currently disagree about whether Route-like workflows are available. |
