`references/module-patterns.md:153`, `:178`, `:259`, `:275`, `:374`, `:387`, `:816`, `:949`, `:1096`.
Verified: ansible-lint 26.6.0 with the sibling's config reports `yaml[truthy]: Truthy value should be
one of [false, true]` on `become: yes`.

**Additional defect — `references/playbook.md:521-536` inverts the programme's own standing
preference.** It instructs an agent to prefer `ansible.builtin.command` with a raw `psql` CLI over
`community.postgresql.postgresql_db` "if collection isn't essential", and gives a worked example at
`:557-582`. That is reimplementing an official capability with a shell-out to dodge a dependency, and
it destroys idempotency, check-mode support and error semantics in the process. The stated
justification — "validation environments may not have collections installed"
(`references/playbook.md:523`) — is a tooling problem being solved by degrading the product.

### 4. Boundary map

**What it names today.** `ansible-validator` only, at `SKILL.md:3`, `SKILL.md:26`, `SKILL.md:37` and
twelve times in `references/playbook.md`. Nothing else.

**What it does not name.** Zero mentions of `alaa-docker-production`, `alaa-k8s-helm`,
`alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`, `alaa-haproxy`, `alaa-makefile`, `caas-arvan-kuber`, or any doctrine owner
(`alaa-security-review`, `alaa-reliability-sla`, `alaa-observability-soc`, `alaa-services-contract`,
`alaa-project-constitution`, `alaa-controlled-ops`, `alaa-testing-strategy`, `alaa-prompting-guide`).
The reciprocal is also true and is itself a finding: grepping the six other batch-7 skills plus
`alaa-frontend-devops` for the string "ansible" returns **nothing**. This seam has never been asserted
from either side.

**The stack-versus-platform gap.** Decision D8 covers CI, containers and the proxy. Ansible is a fourth
delivery mechanism the ruling does not mention, and the omission is load-bearing: an Ansible play can
write a Dockerfile, drive `docker compose`, template an HAProxy config, and be invoked from a GitLab
job. Without a stated seam the generator will happily author all four. It already reaches into
Kubernetes at `references/playbook.md:352-410`, where it generates a full `kubernetes.core.k8s`
Deployment manifest inline — content that unambiguously belongs to `alaa-k8s-helm`, whose description
claims "Kubernetes YAML" as its own.

**Seam sentences this skill should state, in its own words:**

- *CI:* "`alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` (`/alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`, `$alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`) owns the job that invokes
  `ansible-playbook` — the runner image, the `rules:`/`needs:` graph, artifact retention, and how the
  vault password reaches the job. This skill owns the play the job runs: hosts, tasks, handlers,
  idempotency and check-mode behaviour. The boundary is the `ansible-playbook` command line: the
  arguments are the CI skill's, everything the command reads is this skill's."
- *Containers:* "When a play configures a container host, this skill owns the tasks that install the
  engine, place the daemon configuration and manage the systemd unit. It does not author the Dockerfile
  or the Compose file — `alaa-docker-production` (`/alaa-docker-production`, `$alaa-docker-production`)
  owns both, including the fail-closed `${VAR:?}` interpolation invariant. A play that needs a Compose
  file templates one authored under that skill's rules; it does not invent one."
- *Kubernetes:* "Manifests and Helm charts are `alaa-k8s-helm`'s (`/alaa-k8s-helm`, `$alaa-k8s-helm`),
  even when `kubernetes.core.k8s` is the module applying them. This skill owns the play that applies a
  manifest; it does not author the manifest body. The `kubernetes.core` example at
  `references/playbook.md:352-410` is on the wrong side of that line and must move to a
  routing sentence."
- *Proxy:* "HAProxy directives are `alaa-haproxy`'s (`/alaa-haproxy`, `$alaa-haproxy`). This skill owns
  the `template:` task, the `validate:` argument and the reload handler; it does not choose the
  directive."
- *Secrets:* "Ansible Vault mechanics — `ansible-vault encrypt_string`, vault IDs, `--vault-id`,
  `no_log`, and the `vars/` versus `group_vars/` placement of a vaulted value — are this skill's
  ground. Whether a missing secret means stop or proceed is not: `alaa-security-review`
  (`/alaa-security-review`, `$alaa-security-review`) owns fail-closed for security decisions, and the
  discriminating question is whether proceeding without the secret lets something through that must not
  get through. A play that cannot decrypt a vault stops."

**Within the skill.**

| Overview / source freshness / when-not-to-use | `SKILL.md:8-28` | `references/playbook.md:6-23` |
| Mandatory presentation format | `references/playbook.md:437-459` | `references/playbook.md:764-791` |
| Module selection priority | `references/playbook.md:519-536` | `references/best-practices.md:890-895` |

A shingle comparison of `references/best-practices.md` against `references/playbook.md` finds 44
identical normalised content lines, 11.4% of the former.

**Against `ansible-validator`.** See the Shared closing for the full measurement. In summary:
`references/best-practices.md` (895 lines) and `ansible-validator/references/best_practices.md` (860
lines) cover fourteen of the same topics and disagree on one of them; `references/source-map.md` and
`ansible-validator/references/source-map.md` share seven of thirteen content lines.

### 6. Wording-test failures

1. `SKILL.md:37` — "Validate with the `ansible-validator` skill (ansible-lint, check mode, Molecule);
   fix and re-run." *Could a competent agent follow this exactly and still do the wrong thing?* Yes.
   There is no command, no trigger prefix, no exit-code contract, and no stopping condition on
   "re-run". An agent can loop indefinitely or declare victory after one pass.
   **Replacement:** name the script and the accept condition, e.g. "Run
   `bash <ansible-validator>/scripts/validate_playbook.sh <file>` and treat exit 0 with zero
   ansible-lint failures as the only pass. If two consecutive fix-and-re-run cycles do not reduce the
   failure count, stop and report the remaining findings rather than continuing."
2. `references/playbook.md:414` — "**CRITICAL:** Every generated Ansible resource MUST be validated
   before presenting to the user", immediately undercut by `references/playbook.md:467-473` "Only skip
   validation when … User explicitly requests to skip validation." A prohibition with a
   user-request escape hatch is a preference. **Replacement:** state which artefact classes are
   exempt (inline snippets under N lines, documentation fragments) and remove the user-consent clause.
3. `references/playbook.md:143` — "Replace all `[PLACEHOLDERS]` with actual values". Abstract noun
   standing in for an observable condition. There is no list of placeholders and no check.
   **Replacement:** "No generated file may contain the characters `[` or `]` outside a YAML flow
   sequence you intended; grep the output for `\[[A-Za-z_ ]` and fail if it matches."
4. `references/playbook.md:521` — "Always prefer builtin modules over collection modules when
   possible." "When possible" makes the rule unfalsifiable, and as written it produces the
   `command: psql` anti-pattern at `:557-582`. **Replacement:** "Use a collection module when one
   exists for the operation. Declare the collection in `requirements.yml` with a version floor. Do not
   substitute `ansible.builtin.command` for a collection module; a missing collection is an
   environment defect, not a design choice."
5. `references/playbook.md:498` — "Use `no_log: true` for sensitive operations." Abstract noun
   ("sensitive"). **Replacement:** "Set `no_log: true` on any task whose module arguments or registered
   result can contain a value sourced from a vault file, a `lookup('env', …)`, or a variable whose name
   matches `(pass|secret|token|key|credential)`."
6. `references/playbook.md:499` — "Set restrictive file permissions (600 for secrets, 644 for
   configs)". States the value but not the scope or the failure mode; no mode is given for
   directories, and nothing forbids `0777`. Verified: nothing in either skill's toolchain reports
   `mode: '0777'` on a directory. **Replacement:** "Every `file`, `copy` and `template` task states an
   explicit `mode`. No mode may grant write to `other`. Secrets are `'0600'`, their directories
   `'0700'`; configs `'0644'`, their directories `'0755'`."
7. `references/playbook.md:510` — "Disable fact gathering when not needed: `gather_facts: false`."
   "When not needed" is unobservable. **Replacement:** "Set `gather_facts: false` unless the play reads
   an `ansible_*` fact; if it reads fewer than three, use `gather_subset` naming exactly those."
8. `assets/templates/project/ansible.cfg:14` — "Host key checking (disable for dynamic environments,
   enable for production)" followed by `host_key_checking = False`. A comment that describes the
   correct value while the file ships the wrong one. **Replacement:** ship `host_key_checking = True`
   and put the override in an explicitly-named non-production include.

### 7. Stale or unverifiable claims

All checks run 2026-07-29. Tooling installed in-container for verification:
`ansible-core 2.19.11`, `ansible-lint 26.6.0`, `yamllint 1.38.0`, `molecule 26.6.0`, `checkov 3.3.8`.

| Claim | Location | Source checked | Holds? |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Ansible 2.10+" as the stated requirement | `references/playbook.md:61`, `:357`, `references/best-practices.md:841`, `assets/templates/role/meta/main.yml:11` (`min_ansible_version: "2.10"`), `assets/templates/role/README.md:7` | PyPI `ansible-core` metadata; [Releases and maintenance](https://docs.ansible.com/projects/ansible/latest/reference_appendices/release_and_maintenance.html) | **NO.** Current `ansible-core` is **2.21.2** (2026-07-13). Maintained: 2.21 (GA to May 2027), 2.20 (security to May 2027), 2.19 (security to Nov 2026). 2.10 has been out of support for years. |
| Implicit assumption that any Python 3 control node works | throughout; `assets/templates/inventory/group_vars/all.yml:6` pins `ansible_python_interpreter: /usr/bin/python3` | PyPI `ansible-core` `requires_python`; [ansible-core 2.20 porting guide](https://docs.ansible.com/projects/ansible-core/devel/porting_guides/porting_guide_core_2.20.html) | **NO.** `ansible-core` 2.21.2 declares `requires_python >=3.12`. The 2.20 porting guide states "Python 3.11 is no longer a supported control node version. Python 3.12+ is now required" and "Python 3.8 is no longer a supported remote version. Python 3.9+ is now required for target execution." Neither number appears anywhere in the skill. |
| Ansible community package version | never stated | PyPI `ansible` metadata | Current community package is **14.2.0** (2026-07-14), `requires_dist: ansible-core~=2.21.2`. The skill never distinguishes `ansible` from `ansible-core`, which is the single most common version confusion in this domain. |
| `ansible.builtin.yum` exists and is the module for RHEL 7 | `references/module-patterns.md:67-84`, `assets/templates/playbook/basic_playbook.yml:31` | `ansible-doc -t module ansible.builtin.yum` → `[WARNING]: ansible.builtin.yum was not found`; `yum.py` absent from the shipped modules directory; `ansible_builtin_runtime.yml` carries `yum: redirect: ansible.builtin.dnf` under `action:` only | **NO.** The module is gone. `ansible.builtin.yum:` still executes because of an action redirect, but it now runs **dnf** — so the template's "Only use for RHEL 7/CentOS 7 systems" (`:70`) is actively wrong: on RHEL 7 there is no dnf. |
| `ansible.builtin.archive` | `references/module-patterns.md:415-425` | routing table: `archive: {redirect: community.general.archive}` | **NO.** Not a builtin. Correct FQCN is `community.general.archive`, and the collection must be declared. |
| `ansible.builtin.authorized_key` | `references/module-patterns.md:301-318` | routing table: `authorized_key: {redirect: ansible.posix.authorized_key}` | **NO.** Not a builtin. Correct FQCN is `ansible.posix.authorized_key` — which the sibling's `references/module_alternatives.md:65` gets right, so the pair contradicts itself. |
| `ansible.builtin.systemd` | `references/module-patterns.md:242-260` | `ansible-doc -t module ansible.builtin.systemd` → exists, `deprecated: False`; `systemd_service.py` also present | **Holds**, though `ansible.builtin.systemd_service` is the canonical name and `systemd` the alias. Worth stating. |
| `local_action` as a recommended pattern | `references/module-patterns.md:982-1016` (four worked examples) | `ansible-lint -L`: rule `deprecated-local-action` exists, tagged `autofix,deprecations` | **NO.** ansible-lint reports every one of those four examples. The skill's own `assets/templates/project/.ansible-lint:74` places `deprecated-local-action` in `warn_list`, i.e. it downgrades the rule that catches the pattern it teaches. |
| `args: warn: false` (inherited from the sibling's checklist but reachable through this pair) | `ansible-validator/references/security_checklist.md:269-270` | executed: `ansible-playbook` returns `Unsupported parameters for (ansible.legacy.command) module: warn` | **NO.** Removed from `command`/`shell`. |
| `.ansible-lint` rule names in the shipped project template | `assets/templates/project/.ansible-lint:66-85, 151-263` | `ansible-lint -L` (52 rules) | **Mostly hold, three do not.** `unnamed-task` (`:70`) is not a rule — it is `name[missing]`. `deprecated-command-syntax` (`:228`) is not a rule. `meta-no-info` (`:232`) is not a rule. `schema[...]` (`:256-263`) has no `schema` base rule in 26.6.0. `experimental` (`:67`) is a *tag*, which `warn_list` accepts, so that one is fine. Unknown names in `warn_list`/`skip_list` are accepted silently — verified — so all four are dead entries that report nothing. |
| The whole `rules:` severity block | `assets/templates/project/.ansible-lint:151-263` | executed: `ansible-lint -c <that file> playbook.yml` → `Invalid configuration file … $.rules['meta-incorrect'] Additional properties are not allowed ('severity' was unexpected)`, **exit 3**; removing the block makes the same config valid (exit 0) | **NO.** Per-rule `severity:` is not a supported option. The shipped lint config for every generated project is unusable as written. This is the single highest-impact stale claim in the skill. |
| `callback_whitelist` in the project `ansible.cfg` | `assets/templates/project/ansible.cfg:34` | executed: `ansible-config validate -t all` → `[ERROR]: Found unknown key 'callback_whitelist' in section 'defaults'` | **NO.** Renamed `callbacks_enabled` in ansible-core 2.11; the old name is gone. Every other key in the file validates clean. |
| `collections_path`, `interpreter_python = auto_silent`, `[ssh_connection]` block, `[persistent_connection]`, `[inventory] enable_plugins` | `assets/templates/project/ansible.cfg:12,21,75-85,87-90,108-110` | `ansible-config validate -t all` | **Hold.** Only `callback_whitelist` fails. |
| Collection version floors in `requirements.yml` | `assets/templates/project/requirements.yml:10` (`ansible.posix >=1.5.0`), `:14` (`community.general >=8.0.0`) | Galaxy current majors | Floors are so low they assert nothing; `community.general` is many majors past 8. Not wrong, but not a constraint either. |
| Variable precedence list | `references/best-practices.md:138-148` | [Ansible variable precedence](https://docs.ansible.com/projects/ansible/latest/playbook_guide/playbooks_variables.html) and the sibling's own correct 21-item list at `ansible-validator/references/best_practices.md:152-176` | **NO.** The nine-item list is not merely abbreviated, it is mis-ordered: it places "Set_facts / registered vars" (position 5) *below* "Task vars" (2), "Block vars" (3) and "Role and include vars" (4). In real precedence `set_fact`/registered vars outrank block, task and role vars, and role params outrank `set_fact`. It also omits `vars_prompt`, host facts and include params entirely. The two skills in this pair give contradictory precedence tables. |
| Molecule guidance | `SKILL.md:20`, `references/playbook.md:16` mention Molecule as a freshness trigger only | see Appendix H §7 | The generator delegates all Molecule detail, which is correct. |
| Source-map URLs | `references/source-map.md:15-16` | fetched | `https://ansible.readthedocs.io/projects/lint/rules/` and `.../molecule/` both return **302 → docs.ansible.com**. Not broken, but the canonical host has moved and the map should follow. |

**Module sampling — method and defect rate.** The population is every fully-qualified module name in
`references/module-patterns.md`, extracted mechanically with
`grep -ohE '\b(ansible\.builtin|ansible\.posix|community\.[a-z_]+|amazon\.aws|azure\.azcollection|google\.cloud|kubernetes\.core)\.[a-z0-9_]+'`
and de-duplicated: **51 distinct FQCNs** across nine collections (34 `ansible.builtin`, 5
`azure.azcollection`, 4 `amazon.aws`, 2 `ansible.posix`, 2 `community.general`, and one each from
`community.aws`, `community.docker`, `community.hashi_vault`, `community.postgresql`).

I sampled **exhaustively within the `ansible.builtin` stratum** (34 of 34, 100%) because that stratum
is (a) two-thirds of the population, (b) the one the skill tells agents to prefer above all others
(`references/playbook.md:529`), and (c) verifiable offline and deterministically with
`ansible-doc -t module -j <fqcn>` plus a direct read of ansible-core's
`config/ansible_builtin_runtime.yml` routing table. Collection strata were sampled by routing-table
lookup for the redirect question and by name plausibility only; I did not install eight collections to
verify parameter-level currency, and I flag that as the residual unknown.

**Defect rate in the sampled stratum: 3 of 34 = 8.8%.** `ansible.builtin.yum` (removed, silently
becomes dnf), `ansible.builtin.archive` (redirect to `community.general.archive`),
`ansible.builtin.authorized_key` (redirect to `ansible.posix.authorized_key`). All three are the same
failure mode — a module that left core and now works only through a compatibility redirect, so the
example *runs* on a machine with the collection installed and fails on a machine without it, which is
exactly the invisible staleness a 32 KB module reference produces. A fourth, `ansible.builtin.systemd`,
is an alias rather than the canonical name and is a soft finding. No parameter-level defect was found
in the 31 clean builtins.

### 8. Router audit

`SKILL.md:43-48` lists four references, each with a one-line charter. Four is under the nine-reference
threshold, so the inline list is the correct form and there is correctly no `references/00-topic-map.md`.
`references/source-map.md` is a provenance ledger and must be kept.

Defects in the router:

1. **No trigger condition on three of four entries.** `SKILL.md:45-48` gives each reference a
   *description* but only `references/source-map.md` has a stated condition (via `SKILL.md:19-20`). The
   convention requires "a trigger condition each". `references/playbook.md:586-603` does carry
   when-to-read conditions for two of them — which means the trigger conditions exist but live one hop
   away, inside the file they are supposed to gate.
2. **The router does not route to `assets/`.** The scaffold is the skill's largest surface and its most
   defective; `SKILL.md` never mentions `assets/templates/` at all. An agent that reads only the body
   will not know the templates exist.
3. **`references/playbook.md` is a second body, not a reference.** Lines 6-23 restate the body; lines
   584-633 are a second reference map. The file needs splitting (§10).
4. **No `/name` form.** Covered in §3.

**Scripts: none.** This is itself the finding. The generator asserts eight mandatory rules
(`references/playbook.md:751-762`) and ships nothing that can report a violation of any of them.

Contains `# profile: production` commented out at `:92` and again at `:139` — the file recommends the production profile twice and enables it zero times. |

**Verification run — does the sibling pass this scaffold?** Copied to `/tmp` and run:
`bash ansible-validator/scripts/validate_role.sh roles/scaffold_role` → exit 1. Stage 3 (Ansible
syntax) correctly fails. Stage 2 (YAML syntax) prints **"✓ YAML syntax check passed"** despite yamllint
having just printed five `syntax error` lines on screen — see Appendix H §9 for the cause. Stage 4
(ansible-lint) reports one warning (`role-name`) and prints "✓ Ansible lint check passed". So the pair's
verdict on the generator's own scaffold is: one true positive, one false negative, one near-miss.

`bash ansible-validator/scripts/extract_ansible_info_wrapper.sh roles/scaffold_role` → exit 1, with all
five parse errors listed correctly. The extractor is the only tool in the pair that gets this right.

### 10. Rewrite brief for Phase 2

**Target body size:** 2,300–2,600 bytes (currently 2,096). It grows because it must gain the five
boundary sentences from §4 and both trigger forms; it must not gain anything else.

**Body structure (`SKILL.md`):**

```
frontmatter          keep name; keep description, add no clause (426 chars, already compliant)
# Ansible Generator
## Overview          4 lines — unchanged
## Source freshness  2 lines — unchanged
## When NOT to use   5 lines — add the four platform seams as one-liners with /name and $name
## Workflow          6 numbered steps — step 5 gains the concrete command and stop condition
## Boundaries        NEW, 6 lines — the seam sentences from §4, one line each
## Reference map     5 entries with a trigger condition each (gains 01-scaffold.md)
```

**Reference file list after the rewrite:**

| File | Charter | Trigger condition |
|---|---|---|
| `references/playbook.md` | The generation procedure end to end: capability by artefact type, the validation handoff, the delivery format. | Read when actually generating anything. |
| `references/best-practices.md` | The enforceable ruleset: structure, naming, idempotency, error handling, security, tags, performance. Rules only, one statement each. | Read before writing the first task. |
| `references/module-patterns.md` | Copy-ready module usage by operation, FQCN-correct against the current core and collection set. | Read when choosing a module. |
| `references/jinja-and-lookups.md` | **NEW.** The 377-line filter/lookup/template-control body currently buried at `references/best-practices.md:265-641`, moved intact. | Read only when writing a Jinja template or a lookup. |
| `references/scaffold.md` | **NEW.** How to use `assets/templates/`: what each file is, the placeholder convention, and the post-substitution check. | Read when creating a new role or project. |
| `references/source-map.md` | Provenance ledger. Keep. Update the two readthedocs URLs to their `docs.ansible.com` targets. | Read before any version-sensitive claim. |

**Content moves:**

1. `references/playbook.md:6-23` → delete. It duplicates `SKILL.md:8-28`.
2. `references/playbook.md:764-791` → delete. Verbatim duplicate of `:437-459`.
3. `references/best-practices.md:265-641` → move whole to `references/jinja-and-lookups.md`. This alone
   removes 377 lines from the file an agent reads before every generation.

8. `references/best-practices.md:890-895` → delete; `references/playbook.md:519-536` is the single
   statement of module priority.

**Files nominated for retirement** (move to `_to_delete/2026-07-29-batch7/`, nothing is deleted):

- `assets/templates/project/.ansible-lint` — replace with a ~25-line file: `profile: production`,
  `exclude_paths`, `warn_list` with real rule names, `skip_list` empty. The 267-line commented catalogue
  and the invalid `rules:` block both go.
- Nothing else. Every other template is repairable in place and its content must be preserved.

**Repairs that are not moves:**

- Rewrite all six non-parsing templates. Adopt a placeholder convention that survives YAML:
  `ROLE_NAME_package_name: "CHANGE_ME_package"` rather than `[ role_name ]_package_name: [ package_name ]`.
  De-indent every top-level key to column 0. Verify with `python3 -c "import yaml,sys;yaml.safe_load(open(f))"`
  on all 22 template files as an acceptance gate.

  `delegate_to: localhost`.
- Every cross-skill call site gains both trigger forms: `/ansible-validator` (`$ansible-validator`).

**Executable checks the skill should ship and does not:**

1. `scripts/check_templates.py` — parse every file under `assets/templates/` with `yaml.safe_load`;
   exit 1 listing each failure. This would have caught six shipped defects. Must run on Windows: pure
   Python, `pathlib`, no shell, no `Path(__file__).parents[N]` (resolve from `argv` or `Path(__file__).parent`).
2. `scripts/check_config_assets.sh` (and a `.ps1` twin, or a Python driver) — run
   `ansible-lint -c assets/templates/project/.ansible-lint --version` and `ansible-config validate -t all`
   against the shipped `ansible.cfg`; exit **2** for "tool missing", **1** for "config invalid", **0** for
   clean. This would have caught the exit-3 lint config and `callback_whitelist`.
3. `scripts/check_booleans.py` — fail on `: *(yes|no) *$` in any shipped YAML. This is the skill's own
   checklist item 3, currently a preference.
4. `scripts/check_fqcn_currency.py` — for every FQCN appearing in `references/module-patterns.md`, call
   `ansible-doc -t module -j <fqcn>` and fail on "not found" or on a routing redirect out of the stated
   collection. Run it in the repository's own CI on a schedule; this is the only defence against the
   8.8% stratum defect rate recurring.
5. A `tests/` fixture pair mirroring the sibling's: one scaffolded role that must pass the sibling's
   `validate_role.sh` cleanly, one deliberately broken one that must fail on named findings. Today the
   generator has no fixture at all.

---

## Appendix H — `ansible-validator`

### 1. What this skill is today

`ansible-validator` is the largest executable surface in batch 7: nine bash scripts and one Python
extractor totalling 2,153 lines, three configuration assets, six references totalling 3,464 lines, and a
44-line, 2,106-byte body. It is the pair's checking half and the only skill in the batch with a `test/`
directory.

The body (`SKILL.md:6-48`) is structurally identical to its sibling's: Overview, Source freshness, When
NOT to use, a six-step Workflow, and a Reference map listing six references inline — correct form for a
skill under the nine-reference threshold, no `00-topic-map.md`, no two-router defect. The description
(484 characters, no angle brackets) carries a working negative clause: "Do not use to author new Ansible
from scratch (use `ansible-generator`)" (`SKILL.md:3`).

**The body never mentions `scripts/`.** Grepping `SKILL.md` for the string "scripts" returns zero hits.
Ten executables, the skill's entire enforcement capability, are reachable only from
`references/playbook.md:871-993`. An agent that loads the router and follows its six-step workflow
(`SKILL.md:31-36`) will run `ansible-lint` and `ansible-playbook --check` by hand and will never
discover `scan_secrets.sh`, `check_fqcn.sh` or `test_role.sh`.

`references/playbook.md` is 1,125 lines and contains, among other things, a decision tree
(`:45-115`), a doctrine that molecule tests run automatically without asking the user (`:14-17`,
`:466-467`, `:1049-1054`, `:1116-1117` — stated four times), a Checkov policy catalogue (`:251-282`),
and a script catalogue (`:871-993`).

`test/` contains only `README.md` (384 lines). **The fixtures it documents were not staged**: neither
`test/playbooks/good-playbook.yml` nor `test/playbooks/bad-playbook.yml` nor `test/roles/` exists in the
staged tree. I reconstructed both playbooks from `test/README.md:19-49` to run the scripts; every
finding below that references a fixture uses my reconstruction, and I say so at each point.

### 2. Ten-criteria verdict

| # | Criterion | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Correctness and testability | **FAIL** | `scripts/validate_role.sh:208` inverts its own YAML result (proof in §9): a broken file that starts with `---` is counted as passing, a clean file without `---` is counted as failing. `scripts/check_fqcn.sh:168` reports false positives on a fully FQCN-correct playbook. `scripts/extract_ansible_info.py:248-249` classifies every `ansible.builtin.*` module as "custom". `test/` has no runner, only prose. |
| 2 | Failure behaviour | **FAIL** | Every script returns **1** for "no argument", "target not found", "tool unavailable" and "findings present" alike (matrix in §9). `scripts/validate_playbook_security.sh:190` prints `Security validation failed with  security issue(s)` — `$FAILED` is unset in that branch — when Checkov could not produce a report at all. `scripts/test_role.sh:148` under `set -e` aborts before the summary, before `verify`, and before `molecule destroy`, leaking containers. |
| 3 | Security | **FAIL** | `scripts/scan_secrets.sh:129` uses ERE syntax (`{16}`) under BRE `grep`, so `AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE` is not detected — verified. `scripts/scan_secrets.sh:116-124` has no `{{`/`vault_` filter, so a correctly vaulted playbook is reported as two hardcoded secrets — verified. Nothing in the toolchain reports `mode: '0777'` on a directory or `{{ user_input }}` interpolated into `shell:` — both verified passing under `profile: production`. `references/common_errors.md:299` teaches a plaintext `ansible_ssh_pass`; `:322` teaches blanket `NOPASSWD: ALL`. |
| 4 | Observability | **FAIL** | No script emits machine-readable output. ansible-lint's `--format sarif` / `--sarif-file` and `-f json` exist and are used nowhere; `assets/.ansible-lint:95` has `sarif_file` commented out. There is no exit-code contract, no findings count on stdout in a parseable shape, and nothing an aggregator could consume. |
| 5 | Concurrency and load | **FAIL** | `references/playbook.md:1057-1070` sends an agent to run `--check --diff` against production inventory with no word on `--forks`, `--limit` batching, or the load a full check run puts on a live fleet. `scripts/validate_role.sh:207` lints files strictly serially. No other owner is named. |
| 6 | Clean code, SOLID, design patterns | **FAIL** | `scripts/validate_playbook.sh:39-123` and `scripts/validate_role.sh:39-123` are byte-identical (85 lines). `scripts/validate_playbook_security.sh:45-101` and `scripts/validate_role_security.sh:39-95` are byte-identical (57 lines). The summary blocks at `validate_playbook.sh:210-240` and `validate_role.sh:327-357` are identical. Roughly 300 duplicated lines across six scripts, no shared library. |
| 7 | Algorithm and data-structure choice | **FAIL** | `scripts/check_fqcn.sh:52-154` defines 94 module names and `:197-213` runs one `grep -r` pass **per name** — 94 full traversals of the target tree — where one YAML parse would do, and would also eliminate the false positives. No complexity statement anywhere; no other owner named. |
| 8 | Configurability | **FAIL** | `scripts/validate_playbook.sh:142-146` and `:185-189` hardcode `$SKILL_DIR/assets/.yamllint` and `$SKILL_DIR/assets/.ansible-lint`. There is no flag to use the project's own config, no `--profile` selection, no severity threshold, no way to make the security scan advisory. A project with its own `.ansible-lint` is silently overridden by the skill's. |
| 9 | Speed of development and debuggability | **FAIL** | `scripts/validate_playbook.sh:94-118` builds a fresh venv and pip-installs `ansible-core ansible-lint yamllint` on every invocation, and `:98-105` deletes it on exit. Reviewing five files pays that cost five times. `scripts/test_role.sh:106` additionally installs `molecule molecule-docker` per run. No cache, no reuse, no opt-out. |
| 10 | Documentation | SATISFIED | `references/playbook.md:871-993` documents every script with usage and detection list; `references/playbook.md:1015-1100` gives five worked scenarios; `test/README.md` documents the fixture corpus and its expected results. |

Tally: **1 SATISFIED, 9 FAIL, 0 NOT-OWNED.** As with the generator, no `NOT-OWNED` is legitimate: the
only cross-skill call sites in the entire skill are `references/playbook.md:1106-1108`, and one of the
three names a skill that does not exist.

### 3. Defect classes actually found

**Class 2 — wrong trigger syntax. Confirmed.** `agents/openai.yaml:4` uses `$ansible-validator`
(correct, Codex-only). `SKILL.md:3` and `:25` write bare `` `ansible-generator` `` with no prefix.
There is no `/ansible-generator` anywhere in the skill.

**Class 3 — duplication between body and references. Confirmed.** `references/playbook.md:6-23`
restates `SKILL.md:6-21`; `references/playbook.md:39-43` restates `SKILL.md:23-27`;
`references/playbook.md:19-22` is a verbatim copy of `SKILL.md:18-21`. The file carries its own H1 at
`:6`. Internally: the automatic-molecule doctrine is stated at `:14-17`, `:82-87`, `:466-467`,
`:1049-1054` and `:1116-1117` — five times; the "run both Checkov and scan_secrets" doctrine at
`:95-102`, `:309-315` and `:974-975` — three times.

**Class 5 — long numbered procedures nobody reads in order. Confirmed, and this is the worst instance
in the pair.** `references/playbook.md:45-115` is a 70-line ASCII decision tree with eight numbered
stages and nested `├─>` branches. It is the skill's operating procedure and it is unreadable as a
lookup. `references/playbook.md:814-869` ("Error Troubleshooting") is six error headings with
three-line fenced "Solution:" blocks that contain prose, not commands — symptom→advice with no
diagnosis step, no smallest retry, and no escalation.

**Class 6 — description that only says when to use. NOT found.** `SKILL.md:3` is well-formed.

**Class 7 — fragile tooling. Confirmed in a Windows-specific form.** Every script that falls back to a
temporary venv addresses it as `"$TEMP_VENV/bin/<tool>"` — `validate_playbook.sh:48,57`,
`validate_role.sh:48,57`, `validate_playbook_security.sh:53`, `validate_role_security.sh:47`,
`test_role.sh:64`, and `source "$TEMP_VENV/bin/activate"` at `validate_playbook.sh:112`,
`validate_role.sh:112`, `validate_playbook_security.sh:90`, `validate_role_security.sh:84`,
`test_role.sh:102`, `extract_ansible_info_wrapper.sh:33`. On Windows `python -m venv` creates
`Scripts/`, not `bin/`. **Every auto-install fallback path in this skill is broken on Windows.**
Additionally `scripts/validate_role.sh:248` uses `ln -s`, which needs Developer Mode or elevation on
Windows, and `scripts/validate_role.sh:207` iterates an unquoted `$YAML_FILES`, which splits on the
spaces that are normal in Windows paths.

**Class 8 — shipped `__pycache__`. NOT found.** No `__pycache__`, no `*.pyc`. `.gitignore:2-3` covers
them. `.gitignore` is byte-identical to the generator's and ends without a newline.

**Class 11 — companion boundary. Confirmed defective.** `references/playbook.md:1102-1108` is the only
"Integration with Other Skills" list. It names `alaa-k8s-helm` twice (`:1106` and `:1108`) and
`terraform-validator` once (`:1107`) — **`terraform-validator` does not exist** anywhere in the staged
skills tree. A dangling cross-skill pointer in the one place the skill declares its boundaries.

**Class 12 — two routers. NOT found.** Six references, listed inline at `SKILL.md:43-48`. Correct.

**Class 13 — a rule with no tool that reports a violation.** This skill ships ten executables, so the
count is worth stating precisely. What each actually asserts:

| Script | Asserts | Gates? |
|---|---|---|
| `validate_playbook.sh` | yamllint clean (under the skill's config), `ansible-playbook --syntax-check` clean, ansible-lint clean | Yes, exit 1 on error |
| `validate_role.sh` | role has `tasks/`; recommended dirs present; `main.yml` present; YAML clean; role syntax-checks; ansible-lint clean; molecule dir present | Yes on 3 of 7; **the YAML assertion is inverted and asserts nothing** |
| `validate_playbook_security.sh` | Checkov `--framework ansible` reports 0 failed checks | Yes, but "could not run" also exits 1 |
| `validate_role_security.sh` | same, on a role directory | Same |
| `scan_secrets.sh` | 30 grep patterns for credential shapes | Yes, exit 1 — but with both false positives and false negatives |
| `check_fqcn.sh` | 94 module short-name greps | **No — always exits 0** (`:224` and `:252`). It is advisory by construction; `:250` says so. |
| `test_role.sh` | molecule syntax/create/converge/verify, plus a home-grown idempotence check | Yes, but aborts before the summary on the first failure |
| `setup_tools.sh` | seven tools are on `PATH` | Yes for presence; **it prints versions and never compares them to the minimums at `references/playbook.md:801-806`** |
| `extract_ansible_info_wrapper.sh` | PyYAML available | Pass-through |
| `extract_ansible_info.py` | every YAML file in scope parses | Yes, exit 1 on parse error — the most reliable assertion in the skill |

So: ten executables, of which one never fails, one fails backwards, one enforces presence but not the
version floor it documents, and one is a wrapper. Four assert something a checker could test and get it
right.

**Additional defect — the `.ansible-lint` asset silently disables the rule it explicitly enables.**
`assets/.ansible-lint:53` sets `task_name_prefix: "{path}:"`. `{path}` is not a valid substitution key;
the valid one is `{stem}`. ansible-lint 26.6.0 catches the resulting `KeyError` and prints
`WARNING Ignored exception from NameRule.matchtasks … 'path'`, then continues with `NameRule` dead.
Proof, run against my reconstructed `bad-playbook.yml`:

- with the shipped config: **19 failures, zero `name[missing]`**;
- with only `task_name_prefix` removed: **21 failures and 2 warnings, including two `name[missing]`
  and two `name[casing]`**.

`assets/.ansible-lint:38-39` explicitly asks for `name[casing]` and `name[missing]` in `enable_list`.
One malformed line four lines below turns both off. `test/README.md:37` lists "Tasks without names" as
a defect the corpus is supposed to expose; the shipped config guarantees it will not be.

**Additional defect — the two shipped lint configs contradict each other.** `assets/.yamllint:56` sets
`truthy: allowed-values: ['true','false','yes','no','on','off']`, so yamllint **passes** `become: yes`;
ansible-lint with `assets/.ansible-lint` **fails** the same line with `yaml[truthy]: Truthy value should
be one of [false, true]`. Both verified. Conversely `assets/.yamllint:60-61` sets
`document-start: present: no`, so yamllint flags the leading `---` on every conventional Ansible file
as forbidden — and `scripts/validate_role.sh:208` then greps that specific warning back out, which is
the skill papering over a contradiction it created.

**Additional defect — `assets/.ansible-lint:38-43` and `:46-50` both list `name[casing]`.** It is in
`enable_list` and in `warn_list` simultaneously.

**Additional defect — `assets/.ansible-lint:22` excludes `test/`.** On a directory scan, the skill's own
fixture corpus is excluded from linting. (Explicit file targets bypass `exclude_paths`, so the documented
invocations at `test/README.md:78-90` still work; the exclusion bites when an agent lints a project tree.)

### 4. Boundary map

**What it names today.** `ansible-generator` at `SKILL.md:3` and `:25`; `alaa-k8s-helm` twice and
`terraform-validator` once at `references/playbook.md:1106-1108`; `mcp__context7__*` at
`references/playbook.md:610-611` and `:1078`.

**What it does not name.** `alaa-docker-production`, `alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`, `alaa-haproxy`,
`alaa-makefile`, `caas-arvan-kuber`, and every doctrine owner. `alaa-security-review` is the sharpest
omission: this skill *is* the security-audit surface for Ansible (`SKILL.md:35`,
`references/security_checklist.md` in full, three security scripts) and it never once routes a
fail-closed decision to the owner of fail-closed doctrine.

**Seam sentences this skill should state:**

- *Secrets, and the split with `alaa-security-review`:* "Ansible Vault mechanics are this skill's:
  detecting an unvaulted secret, checking `no_log`, verifying that a vaulted file is actually
  encrypted, and reporting where a credential is in cleartext. Whether a finding blocks the run is not
  this skill's: `alaa-security-review` (`/alaa-security-review`, `$alaa-security-review`) owns
  fail-closed, and a secret-scan finding is fail-closed by default because proceeding with a leaked
  credential lets something through that must not get through. A scanner that cannot run is a blocked
  run, not a clean one."
- *CI:* "`alaa-gitlab-ci-cd` (`/alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`, `$alaa-gitlab-ci-cd`) owns the job that runs these
  scripts — the image, the caching of the tool venv, `rules:`, and artifact retention for the reports.
  This skill owns what each script asserts and what its exit codes mean. This skill decides no gate
  placement; it publishes the predicate and the command."
- *Containers:* "When a molecule scenario needs a container image, `alaa-docker-production`
  (`/alaa-docker-production`, `$alaa-docker-production`) owns how that image is expressed. This skill
  owns the scenario, the verifier and the idempotence assertion."
- *Kubernetes:* "Rendered-manifest validation, `helm lint` and chart schema checks are
  `alaa-k8s-helm`'s (`/alaa-k8s-helm`, `$alaa-k8s-helm`). This skill validates the play that applies a
  manifest, not the manifest."
- *Reliability:* "Whether a retry count, a timeout or a `serial` batch size is correct for an SLA is
  `alaa-reliability-sla`'s (`/alaa-reliability-sla`, `$alaa-reliability-sla`). This skill reports that
  a `command` task has no `changed_when`, that a `uri` health check has no `until`, and that a play has
  no `any_errors_fatal`; it does not choose the numbers."
- *Observability:* "The requirement level for a validation report — what must be emitted, at what gate,
  with what reason — is `alaa-observability-soc`'s (`/alaa-observability-soc`,
  `$alaa-observability-soc`). This skill owns the SARIF and JSON emission that satisfies it."

**On the CI-versus-play question specifically.** The clean cut is the process boundary: everything
outside the `ansible-playbook` process is the CI skill's (runner, image, secrets injection, artifact
upload, job dependencies, retry of the *job*); everything the process reads or writes is this pair's
(inventory, play, roles, vault files, check-mode semantics, retry of a *task*). The one genuinely
shared object is the vault password: CI owns how it reaches the runner as a masked variable, this pair
owns `--vault-id` and the requirement that a decrypt failure is fatal.

**On the container-host question.** The cut is the artefact type. A play may install Docker, write
`/etc/docker/daemon.json`, manage the `docker.service` unit and template a Compose file into place —
those are configuration-management tasks and are this pair's. The *content* of the Dockerfile and of
the Compose file, including the `${VAR:?}` fail-closed interpolation invariant, is
`alaa-docker-production`'s. A play that generates Compose YAML inline has crossed the line.

**Against `ansible-generator`.** See the Shared closing.

### 6. Wording-test failures

1. `references/playbook.md:14-17` — "this skill AUTOMATICALLY runs molecule tests if a `molecule/`
   directory is detected … This is non-negotiable and happens without asking for user permission."
   *Could a competent agent follow this exactly and do the wrong thing?* Yes, badly. Molecule
   `converge` runs the role for real inside a container it creates; the rule as written authorises
   spawning privileged containers with `/sys/fs/cgroup` mounted (`assets/molecule.yml.template:22-26`)
   on whatever machine the agent happens to be on, without asking. Under the programme's own
   change-control doctrine this is exactly the class of action that requires authorisation.
   **Replacement:** "Run molecule only when the user asked to test, or when the working tree is a role
   whose `molecule/` scenario the user named. Never run `molecule create` on a machine you did not
   confirm is a disposable test host. If molecule is configured and you are not running it, say so and
   say why."
2. `references/playbook.md:117-119` — "**CRITICAL: Reference files are NOT optional.** … Simply
   mentioning the reference file path is insufficient - the content must be consulted and relevant
   guidance extracted." No observable condition; nothing can check that a file was "consulted".
   **Replacement:** tie it to output: "Every reported finding cites the reference line that supplies
   its remediation, in the form `references/<file>:<line>`. A finding with no citation is not reported."
3. `SKILL.md:35` — "Security-audit against `references/security_checklist.md` (and Checkov where
   applicable)". "Where applicable" is an unstated scope, and the checklist is 22 unactionable
   checkboxes (`references/security_checklist.md:416-436`). **Replacement:** name the three scripts and
   state that all three run on every audit, and that a script that could not run is a blocked audit.
4. `references/playbook.md:1118-1119` — "If molecule tests fail due to environmental issues (Docker not
   running, version incompatibility), document the blocker but don't fail the overall validation."
   This is the fail-open rule stated explicitly, for a test surface, with no owner named and no
   discrimination against the security case. **Replacement:** "A test that could not run is not a
   passing test. Report `BLOCKED` with the reason, and treat a blocked security scan as a failure per
   `alaa-security-review` (`/alaa-security-review`, `$alaa-security-review`); a blocked idempotence
   test is a warning."
5. `references/playbook.md:208-210` — "ansible-lint supports `--fix` for auto-fixable issues. Always
   review changes before applying." `--fix` writes the files first; "review before applying" describes
   an order the tool does not offer. **Replacement:** "Run `ansible-lint --fix` only on a clean working
   tree, then review the resulting diff before committing."
6. `references/security_checklist.md:352` — "Validate SSL certificates - only disable for testing." A
   prohibition with a self-certified exemption. **Replacement:** "`validate_certs` must be absent or
   `true` in every task. A task that sets it `false` must carry a comment naming the internal CA it is
   working around and a linked issue for installing that CA."
7. `references/best_practices.md:802` — "See the molecule configuration and testing section in the main
   skill.md for comprehensive testing guidance." Dangling pointer: the file is `SKILL.md` (different
   case) and it contains no such section; the content is in `references/playbook.md:462-571`.
8. `scripts/check_fqcn.sh:250-251` — "Note: This is a recommendation, not an error. Short names still
   work but may cause issues in future Ansible versions." Preference verb where a constraint was meant:
   the skill's own `assets/.ansible-lint:40` enables `fqcn[action-core]` as an *error*. The script and
   the config disagree about whether FQCN is mandatory.
9. `references/playbook.md:801-806` ("Minimum Versions: Ansible >= 2.9 … molecule >= 3.4.0"). A stated
   floor with no tool that reports a violation — `setup_tools.sh` prints versions and never compares.
   And the floors are wrong; see §7.

### 7. Stale or unverifiable claims

All checks run 2026-07-29.

| Claim | Location | Source checked | Holds? |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Ansible: >= 2.9 (recommend >= 2.12)" | `references/playbook.md:803` | PyPI `ansible-core`; [Releases and maintenance](https://docs.ansible.com/projects/ansible/latest/reference_appendices/release_and_maintenance.html) | **NO.** Current `ansible-core` **2.21.2** (2026-07-13). Supported: 2.21, 2.20, 2.19 (2.19 EOL Nov 2026). 2.9 and 2.12 are years out of support. |
| Control-node Python is unstated | `references/playbook.md:776-799` lists tool checks but no Python floor | PyPI `requires_python` for `ansible-core` 2.21.2 = `>=3.12`; [2.20 porting guide](https://docs.ansible.com/projects/ansible-core/devel/porting_guides/porting_guide_core_2.20.html) | **Missing, and consequential.** "Python 3.11 is no longer a supported control node version. Python 3.12+ is now required." Targets need Python 3.9+. This container runs Python 3.11, so `pip install ansible-core` here silently resolves to **2.19.11** — which is exactly what the skill's unpinned auto-venv (`scripts/validate_playbook.sh:116`) will do on any 3.11 host, producing a validation run against a soon-to-be-EOL core with no warning. |
| Ansible community package version | never stated | PyPI `ansible` | Current **14.2.0** (2026-07-14), `ansible-core~=2.21.2`. |
| "ansible-lint: >= 6.0.0" | `references/playbook.md:804` | PyPI | Current **26.6.0** (2026-06-30), `requires_python >=3.10`. The floor is four calendar-year majors stale. |
| "yamllint: >= 1.26.0" | `references/playbook.md:805` | PyPI | Current **1.38.0** (2026-01-13). Floor stale but harmless. |
| "molecule: >= 3.4.0" | `references/playbook.md:806` | PyPI | Current **26.6.0** (2026-06-30). Molecule 3.x predates the driver rework entirely. |

| `sysvinit` → `ansible.builtin.service` "service module handles sysvinit" | `references/module_alternatives.md:57` | `community.general.sysvinit` is the module | Misleading. `service` is a wrapper, not a replacement for the `sysvinit` module. |
| The "Deprecated Module" column applied to short names | `references/module_alternatives.md:29-84` | — | Category error. `apt`, `copy`, `file` etc. are not deprecated modules; they are unqualified references to live modules. The table conflates two different problems and the skill's own scripts inherit the confusion. |
